## Priority Guide

| Symbol | Level | Meaning |
|--------|-------|---------|
| 🔴 | HIGH | Fix before financial/lease data is added |
| 🟡 | MEDIUM | Fix before Layer 3 build or before scaling to multiple users |
| 🟢 | LOW | Fix when time allows — cosmetic, minor, or future-state only |
| 📌 | DECISION | Not debt — documented so engineers don't accidentally "fix" it |

## 0. Critical Infrastructure Fixes Applied

### 0.1 Babel Standalone Version — Pinned to 7.23.10 ✅ FIXED June 2026
- **Problem:** `@babel/standalone` was loaded unpinned (`/babel.min.js` with no version). A breaking update to Babel standalone caused it to attempt to transform `<script type="module">` blocks — including the Firebase ES module init script — which threw `Cannot use import statement outside a module` and crashed the entire app. The app was completely non-functional for an extended period.
- **Fix applied:** Pinned Babel to `7.23.10` (`@babel/standalone@7.23.10/babel.min.js`). Firebase init script moved to load *before* Babel so it is never in scope for Babel's script scanner.
- **Why this matters:** Unpinned CDN dependencies are a silent risk. Any CDN update can break the app with no code change on our side.
- **Action required:** When upgrading Babel in future, test thoroughly in a branch before merging. Never use unpinned CDN URLs for critical dependencies.
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix is applied, do not revert to unpinned

---

### 0.2 Engine LLP Upload — Sonnet Hard Parse Failure ✅ FIXED June 2026
- **Problem:** Switching the Engine LLP Sheet extraction model from `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` to `claude-sonnet-4-6` (made to fix Haiku scrambling rows on the dense 4-section FAN/HPC/HPT/LPT table) caused a hard parse failure on every LLP upload — red banner "The AI could not extract structured data from this file." Not wrong data, total failure.
- **Root cause:** Sonnet returned a worked narrative ("I'll carefully analyze the document...") before the JSON, rather than JSON-only as Haiku had. The existing `api/extract.js` used a fragile regex (`/"type":"text","text":"([\s\S]*?)"\s*\}/`) against the raw Anthropic response text, which is not robust to reasoning-before-answer output and failed to isolate the JSON. Confirmed via temporary debug logging added to `extract.js` (`stop_reason: end_turn`, single content block, valid 200 response — the API call itself was fine; the failure was purely in how the response text was parsed/extracted).
- **Fix applied:** Two changes, both required together:
  1. `extract.js` rewritten to properly `JSON.parse` the Anthropic envelope and read the `.content` array (handles multi-block responses), then look for a fenced ` ```json ... ``` ` block and extract only what's inside it, with a brace-matching fallback if no fence is present (keeps Haiku's existing terse-JSON behaviour working unchanged for utilisation/APU LLP uploads).
  2. The LLP extraction prompt in `index.html` updated to explicitly permit Sonnet to reason through the table first, then mandates the final answer be wrapped in a ` ```json ``` ` fence with nothing else inside it.
- **Why this matters:** Different models can satisfy the same "return only JSON" instruction differently — terser models may comply literally while more capable models may reason first even when told not to. Response-parsing code should not assume a single content block or a JSON-only response; it should be robust to reasoning preludes, since this is likely to recur whenever models are swapped or upgraded for other extraction prompts (APU LLP, utilisation reports) in future.
- **Action required:** If Haiku is ever swapped to Sonnet (or any other model) for the remaining two extraction prompts (APU LLP, utilisation report), consider applying the same "reason then fence" prompt pattern proactively rather than waiting for a hard failure to surface it.
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix is applied; do not revert `extract.js` to regex-only parsing

---


### 0.3 Tech Spec Cover/Gallery Photo Placement — ✅ FIXED June 2026
- **Problem:** Cropping a LOPA via the LOPA Crop Tool before any other photo existed on the asset caused it to incorrectly become the tech spec cover photo; a subsequently uploaded Airframe photo would then route into the misc photo gallery instead of the cover slot.
- **Root cause:** Cover photo selection used `asset.photos[0]` (whatever happened to occupy array index 0) and the gallery used `photos.slice(1)` (skip index 0) — both positional, not label-based. The LOPA Crop Tool filters out any existing LOPA entry then appends the new one; if `photos` was empty at that point, the new LOPA landed at index 0 and was picked up by the cover-photo logic, which has no concept of "LOPA" vs "Airframe."
- **Fix applied:** Cover photo now selects `photos.find(p => p.label === "Airframe")`; gallery now filters `photos.filter(p => p.label !== "LOPA" && p.label !== "Airframe")`. Both label-based, upload order no longer matters.
- **Why this matters:** Any future logic touching the `photos` array should select/exclude by `label`, never by array position — the array order reflects upload sequence, not semantic meaning.
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix applied; do not reintroduce positional photo selection

---

### 0.4 Landing Gear "Calculated Current" — ✅ FULL REDESIGN DONE (June 2026)
- **Problem:** Landing Gear's "Calculated Current (Since New)" always exactly mirrored the airframe's TSN/CSN, regardless of whether a leg's P/N/S/N had ever been entered — surfaced during email-ingestion testing on the first new asset created via email (MSN 1946).
- **Root cause:** `refAirframeFH`/`refAirframeFC` had never been set to anything but `0` anywhere in the codebase, for any asset — not a bug specific to email-created assets. The formula `startFH/FC + (airframe now − refAirframe)` therefore always collapsed to just the airframe's own current total.
- **Stopgap fix (now fully superseded):** captured the airframe's current TSN/CSN into `refAirframeFH/FC` the moment a leg's P/N or S/N was first entered (`gearStartSet` flag). This has been replaced wholesale — see below.
- **Full redesign — done:** new data model built around **Reference Reading** (`refLegFH/FC` + `refAirframeFH/FC`, any matched leg+airframe reading taken at the same moment, doesn't need to be from an overhaul) for calculating current totals since new, and a separate **Last Overhaul Record** (`lastOverhaulDate`, `lastOverhaulFH/FC`) for anchoring Next Overhaul Due — deliberately kept as two independent inputs rather than conflated, since a reference reading using the overhaul-cycles countdown would give a wrong baseline. `currentFH/FC` ground truth override per leg (from the two aircraft's Gear Status Reports) always wins over the calculated figure, no tolerance check. Next Overhaul Due is now a dual calendar (10yr default)/cycle (20,000 default) limiter, lowest-wins, both editable, same red/amber/green pattern as LLPs. `startFH`/`startFC`/`gearStartSet` removed entirely. Applied across `index.html` (GearCard + LandingGearTab), `techSpecBuilder.js` (`lgSec`), and `calculations/utilisation.js`.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt

---

### 0.5 Email-Ingested Report Warnings — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026 (Review Queue built)
- **Original gap:** Brain 1 correctly detects warnings on email-ingested reports (gap, delta mismatch, S/N change) but nothing in the app surfaced them — only visible by opening Firestore directly. See roadmap Section 12a for the original open design question.
- **Design decision taken:** Severity is already implicit in Brain 1's own warning text — every warning that should hold a report back already starts with "⚠" (S/N change, delta mismatch, gap detected); informational notes (same-month merge, removal log) start with "ℹ"/"🔧" and are fine to apply immediately. No Brain 1 changes were needed to classify this — `hasHighSeverityWarning()` in `api/email-ingest.js` just checks the existing leading glyph.
- **Fix applied — hold-back via staging collection:** `api/email-ingest.js` now branches after Brain 1 runs: if `hasHighSeverityWarning(result.warnings)` is true, the already-computed `mergedAsset`/`utilisationRecord`/`warnings` are written to a new `pendingReports` Firestore collection instead of live `assets`/`utilisation` — nothing touches live state until reviewed. Low-severity-only reports (or `historyOnly` out-of-order uploads, which already never touch live state) continue exactly as before, applying immediately.
- **Review Queue UI:** New `ReviewQueueBanner` component on the Dashboard — collapsible banner ("⚠ N email reports awaiting review"), expands to a simple list (MSN, period, warning text, Apply/Discard per row). **Apply** writes the already-computed `mergedAsset`/`utilisationRecord` to their real collections (no re-parsing, no re-running Brain 1 — avoids any risk of a different result between ingest time and review time) and deletes the pending doc. **Discard** just deletes the pending doc, leaving the asset untouched — equivalent to rejecting the report outright.
- **Verified working June 2026:** Alan tested with a real months-gap scenario — held back correctly, appeared in the review queue, applied successfully.
- **Known tradeoff, accepted as-is for now:** a held-back report has no auto-apply timeout — if left unreviewed, the asset's figures stay stale indefinitely. Acceptable for a single-user pilot where Alan reviews promptly; revisit if this ever becomes a multi-user concern.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt

---

### 0.7 Backup/Restore — ✅ FIXED AND RETESTED July 2026
- **Problem discovered:** Nightly backup had been silently failing since approximately 25 June 2026. Root cause: the original Cloud Scheduler job called the Firestore export API directly, exporting to a fixed path `gs://vector-fleet-firestore-backups/nightly/`. On the first run this path was created. On every subsequent run, the export failed with a "path already exists" error — the overwrite behaviour assumed in the original design was never actually supported by the API. No alert was in place, so the failure went unnoticed.
- **Fix applied:** Replaced the Scheduler→API-direct pattern with a Cloud Function (`firestore-backup`, Node.js 22, europe-west2). The function generates a dated output path (`daily/YYYYMMDD/`) on each invocation, so the path is always new and the export never collides with a prior run. Cloud Scheduler rewired to trigger the function (HTTP target) rather than calling the Firestore export API directly. The stale `nightly/` folder in Cloud Storage was deleted. Rolling window is 7 days — older `daily/YYYYMMDD/` folders can be pruned manually or via a lifecycle rule.
- **Restore procedure (updated):**
  1. `gcloud firestore databases create --database=RECOVERY_NAME --location=europe-west2 --project=vector-fleet`
  2. `gcloud firestore import gs://vector-fleet-firestore-backups/daily/YYYYMMDD/ --database=RECOVERY_NAME --project=vector-fleet`
  3. Verify data in Firebase Console for `RECOVERY_NAME` before deciding next steps
- **Retest — 1 July 2026:** Fresh export → restore into temporary database → verified all current collections present (`assets`, `utilisation`, `shareTokens`, `pendingReports`, `settings`, invite-flow user records) → temporary database deleted. All collections confirmed intact.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt. See 1.7 for full infrastructure reference.

### 0.8 Audit Trail — ✅ DECISION MADE July 2026
- **Decision:** Global `auditLog` Firestore collection. Every material write (asset data, LLP uploads, utilisation confirmations, lease data when Layer 2 arrives) appends an entry with: `userId`, `userEmail`, `timestamp`, `assetId`, `assetMSN`, `action` (plain English — e.g. "Updated engine LLP data", "Confirmed utilisation report"). No field-level diffing — action-level only. Proportionate for a trusted inhouse team of 5–6 users.
- **Why global collection (not per-asset array):** Enables cross-fleet queries ("show everything user X changed in the last 7 days"). Per-asset arrays also risk hitting Firestore's 1MB document size limit over time on active assets. The plumbing is nearly identical — one helper function, called at every write, writing to `auditLog/{autoId}` instead of appending to an array.
- **Why not field-level diffing:** Overkill for a small trusted team. Action-level attribution is sufficient to investigate any "what happened here" question in practice. Can be added later if compliance requirements change.
- **Build timing:** Same session as role-based access (Admin/Editor/Viewer Firebase Auth custom claims — 1.3 follow-on). These are one piece of work, not two — you need to know who a user is before you can meaningfully stamp a write with their identity. Must be done before any second user is onboarded.
- **Viewer-only users:** Generate no audit entries by definition — only Editor/Admin writes are logged.
- **IT posture:** "Edit access is restricted by role. Every material write is attributed to the acting user with a timestamp and action type in a dedicated audit log. System-level sign-in logging is handled natively by Firebase Auth."
- **Priority:** 🔴 HIGH — must be in place before a second user is invited

### 0.6 Naming Inconsistency — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026 (docs-only pass complete)
- **The discrepancy (historical):** This document's own header and Section 13 ("VectorIQ Standard Tech Spec") and Section 15 (pricing/positioning), plus the equivalent sections of `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md`, used **VectorIQ** as the final product name, per the old history line "Vector Fleet → FleetIQ → TailIQ → VectorIQ (final)". All branding actually built and shipped had used **TailiQ** (lowercase i, capital Q) for several consecutive sessions before this was resolved — the codebase rename was already done in substance, only the strategic-section wording in these two documents lagged behind.
- **Resolved:** Alan gave explicit sign-off June 2026 ("yes, TailiQ, update the docs"). Full docs-only naming pass completed across both `TECH_DEBT.md` and `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` — every forward-looking/strategic "VectorIQ" reference (titles, product descriptions, the tech spec standard section, pricing/positioning copy, the "Generated by VectorIQ" footer line corrected to match the actual shipped "Powered by TailiQ Fleet Intelligence" copy) updated to TailiQ. The product naming lineage corrected to the full actual history: Vector Fleet → FleetIQ → TailIQ → VectorIQ → Rhumb → **TailiQ** (final, settled name). Historical changelog entries describing *past* find-and-replace work (e.g. "remaining VectorIQ/Vector Fleet Manager/Vector Group strings corrected to TailiQ") were deliberately left as-is, since they're accurately quoting the old strings that were being removed at the time, not asserting VectorIQ as current.
- **Deliberately not touched:** the `VECTORIQ_BANNER` base64 logo constant name and `viq-banner` CSS class in `techSpecBuilder.js` (internal/invisible, not user-facing copy — see the June 2026 Full Branding Sweep entry below for why), and the GitHub repo slug `vector-fleet` / live Vercel fallback URL `vector-fleet.vercel.app` (infrastructure identifiers, not product branding — renaming either is a separate decision, not raised this session).
- **Open follow-up, not blocking:** the two document filenames themselves (`TECH_DEBT.md`, `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md`) still carry "VECTORIQ" — left alone this session since renaming project-knowledge files is something Alan needs to do directly (delete + re-add under a new name), and other instructions/memory reference these files by their current names. Worth a quick decision next session on whether to rename them too (e.g. `TAILIQ_ROADMAP.md`) for full consistency.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt

## 0a. Notable Feature Additions (Non-Debt, Logged for Context)

### June 2026 — TailiQ Rebrand (App Header + Tech Spec)
Replaced the old orange "TailiQ | FLEET INTELLIGENCE PLATFORM" divider-style logo with a new slate/navy wordmark (green/red nav-light dots over the two i's, swoosh-tail "Q" icon) across two surfaces: the app header (`index.html`, transparent-background lockup sized for the dark header bar) and the tech spec cover banner (`VECTORIQ_BANNER` constant in `techSpecBuilder.js`, white-background version sized to match the previous banner's exact aspect ratio). Tech spec footer simplified from "Powered by TailiQ Fleet Intelligence Platform" to "Powered by TailiQ Fleet Intelligence" (dropped "Platform"). Logo assets were derived from a designer-approved reference image (cropped/recoloured/re-anti-aliased programmatically, not regenerated by AI), since AI image tools struggled to produce a clean horizontal banner lockup directly — this is the change that first surfaced the docs-vs-branding naming mismatch later resolved at 0.6.

### June 2026 — Mobile Header Layout Fix (No Horizontal Scroll)
Header previously forced `flexWrap:"nowrap"`, causing the Fleet Portfolio button and nav pill (Dashboard/Upload/Admin/Sign Out) to overflow off-screen on mobile, requiring horizontal scrolling to reach Admin/Sign Out. Fixed with responsive breakpoints: header row wraps below ~680px (logo on top, nav full-width underneath), nav buttons flex equally to fill available width, and Admin/Sign Out collapse to icon-only (⚙/⎋) below ~420px since Dashboard/Upload have no icon fallback and must keep their text.

### June 2026 — Tech Spec Specifications Two-Column Layout
Specifications table was pushing onto a second page on assets with many fields populated. Split into two columns (first half of fields left, second half right, including custom fields) using a **table-based** layout, not CSS grid/flexbox — confirmed via test render that wkhtmltopdf's renderer doesn't support `display:grid` and silently collapses it back to single-column. Worth remembering for any future tech-spec print CSS work: stick to floats/tables for multi-column layouts in this specific PDF pipeline.

### June 2026 — Dashboard Stat Boxes Removed
Removed the four summary stat boxes (Total Assets / All Clear / Attention / Critical) from the top of the Dashboard view per team feedback — judged redundant against the existing colour-coded status dot/LED system already on every row. Also removed now-dead CSS (`.stat-box`, `.stat-val`, `.stat-label`, `.data-row` — none were referenced by any other component, confirmed before removal).

### June 2026 — Tech Spec Logo Configurability
Logo on tech spec covers was previously hardcoded as a base64 constant in `index.html` (`TECH_SPEC_LOGO`, fixed 200px). Added a "Tech Spec Logo" card to Admin → Settings (slider 80–320px with live preview, custom upload via Cloudinary, revert-to-default), stored as a Firestore setting (`tech_spec_logo_url` / `tech_spec_logo_width`) using the same pattern as Engine Stock Photos. Falls back cleanly to the original hardcoded logo if unset, so no migration needed.
**Note:** This does not change the "TailiQ Standard Tech Spec" deliberate decision (Section 6) — the format itself remains non-configurable per client. Logo sizing/branding was already a permitted customisation; this just moved it out of source code.

### June 2026 — Specs/Landing Gear Additions (Team Feedback)
Added per internal team request: Winglets type field, Electronic Flight Bag installed/not-installed toggle (both in Specifications), 2 Year Check support in Check History, and a Wheels & Brakes section in the Landing Gear tab (separate from the three gear-leg cards — Main Wheels/Nose Wheels/Brake Units, each with optional Qty/P-N/Manufacturer). Tech spec renders Wheels & Brakes as an ATA-chapter-style table matching the AerCap reference format the team supplied, and only displays rows/the section at all where data has actually been entered.

### July 2026 — Specs / Landing Gear / APU Additions (Live Fleet)
A second round of field additions, extending the live fleet's asset detail views (the ordered Specifications set had previously been built out in the Prospect editor first — see 4.15 — and this pass brought the live fleet into line). Added: **Manufacturer** fields, **Cabin Seats P/N**, and installed/not-installed toggles for **QAR**, **RFDD** and **Enhanced Mode-S** (all in Specifications, each carrying the same 👁/🚫 `hiddenSpecFields` visibility toggle as the existing fields), plus a new **Avionics** photo upload type. Confirmed live by Alan, July 2026. The structured field-level Avionics tab is a separate item — see 4.14.

### June 2026 — Custom Domain Live (`app.tailiq.app`)
Added via Vercel → Cloudinary DNS-only CNAME (not proxied). `vector-fleet.vercel.app` kept active alongside it during the transition (see 1.5 for the resulting dual-origin CORS work). Share links generated anywhere in the app (asset view, Fleet Portfolio cards, QR codes, WhatsApp share) are now hardcoded to always build against `app.tailiq.app` regardless of which domain the admin happens to be browsing from when they generate the link — previously the link followed `window.location.origin`, so a link generated while browsing the old Vercel URL would bake that URL into the share link itself.

### June 2026 — Mobile UX: Contained Scroll Instead of Page-Level Scroll
Two separate mobile overflow issues, same fix pattern: the asset-view tab bar (Overview/Engines/Landing Gear/APU/Specs/History/Documents) was wider than the viewport on mobile, pushing Documents off-screen and forcing the whole page to scroll horizontally to reach it; the Utilisation History table had the same problem with its Delete button. Both fixed by scoping `overflowX:auto` to the tab bar/table container itself rather than the page — the rest of the layout stays fixed, and the tab bar/table scroll independently with a swipe.

### June 2026 — Landing Gear Reference Reading Now Edit-Only
The four Reference Reading fields (Leg TSN/CSN, Airframe TSN/CSN at reference — see 0.4) are calculation-only inputs, not something day-to-day viewers need to see. Hidden entirely outside admin edit mode on the Landing Gear tab; still fully functional for the current-totals calculation, just no longer cluttering the read view.

### June 2026 — Share Button Added to Fleet Portfolio Cards
Each card on the Fleet Portfolio grid view now has its own Share button beside Tech Spec, using the exact same per-asset `ShareModal`/token flow as the existing asset-detail Share button — a second access point to the same sharing mechanism, not a new portfolio-wide share concept (per-asset sharing only, confirmed with Alan).

### June 2026 — Full Branding Sweep + Tech Spec Disclaimer System Rebuilt
Closed out the remaining "VectorIQ"/"Vector Fleet Manager"/"Vector Group" strings across `index.html` (sign-in screen, browser tab title, print banners, in-app guide text), `share.html` (title, error states, read-only banner), `techSpecBuilder.js` and `calculations/utilisation.js` (default disclaimer text), and `api/share/[token].js` (code comments). Deliberately left untouched: the `VECTORIQ_BANNER` base64 logo constant name and `viq-banner` CSS class in `techSpecBuilder.js` — both are invisible internals, and the former sits directly against the large embedded image data that must never be edited around.

Also rebuilt how the tech spec disclaimer text works, since there was previously no UI to edit it at all (it could only ever be set once, at asset creation, by editing a hardcoded template string in `index.html`). New three-tier precedence: **per-asset override** (new editable field, Specs tab → Edit All) → **fleet-wide default** (new "Tech Spec Logo & Disclaimer" card in Admin → Settings — combined with the logo settings into one card deliberately, so both are edited together) → **hardcoded fallback** in `techSpecBuilder.js`/`utilisation.js` if neither is set. `buildTechSpecHTML()` gained a fourth parameter (`disclaimerOverride`) to carry the fleet-wide default through. The public share-link flow (`api/share/[token].js` → `share.html`) was a known gap initially — the public token endpoint had no way to see the fleet-wide Settings value, since it only returns an allowlisted slice of one asset's fields — closed by having `api/share/[token].js` fetch `settings/default_disclaimer` directly (outside the asset allowlist mechanism, wrapped in its own try/catch so a settings-read failure can never break the share link itself) and `share.html` pass it through as the same fourth parameter. Verified working: a fleet-wide default set in Settings now appears correctly on a public share link for an asset with no per-asset override.

### June 2026 — Engine At-Shop / Title Engine Tracking (New Feature)
A long-dormant scaffold turned out to already exist: `eng.atShop`/`g.atShop` flags were already read by the Dashboard's `LLPCell`/`LGCell`, the Engines tab title badge, and the Landing Gear section title (all rendering a 🔧 spanner) — but nothing in the codebase ever set `atShop` to `true`. This session wired up the write path for engines specifically (landing gear at-shop tracking remains display-only, not in scope here).

**Trigger — no manual data entry needed.** The existing engine S/N-change detection (Brain 1, unchanged) already fires whenever a utilisation report shows a different engine S/N than what's on file — that detection is now the trigger. In the post-save warnings panel, an engine S/N mismatch renders as an interactive `EngineSNAction` row instead of plain text, offering three choices:
- **At Shop** — opens a small form to capture the previous engine's removal snapshot (S/N locked/auto-filled, TSN/CSN/date pre-filled from the last known report but fully editable, since the actual removal happens mid-month and won't match the last report's figures exactly — confirmed with Alan this must stay manually correctable). On confirm, the snapshot is written to `eng.titleEngine` and `eng.atShop=true`; the new S/N from the report is already live (merge already happened before the warning is shown).
- **Permanent** — no-op in the normal case; if `atShop` was already true (a second swap), clears `atShop`/`titleEngine` to finalise the new engine as the permanent one.
- **Engine Returned** — auto-suggested when the new report's S/N matches the frozen `titleEngine.sn`; just clears `atShop`/`titleEngine` (the merge already wrote the correct current TSN/CSN from this report).

**Engines tab** gained a frozen, read-only "Title Engine — Removed [date]" panel whenever `atShop` is true, with the same Engine Returned / Confirm Permanent actions available persistently (covers the case where the decision isn't made right at upload time).

**Tech spec required zero changes** — `techSpecBuilder.js` only ever reads the live engine record, never `titleEngine`, so the currently-fitted engine is automatically all that ever appears, even while at shop. Per Alan: a tech spec ever needing to show an at-shop engine is a rare edge case, deliberately deferred.

**`calculations/utilisation.js` (Brain 1) needed zero changes** — `detectSNChanges`'s existing `{component, position, previousSN, newSN}` output already carries everything the Body-layer toggle needs; this was deliberately kept this way to avoid touching tested delta/S/N logic.

### June 2026 — Email Review Queue (New Feature, see 0.5)
See 0.5 above for full detail — Dashboard banner + Apply/Discard list for email-ingested reports carrying high-severity warnings, backed by a new `pendingReports` Firestore collection.

### June 2026 — Nav-Light Dot Order Corrected Across All Brand Assets
The red/green nav-light dots over the two i's in the TailiQ wordmark are meant to follow real aircraft convention (red = left/port, green = right/starboard), but five separate embedded assets had it backwards (green-left/red-right): `techspecheader.png`, `websiteheadernavy.png` (both project-knowledge source files), `VECTORIQ_BANNER` (base64 constant in `techSpecBuilder.js`), `HEADER_LOGO_NAVY`/`HEADER_LOGO_WHITE` (base64 constants in `index.html`), and the landing page wordmark (`tailiq_landing.html`). Fixed by swapping the actual pixel blocks of each dot (not recolouring — recolouring left an anti-aliased fringe of the wrong colour at the edges) so the swap is artefact-free.
**Workflow lesson:** mid-session, a follow-up build pass accidentally re-copied from the original uploaded `techSpecBuilder__5_.js` instead of the already-fixed output from earlier in the same session, silently reverting the `VECTORIQ_BANNER` fix. Caught when Alan spotted it live in a generated PDF. When resuming work on a file already fixed earlier in a session, always continue from the last fixed output, never re-copy from the original upload.

### June 2026 — Tech Spec Cover QR Code & Footer Redesign (QR pill now disabled — code retained)
A gold-outline pill was built for the tech spec cover page — red/green nav-light dots, "Find out more" + `tailiq.app`, and a real scannable QR code (generated server-side via the Python `qrcode` library, embedded as a base64 `QR_TAILIQ` constant in `techSpecBuilder.js`). This links to `tailiq.app`, separate from the existing per-asset Share-modal QR — the two should not be conflated. Confirmed clickable via `/URI` link annotation in actual generated PDF output.

**Current status: pill disabled for internal use.** `QR_TAILIQ` constant is retained in `techSpecBuilder.js`. `COVER_PILL` is now set to `''` with a comment. To re-enable, restore the original COVER_PILL definition from git history. No other changes needed.

Footer simplified across every page to the same red/green nav-light dots + slate text, no pill container — this footer change remains in place.

**Notable gotcha — wkhtmltopdf/browser print pagination and `height:100%`.** Getting the pill to sit at the true bottom of the cover page (rather than floating immediately under the date) required several iterations:
- An explicit fixed-unit height (`height:235mm` or similar) on a `display:table` cover element is **not reliably honoured** for pagination purposes — content can render at a fraction of that height with no error, no console warning, nothing — confirmed by direct pixel measurement of rendered output, not by eye (two earlier "this looks right" visual checks were later proven wrong this way). The fix that actually works: `html,body{height:100%}` chained to `.cover{height:100%}` (a percentage, not a fixed unit) on a `display:table`/`table-row`/`table-cell` structure with the bottom cell `vertical-align:bottom`.
- That fix must be **scoped inside `@media print`**. The same generated HTML serves two different contexts — the in-app preview (rendered on-screen, unpaginated, normal scroll) and the actual Print/Save PDF output (the browser's native print engine, which does paginate and where `@page` rules come alive). Applying the `height:100%` table trick unscoped stretches the live on-screen preview to fill its container, producing a near-empty, very tall page in the modal — exactly the bug Alan caught. Once scoped to `@media print`, the preview renders compact and natural while the printed/saved PDF still gets the bottom-anchored pill. Worth remembering for any future tech-spec layout work that needs different behaviour in the live preview vs. the printed output.

### June 2026 — APU Given Its Own Tech Spec Page
Extends the page-break work already logged in 4.2 (which addressed APU shop-visit history scope, not page placement). APU now gets `${PAGE_FOOTER}<div class="pb"></div>` immediately before its `<h3>` heading, the same pattern already used between the two engines and before Landing Gear — it no longer shares a page with Landing Gear/Wheels & Brakes. Frees up room for both: Landing Gear/Wheels & Brakes now have a full page to themselves, and APU's full LLP table (which was never actually row-limited — the existing `llpRows()` renders every entry regardless of count, it just looked sparse against the available space when sharing a page) now has visible room to show a long LLP stack on a real asset. Shop visit display intentionally **left unchanged** — confirmed with Alan this should stay most-recent-only on the full airframe spec per the existing 4.2 decision, not expanded to full history alongside the new page room.

### July 2026 — Engine Cover Art Set Complete (All 8 Engines)
All engines from the Section 9 supported-types list (`tailiq-engines-scoping-handoff.md`) now have finished cover art: CFM56-5B, CFM56-7B, V2500-A5, LEAP-1A, LEAP-1B, PW1100G, CF34, CF6. "Other" deliberately has no art (manual entry only, by design — not an omission).

**Background color-match fix applied across the board.** Each AI-generated image came out slightly off the locked brand navy (`#102A43`) — actual sampled backgrounds ranged from `#011A3E` to `#0B203F` depending on the generation batch. Corrected via a uniform pixel-shift: compute the delta between the sampled background color and `#102A43`, then apply that same delta to every pixel in the image. This re-bases the whole image onto the correct navy while leaving linework and anti-aliasing completely untouched — more reliable than a threshold-based color-replace for this kind of flat-background technical art, since it doesn't require isolating foreground from background at all.

Files named `{ENGINE}_engine_cover.png`, matching existing project convention.

**Process note:** two naming mix-ups occurred mid-session when matching uploaded AI-generated images to engine names (LEAP-1B/PW1100G swapped initially, then a CF34/CF6 pairing had to be inferred from an upload-order note and flagged for visual confirmation before filing). Worth a quick visual sanity-check against the actual generated art on any future batch, since AI image generation tools don't preserve engine-name metadata — the mapping is manual and error-prone.

**Not done, not urgent:** CFM56-7B gold-line variant (`#C9A227`) — the original prompt template called for a white-vs-gold side-by-side comparison before picking a standard. Only worth revisiting if gold is considered as an alternative treatment.

Built from a user-generated reference image, cleaned up via connected-component extraction (isolating the ring and tail as the two real glyph pieces, discarding a stray line artifact and anti-aliasing noise), colors snapped to locked brand hex (`#102A43` navy, `#FFFFFF` white, `#657584` slate). Delivered as a full size set — 1024/512/192/180/48/32/16px, both solid-background and transparent variants — plus a bundled `favicon.ico`. Verified legible at true 16px size, which is the size that killed the earlier wordmark-crop attempt. ## 1. Critical Security & Risk — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026


All items in this section were addressed in a dedicated security session (June 2026), prompted by Brain 1 + Brain 2 extraction completing and Layer 2 (financial data) approaching. Kept here with full history rather than deleted, since the "why" and "what we learned" still matter for future sessions.

### 1.1 Firebase Config Exposed Client-Side — ✅ RESOLVED
- **Original concern:** Firebase config object (apiKey, projectId, etc.) lived hardcoded inside `index.html`, visible via page source/DevTools.
- **What we actually learned before fixing it:** This was **not a real secret**. Firebase's web SDK is designed to ship this config to the browser — `apiKey` here just identifies which Firebase project to talk to; it grants no access by itself. The real access-control boundary is Firestore security rules (see 1.2) and Firebase Auth (see 1.3), not hiding this object. Reframing this correctly mattered — it changed the fix from "urgent before any financial data" to "hygiene, do when convenient."
- **Fix applied anyway, for hygiene:** Added `api/config.js` (Vercel serverless function) that reads `FIREBASE_API_KEY`, `FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN`, `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, `FIREBASE_STORAGE_BUCKET`, `FIREBASE_MESSAGING_SENDER_ID`, `FIREBASE_APP_ID` from Vercel env vars and returns them as JSON. `index.html`'s `firebase-init` module script now `fetch('/api/config')` and calls `initializeApp(cfg.firebase)` once that resolves, instead of using a hardcoded object. This keeps the values out of GitHub going forward (existing git history still has them — not rotated, since they aren't real secrets).
- **Note for future sessions:** Because `index.html` is a static file with no build step, this required adding a tiny serverless endpoint rather than a true build-time env var substitution. `initializeApp` now waits on a `fetch`, so Firebase init is asynchronous where it previously wasn't — see new `_configError` / `firebase-config-error` event handling in `App` for the failure-mode UI (shows a "Configuration Error" screen instead of hanging silently if `/api/config` fails).
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix applied; do not revert to hardcoded config

---

### 1.2 Firebase Security Rules — Development / Permissive Mode — ✅ RESOLVED
- **Original concern:** Firestore rules were in test mode (`allow read, write: if true`) with an auto-expiry of July 12, 2026, after which the app would have locked out everyone, including Alan.
- **Fix applied:** Rules tightened to `allow read, write: if request.auth != null;` — no expiry. Published in Firebase Console and confirmed working (signed in successfully, fleet data loads, before locking the rule in — correct order to avoid self-lockout).
- **What's still open:** This is a real auth gate, but **not yet scoped by `companyId` or role** — it's "any signed-in user can do anything," which is fine for a single-user internal pilot but is not multi-tenant isolation. See 2.3 (companyId on documents) — that and role-based rules are the same piece of remaining work, now correctly understood as one item rather than separate.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM (downgraded from 🔴) — scoping by companyId/role needed before a second user is added, not urgent for current single-user pilot
- **Effort remaining:** 1 session, bundled with Brain 1's `companyId` backfill work (2.3) and role-based Firebase Auth (1.3 follow-up)

---

### 1.3 No Authentication — ✅ RESOLVED (minimal version)
- **Original concern:** No login at all — anyone with the URL had full read/write access.
- **Fix applied:** Firebase Auth (email/password) added. Single user created (Alan). `index.html` now shows a `SignInScreen` component blocking the whole app until `onAuthStateChanged` resolves to a signed-in user; a Sign Out button added to the header. Confirmed working end-to-end.
- **What's still open:** This is a single-user gate, not role-based access. Admin/Editor/Viewer roles (originally scoped here) are still backlog — see Section 5, Infrastructure/Product.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM (downgraded from 🔴) — role-based access is the remaining piece, needed before a second user joins, not urgent today
- **Effort remaining:** 1–2 sessions for role-based access on top of the existing single-user auth

---

### 1.4 Cloudinary API Credentials — ✅ RESOLVED
- **Original concern:** Cloudinary `CLOUD_NAME` and `UPLOAD_PRESET` exposed client-side alongside Firebase config.
- **What we learned before fixing it:** Same nuance as 1.1 — these are **not secrets**. An unsigned upload preset is specifically designed to be used client-side without a secret; that's what "unsigned" means. There is no actual Cloudinary API secret anywhere in this codebase.
- **Fix applied anyway, for hygiene:** Folded into the same `api/config.js` endpoint as 1.1 — `CLOUDINARY_CLOUD_NAME` and `CLOUDINARY_UPLOAD_PRESET` now live in Vercel env vars, fetched at load and stashed on `window._cloudinaryConfig`. `uploadToCloudinary()` reads them via `CLOUD_NAME()`/`UPLOAD_PRESET()` (now functions, not constants) with a guard that throws a clear error if called before config has loaded.
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix applied; do not revert to hardcoded constants
- **Note:** If Cloudinary signed uploads are ever introduced (e.g. to restrict upload abuse), that would involve a real secret (the Cloudinary API secret) and would need a proper signed-URL serverless endpoint — different from what exists today.

---

### 1.5 Anthropic API Key Handling — ✅ CONFIRMED ALREADY SAFE
- **Original concern:** Assumed the Claude API key might be exposed client-side or called directly from the browser.
- **What we found on review:** It already wasn't. `api/extract.js` reads `process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` server-side and proxies the request — the key has never been in client-side code. No fix was needed here; this item existed on the original list as an assumption that turned out to be wrong.
- **Real issue found instead:** `api/extract.js` had `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: '*'` — meaning *any* website could call this endpoint and burn through the Anthropic API budget, even though the key itself was safe. **Fixed:** locked to `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: 'https://vector-fleet.vercel.app'`.
- **Update (custom domain session):** `app.tailiq.app` went live as a second domain alongside the existing `vector-fleet.vercel.app`. `api/extract.js` and `api/share/[token].js` now use an `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` array (both domains) that echoes back whichever origin matches, instead of a single hardcoded string. `api/email-ingest.js`'s `APP_ORIGIN` constant was deliberately left untouched — that's a server-to-server fetch, not a browser call, so it isn't subject to CORS at all. Drop the `.vercel.app` entry from `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` in a future cleanup session once `app.tailiq.app` is confirmed solid for everything.
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — CORS fix applied; do not revert to wildcard origin

---

### 1.6 Abandoned Supabase Projects — ✅ RESOLVED
- **Original concern:** Two Supabase projects (Stockholm `agyjnlecslwcbakahyax`, Frankfurt `jxmzcdthrcsolddldhgqh`) remained live with Edge Functions deployed and JWT verify off.
- **Fix applied:** Both projects decommissioned (June 2026).
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked here, removed from Infrastructure Reference table (Section 7)

---

### 1.7 No Firestore Backup — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026 / FIXED AND RETESTED July 2026
- **Original concern:** No backup or recovery path existed for Firestore data — a deleted document, a bad bulk write, or a Firebase-side incident would have meant permanent data loss with no way back.
- **Initial fix (June 2026):**
  1. Upgraded Firebase from Spark to Blaze billing plan (required for scheduled exports). Set a €5/month Cloud Billing budget alert as a tripwire.
  2. Created Cloud Storage bucket `gs://vector-fleet-firestore-backups` in `europe-west2` (same region as Firestore — GDPR data residency, see Section 6).
  3. Created Cloud Scheduler job calling the Firestore export API directly, exporting to `gs://vector-fleet-firestore-backups/nightly/`. Manually triggered and verified once at setup.
- **Bug found and fixed (July 2026 — see 0.7):** The original Scheduler→API-direct pattern was silently failing since approximately 25 June 2026. The Firestore export API does not overwrite an existing path — it fails with a "path already exists" error on every run after the first. No alert was in place, so the failure went unnoticed.
- **Fix applied:** Cloud Function `firestore-backup` (Node.js 22, europe-west2) generates a dated output path (`daily/YYYYMMDD/`) on each invocation — path is always new, no collision. Cloud Scheduler rewired to trigger the function via HTTP. Old `nightly/` folder deleted. Rolling 7-day window of dated snapshots.
- **Retest — 1 July 2026:** Fresh export → restore into temporary database → all current collections confirmed intact (`assets`, `utilisation`, `shareTokens`, `pendingReports`, `settings`, invite-flow user records) → temporary database deleted. Supersedes the original June 2026 test which predated several of these collections.
- **Restore procedure:**
  1. `gcloud firestore databases create --database=RECOVERY_NAME --location=europe-west2 --project=vector-fleet`
  2. `gcloud firestore import gs://vector-fleet-firestore-backups/daily/YYYYMMDD/ --database=RECOVERY_NAME --project=vector-fleet`
  3. Verify data in Firebase Console for `RECOVERY_NAME` before deciding next steps (promoting it, or extracting specific documents back into `(default)`)
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix applied; do not remove the Cloud Function or Cloud Scheduler job, do not downgrade back to Spark

---

### 1.8 Git Tagging Convention — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026
- **Original concern:** No way to identify or roll back to a known-good deployed state — every session overwrote `index.html` wholesale with no record of "this was the version that was working."
- **Fix applied:** Adopted GitHub Releases (not local git tags, since Alan works exclusively via the GitHub web UI with no local clone) using the format `vX.Y-description`, with the release description built from that session's summary bullet points. First tag published: `v1.3-techspec-branding-backups`.
- **Why this matters:** Gives a concrete rollback point and a human-readable changelog without requiring any change to Alan's existing upload-and-replace workflow.
- **Action required going forward:** Tag a release at the end of any session that leaves the app in a good, deployed state — especially before starting something riskier (e.g. email ingestion).
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix applied; keep tagging stable states going forward

---

### 1.9 Public Share-Link Endpoint — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026
- **Original concern:** None pre-existing — this is a new surface area (Section 12 of the roadmap), logged here because it's new unauthenticated attack surface and deserves the same scrutiny as everything else in this section.
- **Fix applied:** `api/share/[token].js` — a public, unauthenticated Vercel function using the Firebase Admin SDK (which bypasses Firestore rules by design, for trusted server-side code only). It looks up `shareTokens/{token}`, fails closed on missing/expired/revoked tokens, and returns asset data through an **allowlist**, not a denylist — `ALLOWED_FIELDS` in that file is the only thing that can ever leave Firestore via this endpoint, meaning Layer 2 financial fields can't leak through it even by accident later, since they'd need to be explicitly added to appear at all. CORS locked to the production domain, same pattern as `extract.js` (see 1.5).
- **Why a serverless function instead of relaxing Firestore rules:** keeps the existing `request.auth != null` blanket rule completely untouched — no new hole was opened in the rules themselves.
- **Note for future sessions:** if Layer 2 financial fields are ever added to an asset document, double-check `ALLOWED_FIELDS` in `api/share/[token].js` is NOT updated to include them unless a financial share tier is explicitly designed and signed off (see roadmap Section 12 — only the Tech Spec tier was built; Portfolio/Full Asset tiers are deferred).
- **Priority:** 📌 DECISION — fix applied; do not switch this endpoint to a denylist, and do not relax Firestore rules as an alternative approach

---

### 1.10 GitHub Pages Decommissioned — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026 (resolves 2.5)
- **Original concern:** App was deployed on both GitHub Pages and Vercel with no clear canonical URL — see 2.5 below.
- **Fix applied:** Confirmed GitHub Pages was live and still rebuilding from `main` on every push, then unpublished it via repo Settings → Pages. Vercel confirmed as the sole live URL, with sign-in and fleet data loading correctly tested post-change.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — see 2.5 for the original entry, kept for history

## 2. Architecture 🟡

### 2.1 Single HTML File Structure
- **Current state:** Entire application lives in one large `index.html` / JSX file. All UI, logic, Firebase calls, and parsing prompts are combined
- **Intended fix:** Migrate to proper component and module structure — either:
  - Clean up within current stack (split into separate JS/JSX files)
  - Full migration to Next.js (recommended if scaling to multi-user)
- **Risk if left:** Increasingly painful to maintain as features grow. Hard for any engineer to navigate. Calculation logic cannot be tested independently of UI. Claude sessions become less efficient as the file gets longer
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — before Layer 3 build or before any engineer joins
- **Effort:** 2–3 sessions for reorganisation; 3–5 sessions for full Next.js migration

---

### 2.2 Calculation Logic Not Separated From UI (Brain/Body) — ✅ Brains 1 & 2 both done
- **Current state (updated June 2026):** Both Layer 1 brains are now extracted and confirmed. Brain 2 (LLP countdown) — `llpCalculator.js` in `/calculations`, pure functions (`calcLLPRem`, `lowestLimiter`), no UI/Firebase deps, all call sites in `index.html` verified (dashboard, asset detail, tech spec generation, forms). Brain 1 (utilisation report parsing — delta verification, S/N change detection, merge-into-existing-asset logic) — confirmed extracted to `/calculations/utilisation.js` as pure logic, following the same pattern as Brain 2.
  Brains 3–6 (Fly-Forward, Risk Peak, Shortfall, Maintenance Calendar) should be built directly into `/calculations` from day one — no embedded-then-extract step needed.
- **Risk if left:** None remaining for Layer 1 — both brains clean. Future risk is only in not maintaining this discipline for Brains 3–6 during Layer 2 build.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done for Layer 1 — discipline must continue for Layer 2 brains
- **Effort:** Brain 1 — done. Brain 2 — done.

---

### 2.3 No `companyId` on Existing Firestore Documents
- **Current state:** Current Firestore documents do not have a `companyId` (tenant identifier) field — built as single-tenant from the start
- **Intended fix:** Add `companyId` field to all existing and future documents before any multi-tenant features are built. All queries must filter by `companyId`
- **Risk if left:** Multi-tenant expansion requires a full data migration and query rewrite if not addressed early. The longer this is left, the more documents exist without the field
- **Now bundled with:** Firestore rules are auth-gated (1.2, resolved June 2026) but not yet scoped by `companyId`/role — this backfill and the rules-scoping work are effectively one session, not two
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — do this before any second user or team is onboarded
- **Effort:** 1 session — migration script + update all write/read functions + scope Firestore rules by companyId/role together

---

### 2.4 Google Sheets / Google Apps Script Legacy
- **Current state:** App originally used Google Sheets as database via Apps Script. Migration to Firebase completed. Old Google Sheets may still exist with some production data
- **Intended fix:** Confirm all data migrated to Firebase. Archive or delete old Google Sheets. Remove any remaining Apps Script references from codebase
- **Risk if left:** Confusion about source of truth. Potential for someone to update old Sheet thinking it still matters
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM
- **Effort:** 30 minutes to confirm and archive

---

### 2.5 Dual Hosting (GitHub Pages + Vercel) — ✅ RESOLVED June 2026 (see 1.10)
- **Current state:** Resolved — GitHub Pages unpublished, Vercel confirmed as sole live URL. Full detail logged at 1.10 (kept under Security & Risk since this was treated as part of the V1 surface-area cleanup, not just a tidiness item).
- **Priority:** ✅ Done

## 3. Data & Calculation Logic 🟡

### 3.1 LLP Extrapolation — Linear Only
- **Current state:** When a disk sheet is older than the most recent utilisation report, LLP remaining life is extrapolated forward using a simple cycle delta (disk sheet CSN subtracted from current CSN)
- **Intended fix:** Account for seasonal variation — a disk sheet from February on a summer-seasonal operator should extrapolate using the seasonal profile, not a flat monthly average
- **Risk if left:** Minor inaccuracy for seasonal operators. Acceptable for now but should be refined during Layer 2 build
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — refine during Layer 2 build alongside seasonality profile feature
- **Effort:** 1 session once seasonality profiles are built

---

### 3.2 Delta Verification — Edge Cases
- **Current state:** Delta verification checks period FH/FC against the calculated difference between reports. Some edge cases (first report ever uploaded, reports uploaded out of order, gap months) may not be handled gracefully
- **Intended fix:** Harden the delta logic to handle:
  - First report (no previous to compare against)
  - Out-of-order uploads
  - Gap months (missing reports)
  - Component changes mid-period
- **Risk if left:** False positive or false negative validation flags confuse the user
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM
- **Effort:** 1 session

---

### 3.3 S/N Change Detection — Partial Implementation
- **Current state:** Serial number change detection (engine, APU, landing gear swaps) flags when a component S/N changes between reports. Unclear if all component types are covered and whether the flag persists correctly in Firestore
- **Intended fix:** Verify all component types covered. Ensure change is logged with date and previous/new S/N in Firestore audit trail — not just a UI flag
- **Risk if left:** Component swap not properly recorded could affect LLP tracking accuracy and lease return calculations
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM
- **Effort:** 1 session to audit and harden

---

### 4.1 Admin PIN Protection — Superseded by Firebase Auth, Legacy Code Removed — ✅ DONE
- **Original state:** Admin PIN protection had been removed during development; app was unprotected at the admin level even before the broader no-auth issue (1.3) was resolved.
- **Resolution:** Firebase Auth (1.3) now gates the entire app, including admin functions — superseding the need for a separate PIN layer.
- **Fix applied:** Legacy PIN code path (`admin_pin` setting, `isAdmin` state) confirmed removed — no longer a second overlapping "admin access" concept in the code.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt

### 4.2 Tech Spec Page Break Refinements — APU Shop Visit ✅ RESOLVED June 2026 (Specifications fix from earlier in this section remains as logged)
- **Original problem:** PDF tech spec page breaks not perfectly optimised — APU Shop Visit History table observed creeping onto a second page on assets with several shop visits recorded.
- **Root cause reframe:** Discussed with Alan — the tech spec only ever needs to show the *most recent* APU shop visit, not the full history (full history remains genuinely useful elsewhere, just not on a client-facing spec). This turned a pagination-fighting problem into a scope change: stop rendering the full table.
- **Fix applied:** `techSpecBuilder.js`'s APU section now passes `(apu.shopVisits||[]).slice(-1)` into `svRows()` instead of the full array; heading changed to "Most Recent APU Shop Visit". The "Since Last Shop Visit" summary row is unaffected (already derived from the last visit either way). In-app APU tab (`index.html`) mirrored the same display-only change — table shows only the most recent visit (with a "(N on file)" hint in the header when more exist), Add/Edit/Delete still operate on the full underlying array, so no history is lost and no Firestore migration was needed.
- **Engine shop visits — same pattern, but split by context:** the full airframe tech spec also now shows most-recent-only for both engines (matches APU), but the *standalone single-engine* tech spec (Engines tab → Generate Tech Spec on one engine) still shows full shop visit history, since that document's whole purpose is a complete engine record for a buyer/MRO. Implemented via a `fullHistory` parameter on `engSec()` in `techSpecBuilder.js` — full-airframe calls default to `false`, the engine-only branch passes `true`.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt

### 4.8 In-App Guide Is Stale — ✅ DONE
- **Original problem:** The in-app guide had not been updated to reflect recent changes — Firestore backup/restore procedure, share-token/QR system, email ingestion, rebrand, and other Layer 1 hardening since it was last written.

### 4.9 Self-Service User Invite Flow — ✅ DONE
- **Original problem:** Adding a new user meant Alan manually creating them in the Firebase Console with a temporary password he set and shared directly — no in-app flow, and the new user couldn't set their own password from the start.
- **Fix applied:** New `api/invite-user.js` — verifies the caller has a valid Firebase ID token, creates the Firebase Auth user server-side via the Admin SDK (reusing the existing `FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY`/`FIREBASE_CLIENT_EMAIL`/`FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` credentials already in place for the share-token system), generates a password-reset code, and sends a TailiQ-branded invite email via SendGrid (`invites@tailiq.app`, new `SENDGRID_API_KEY` env var, Mail Send-only permission). New "Invite User" card in Admin → Settings, and a new `SetPasswordScreen` view (`?view=set-password&oobCode=...`) checked before the sign-in gate.
- **Bugs hit and fixed along the way:** (1) `app.tailiq.app` had to be added to Firebase Console → Authentication → Settings → Authorized domains, or Firebase rejected the reset link. (2) Firebase's `generatePasswordResetLink()` defaults to its own hosted action page (`*.firebaseapp.com/__/auth/action`), which loses the `oobCode` on its redirect to `continueUrl` — fixed by extracting the `oobCode` directly and linking straight to `app.tailiq.app/?view=set-password&oobCode=...`, bypassing Firebase's hosted page entirely. (3) A real init-order race caused a blank-screen crash: the Firebase init module set `window._firebaseReady = true` (firing the `firebase-ready` event) before `window._auth` was actually assigned a few lines later — any code reacting immediately to the event found `_auth` undefined. Fixed by reordering so `_auth` is fully assigned before the ready event fires.
- **Still open:** The endpoint only checks "is this any signed-in user," not specifically "is this an admin" — matches the existing app-wide gap, since "Admin" is currently just a client-side PIN, not a Firebase custom claim (see 1.3/Section 5, role-based access). Real RBAC is separate future work.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — verified end-to-end (invite sent → branded email arrives → link lands on `app.tailiq.app` → password set → sign-in succeeds)

An "Add Check" control was added (June 2026) with 2/6/12 Year presets (filtered to exclude what's already on the asset) plus a free-text name option, so nothing stops duplicate or inconsistently-named entries if someone uses the free-text path carelessly.
- **Detail:** The auto-next-due calculation now parses years generically from the check name (`/(\d+)\s*Year/i`), so a check must be named "N Year Check" to get automatic due-date calculation — anything else just won't auto-fill (graceful degradation, not a crash).
- **Risk if left:** Low — worst case is a messy or duplicate check name that doesn't auto-calculate its due date, not a data integrity issue.
- **Priority:** 🟢 LOW — revisit only if duplicate/messy check names start appearing in practice

## 6. Deliberate Design Decisions

📌 **These are NOT debt. Document so engineers don't accidentally reverse them.**

### Parse and Discard for Lease PDFs
Lease documents are commercially sensitive — they contain negotiated reserve rates that are proprietary to the lessor. Deliberate decision: PDF is parsed in browser memory only. Extracted figures are shown for user confirmation. PDF is never written to Firebase Storage or any persistent location. Only confirmed figures are saved to Firestore. Do not change this without explicit sign-off.

### companyId on All Firestore Documents
Even though TailiQ currently operates as single-tenant (one company), every Firestore document must have a `companyId` field. This is intentional future-proofing for multi-user expansion if needed. Do not remove these fields or write queries without `WHERE companyId = x` filters.

### Firebase europe-west2 Region
Deliberate choice for GDPR compliance — data stored in EU. Do not migrate to US regions without reviewing GDPR implications and getting sign-off.

### Firebase Over Supabase
Supabase was used initially but abandoned due to persistent DNS resolution issues with Edge Functions. Firebase is the permanent backend. This was not a temporary decision. Do not attempt to reintroduce Supabase.

### Brain/Body Separation (Calculations vs UI)
All mathematical logic must live in `/calculations` folder as pure functions — input numbers in, output numbers out, no Firebase calls, no UI dependencies. UI components call service layer which calls calculation layer. This is non-negotiable architecture. Do not embed calculations in components.

### TailiQ Standard Tech Spec Format
The tech spec output format is the TailiQ standard — it is not configurable per client. This is a deliberate product decision. Do not build per-client format customisation. Company logo and branding are the only permitted customisation. "Powered by TailiQ Fleet Intelligence" footer must always appear.

### Sub-50 Asset Lessor Focus
TailiQ is deliberately built for lessors with under 50 assets. Features and UI are optimised for this segment. Do not scope features that only make sense for large enterprise lessors (100+ assets, multi-desk teams, CRM pipeline management). Stay focused.

### 4.15 Ad Hoc / Prospect Asset Tech Spec (Prospect Asset Flow) — ✅ DONE July 2026 (v1.12)
- **Built (initial pass):** New `type:"prospect"` flag on the shared asset schema (`makeBlankAsset()` factory now backs both live-fleet and prospect creation). Prospect assets are filtered out of Dashboard, Fleet Portfolio, Admin → Assets, and Upload's matching pool — completely separate from the live fleet everywhere except the shared tech spec generator. Full Specifications field set rewritten as one ordered list matching Alan's reference sheet exactly (Aircraft Configuration → Passenger Seating Config/Manufacturer/P·N → Attendant Seats → Galleys Installed → Lavatories → Winglets → CDSS → RFDD → QAR → EFB → Enhanced Mode-S → ADS-B → CPDLC → TCAS 7.1 → Cargo), each with the same 👁/🚫 `hiddenSpecFields` visibility toggle as the live fleet's `SpecsTab`, plus 5 custom-field slots. Landing Gear "Current Totals (Ground Truth)" FH/FC fields added per leg. Checks Next Date now auto-populates from Last Date via the existing `CheckDateInput` component (same year-count-from-check-name logic as live fleet). Top nav reordered to Dashboard → Upload → Prospects. `tabIndex={-1}` added to the 👁/🚫 and operator-label toggle buttons so Tab moves cleanly field-to-field. Inline Engine LLP / APU LLP extraction added — reuses the exact same `/api/extract` prompts and Sonnet/Haiku model choice as `UploadView`'s "llp"/"apu_llp` flow (prompts pulled into shared `ENGINE_LLP_PROMPT`/`APU_LLP_PROMPT` constants so the two flows can never drift), just without the fleet-matching step since the target asset is already known. Share button removed from the closing "That's the full template" card (still available in the header and on Prospects-list cards, per Alan: "only share once done and saved").
- **Built (feedback round 2 — standalone engine prospects):** New/Prospect creation flow now opens with an explicit **✈ Whole Aircraft** / **⚙ Standalone Engine** choice rather than a second field set under MSN (deliberate: avoids ambiguity over a half-filled MSN + half-filled ESN). Engine choice creates a `prospectKind:"engine"` asset — single engine slot (not two), keyed by ESN instead of MSN (`makeBlankEngineProspect()`). `ProspectEditor` hides all aircraft-only sections (Overview/Weights/Specs/Landing Gear/APU/Checks) for engine-kind prospects, showing only the Engine card, its LLP extractor, and Photos. A `Photos` card was added for both prospect kinds, wired directly to the live fleet's `PhotoManager` (Airframe/LOPA/Avionics/Cabin/etc. categories, LOPA crop tool included) via a `photoSaveAsset` wrapper that keeps the local form state and live preview in sync with PhotoManager's own internal `saveAsset` calls.
- **Bug found and fixed (root cause, not a patch):** the Prospects-list "📋 Tech Spec" button and the Share/QR path were both rendering standalone-engine prospects as full (and largely blank) aircraft specs, because `_engineOnly`/`_enginePos` had only ever been derived inside `ProspectEditor`, never persisted, and never re-derived by any other caller. Fixed by moving the derivation into `generateTechSpec()` itself (checks `asset.prospectKind==="engine"` and applies the flags before calling `buildTechSpecHTML`), so every call site gets it for free instead of each one needing to remember to special-case it. Also hardened `makeBlankEngineProspect()` to blank out the stray `model`/`manufacturer`/`registration`/`operator` defaults inherited from the aircraft blank-template, as defense in depth. Same root cause existed in the public share path (`api/share/[token].js` + `share.html`) — `prospectKind` wasn't in `ALLOWED_FIELDS` so it was being silently stripped before `share.html` ever saw it; added it to the allowlist and added the identical `_engineOnly` derivation in `share.html` before its `buildTechSpecHTML` call. `ShareModal`'s header also fixed (was showing "Share MSN" with a blank MSN for engine prospects; now shows the ESN). Confirmed working end-to-end by Alan post-fix.
- **Scope calls made across the session:** MSN required upfront for aircraft prospects (per Alan). LLP/shop-visit editing for whole-aircraft prospects only available via the inline extractor, not manual row entry. Delete gated to Admin/Editor (Viewer can create/edit but not delete). No "DRAFT" watermark on prospect tech specs — output is byte-for-byte identical to a live spec.
- **Not built (future idea, not requested this session):** a "Promote to Live Fleet" action flipping `type` from `"prospect"` to `"aircraft"` once a deal closes — currently needs a manual Firestore edit.

### 4.19 Brain 2 Must Expose Full Per-Part LLP Remaining-Life Vector — ✅ DONE
- **Problem / need:** Brain 3's EN-LP (Engine LLP Replacement) pot is a **stack simulation**, not a static per-cycle projection. The lowest limiter across the LLP stack triggers the shop visit, and the workscope then harvests every part within ~2,000 FC (`harvestThresholdFC`) of its own limit — so Brain 3 needs the remaining life of **every** LLP in the stack, not just the single lowest figure. Brain 2 (`llpCalculator.js`) already computes per-part life internally (`calcLLPRem`) and `lowestLimiter`, but the roadmap's old "Remaining LLP life (from Brain 2)" input was scoped as a scalar.
- **Fix applied:** Brain 2 hands Brain 3 the full per-part `llpVector()` output for each engine. `projectEnLpPot()` (`flyForward.js`) consumes it directly — after each simulated SV, harvested parts reset to full life and the stack re-projects. Tested end-to-end against real live-asset LLP data via the Fly-Forward Demo (see 4.23) — including a real correctness bug this surfaced: the true lowest limiter must be found from the FULL raw LLP list (not `llpVector()`'s approvedLife-filtered output), since a part missing `approvedLife` can still be the one driving a shop visit, and using the filtered vector alone silently missed real events.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt.

Alan reviewed these against real-world expectations and confirmed they're good enough to keep for now — this is a deliberate parked decision, same category as the other Brain-3-scoping parked decisions below, not outstanding debt. Revisit only if a real EPR quote/schedule becomes available and it's worth tightening.
- **Priority:** 📌 Parked — no longer tracked as blocking or open.

### 4.21 Stub-Buffer vs Harvest-Threshold Validation Check — ✅ FIXED & WIRED IN
- **Context:** EN-LP funds scrapped stub life via a ~10% uplift in the accrual rate (`stubBufferPct`), calibrated to the ~2,000 FC harvest threshold being ≈10% of a ~20,000 FC approved life. The two are only consistent while they stay in that ratio.
- **Correction during scoping:** the lowest limiter itself is **not** stub waste — it runs down to near-zero and gets replaced on its own merits regardless of the buffer. The buffer only needs to cover the *other* LLPs harvested early, while they still have real life left on the table. The part most likely to expose an under-funded buffer is whichever non-limiting LLP has the **shortest** `approvedLife`, since a fixed FC threshold eats a bigger fraction of a short-life part's value than a long-life part's.
- **Fix applied:** `validateStubBuffer(eng, reserveRate, tolerancePts=2)` added to `llpCalculator.js` alongside `llpVector`. It excludes the lowest limiter from `llpVector()`'s output, finds the shortest-`approvedLife` part among what remains, computes `impliedMinBufferPct = (harvestThresholdFC / shortestLifePart.approvedLife) * 100`, and returns a Brain-1-style `⚠`-prefixed warning string if `stubBufferPct` sits more than 2 percentage points below that implied minimum (tolerance absorbs rounding in catalogue config numbers). Returns `null` if there's no second LLP to assess or the buffer is adequate.
- **Now wired into Brain 3:** `projectEnLpPot()` (`flyForward.js`) calls `validateStubBuffer()` at each simulated shop visit and folds the result into the pot's warnings array — confirmed firing correctly in the Fly-Forward Demo (4.23) against both fabricated and real LLP stacks.
- **Verification item resolved:** `stubBufferPct` is used and stored as a plain percentage number (e.g. `10`, not `0.10`) throughout — confirmed consistent across `llpCalculator.js`, `flyForward.js`, and the demo lease terms.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt.

---

- **Context:** Lease redelivery conditions / end-of-life compensation are out of scope for Brain 3 v1, but Alan confirmed they're coming and the schema should tolerate their eventual arrival. They also intersect the horizon rule: events past lease end flag "partial-funded, settles at redelivery" rather than as a shortfall.
- **Fix (now):** a nullable `redeliveryConditions` collection stub was added to the Layer 2 schema (roadmap Section 5) so Brain 3 is written to tolerate its absence from day one. No behaviour built.
- **Priority:** 🟢 LOW — stub only; real EOL modelling is Layer-3-ish, well after Brain 3 proves out.

### 4.23 Fly-Forward Demo — Internal Validation View — ✅ BUILT & DEPLOYED
- **Purpose:** Prove the Brain 3/4/5 formula against real data ahead of the actual Layer 2 build, without waiting on the IT/Security Gate — since it uses fabricated lease terms only (no real financial data enters Firestore), it sits outside that gate's boundary entirely. Reached via a "🚀 Fly Forward" button on a real asset (`AssetView`), same entry pattern as "Generate Tech Spec" — always tied to one specific real asset, not a standalone nav item.
- **Files added:** `calculations/flyForward.js` (Brain 3), `riskPeak.js` (Brain 4), `shortfall.js` (Brain 5) — written as the real Brain 3/4/5, not throwaway demo code. `calculations/realAssetContext.js` (derives a genuine monthly FH/FC rate from an asset's own report history; DD/MM/YYYY date parsing). `calculations/demoLeaseData.js` (fabricated lease terms ONLY — accrual rates, escalation, triggers, outflow costs; EN-PR/EN-LP are per-engine templates cloned per real engine found on the asset, not fixed single pots). `calculations/llpCatalogue.js` (real per-part 2026 catalogue prices for CFM/V2500, sourced from `Engine_LLP_Escalation_Model.xlsx` — see below).
- **What's real vs fabricated:** utilisation rate (from real report history), engine/APU LLP stack (real Firestore data), AF-6Y/AF-12Y/LG-OH next-due dates (real, from `asset.checks`/`asset.landingGear`), engine family CFM/V2500 detection (`isCFM()`), and — as of the catalogue upload — per-part LLP replacement cost where a P/N match exists. Fabricated: accrual rates, escalation regimes, trigger intervals, outflow costs, opening balances (all editable in-view via a "Lease Assumptions" panel).
- **Real bugs found and fixed during the build (all in `flyForward.js` unless noted):**
  1. **EN-LP cycle double-counting** — after a harvest reset, engine FC was computed from the absolute month index instead of months-since-reset, causing runaway phantom shop visits (89 in one test run).
  2. **`catalogPrice` fallback silently disabled** — a `0` default (instead of `undefined`) meant `catalogPrice != null` was always true, so the proportional-cost fallback never fired for real (costless) LLP records.
  3. **Calendar pots anchored to "today" instead of real next-due dates** — AF-6Y/AF-12Y/LG-OH originally counted their fabricated interval fresh from the lease-start date instead of trusting `asset.checks[].nextDate` / `asset.landingGear` legs' `nextDue`, which are already real and tracked. Fixed via a `firstEventOverrideDate` mechanism in the event generators.
  4. **EN-LP silently reporting "0 events" when `approvedLife` is blank** — `llpVector()` (Brain 2) correctly drops parts missing `approvedLife`, but `projectEnLpPot` was using that same filtered vector to find the lowest limiter, so an engine with no `approvedLife` populated anywhere silently skipped the harvest check for the whole horizon, looking risk-free instead of "couldn't check." Fixed by finding the true limiter from the FULL raw LLP list (independent of `approvedLife`), and flagging (`costIncomplete`) rather than hiding when the limiting part's cost can't be estimated.
  5. **Flat $/FC cost estimate could exceed the real full-stack cost** — an early illustrative constant ($22/approved-FC) could sum past a known real full-stack replacement figure when several parts bundled into one harvest, which is nonsensical for a partial replacement. Replaced with a proportional-share model (each part's estimated cost = its share of `fullStackReplacementCost`, sized by `approvedLife`), later superseded by real per-part catalogue pricing (below) wherever a P/N match exists.
  6. **Lease-start-date / accrual-catch-up feature — tried, then reverted.** Explored letting the person set a historical lease-start date so pot balances could "catch up" to a realistic today instead of starting at fabricated $0. Alan correctly identified the flaw before it was deployed: reserve pots carry over across lease transitions, so a fictional lease-start date doesn't reconstruct anything real — it just trades one guess for a different one, while also ignoring any real historical withdrawals. Reverted; **the correct lever for "what's really in a pot today" is each pot's existing editable Opening Balance field**, not a synthetic accrual history. (The engine-cycle/APU-condition "now" anchoring logic added to support this — `ctx.nowOffsetMonths` — was left in place as a harmless no-op; it's correct, general-purpose code that simply does nothing while `leaseStart` stays fixed at today.)
- **Real LLP catalogue pricing (`llpCatalogue.js`):** built from `Engine_LLP_Escalation_Model.xlsx` (uploaded July 2026) — real 2026 per-part prices for 18 CFM parts and 25 V2500 parts, each family's real escalation rate (CFM 6.0674%, V2500 10.5257%) and real blended $/FC accrual rate (CFM $295.80, V2500 $345.48). Cost model is now three-tiered: (1) real catalogue price by exact P/N match, tightest band ±3% (2) directly-supplied price, for fabricated demo data (3) proportional-share-of-full-stack fallback for any part not in the table, wider band ±8%. Full-stack totals are now real too (V2500 $6,974,550, CFM $6,752,200), used only as the tier-3 fallback anchor.
- **Engine differentiation:** EN-PR/EN-LP generate one pot per real engine found on the asset (`EN-PR-1`/`EN-LP-1`, `EN-PR-2`/`EN-LP-2`, etc.), each using that specific engine's real LLP data and the correct family's real rates — not a single shared engine assumption.
- **Known coverage gap:** the catalogue doesn't cover every possible real P/N — parts outside its 18/25-part lists fall back to the tier-3 estimate, which is bounded and sane but not a real price.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — deployed by Alan. Directly de-risks the actual Layer 2 build: the calculation engine (Brain 3/4/5) is now tested against real asset data, not just a synthetic test case.
