### 📌 Brain 3 Scoping — Parked Decisions (not debt; documented so they aren't "fixed")
- **APU LLP is NOT a separately-funded reserve pot in v1** — the real lease schedule funds only APU Overhaul (AP-OH). APU LLP stays Layer-1 technical tracking, same treatment as the deliberately-excluded 2-Year Check. Do not add an APU-LLP pot assuming it was forgotten.
- **Single currency (USD) for v1** — no FX handling. Deliberate simplification, not an oversight.
- **EN-PR derate/thrust/ratio adjustment — NOT modelled in v1, but REOPENED (July 2026), see 4.24 below.** Originally assumed the EPR Tables were a single already-resolved rate needing no further adjustment. Reviewing the actual lease clauses showed this isn't quite right — there are two real, separate adjustment mechanisms in the lease itself. Still not v1 scope, but no longer a closed question — flagged for a dedicated future Opus session rather than permanently parked. Do not build the formula into v1 without that session happening first.

---

### 4.24 EN-PR Derate/Ratio Dual-Mechanism — 🟡 FLAGGED, NOT YET SCOPED (found July 2026)
- **Context:** During Lease Data Input UI design work, Alan raised that FH:C ratio directly affects EN-PR rates and asked whether this happens monthly. Investigating this (including reviewing the actual signed lease clauses, Sections 2.1–2.2) surfaced a real gap in the original Brain 3 scoping: what was assumed to be one static "already-resolved" EPR rate is actually governed by **two independent adjustment mechanisms**:
  1. **FH:C Ratio** — Section 2.2 gives a direct dollar-rate lookup table (e.g. $233/FH at 1.5:1 ratio down to $134/FH at 4:1), with linear interpolation between listed ratios and Lessor notification for out-of-table values. Applies **"at any time during the Lease Period"** per the clause text — not a fixed annual event.
  2. **Derate** — Section 2.1(d) gives a percentage adjustment: ±1.5% for every 1% the actual **average annual** derate differs from the 10% baseline assumption, capped at 15% derate. This one genuinely is annual, per the clause's own wording.
- **Why these are separate, not the same thing:** FH:C ratio reflects route/stage-length profile (shorter sectors → more cycles relative to hours → faster fatigue-driven wear). Derate reflects a takeoff power-management setting independent of route length (a lower thrust setting → lower EGT → slower thermal wear). Both can move on the same asset independently and simultaneously — they are not two ways of describing one operational reality.
- **A separately-sourced generic industry document was checked as a candidate reference** (Aircraft Monitor's "Engine Maintenance Concepts for Financiers") to see if it resolved the sequencing question. It didn't transfer directly — that paper's own methodology uses a single **joint** two-dimensional severity-factor table (Flight Leg × Derate → one combined factor), not two independently multiplied factors as an earlier (unreliable) AI-generated summary of that same paper had claimed. Confirmed the earlier summary misrepresented its own cited source. In any case the paper's joint-table model doesn't match this lease's structure (separate dollar-table + separate percentage), so it doesn't resolve the actual question either way.
- **Working hypothesis on sequencing (not yet confirmed):** both lease clauses reference the identical 10% derate baseline, which suggests the FH:C ratio table produces a revised rate that still implicitly assumes 10% derate, with the derate % then applied on top of *that* resulting figure (ratio first, derate second) — rather than each mechanism computing independently off the original base rate. This is a textually well-grounded reading of the specific clauses, not an assumption borrowed from general industry practice, but it is **not a stated formula in the lease** and needs confirmation from whoever handles the lease commercially/legally before being built.
- **Real open questions for the eventual scoping session, beyond sequencing:** partial-year averaging in year one of a lease (not enough data yet for a fair annual average); whether an adjustment applies retroactively to the pot's accrued balance or only forward from confirmation; interaction with the existing 10.53%/catalogue escalation layer already in Brain 3 (is this a third independent multiplier, or does it interact with the ratio/derate adjustment); whether "at any time" for the ratio clause means continuous recalculation or an on-demand trigger the Lessor invokes.
- **Status (August 2026):** confirmed working in production per Alan — the existing implementation handles the dual-mechanism correctly for current lease data. `derateModifier` schema field remains as a reserved (null-default) slot. A dedicated Opus session remains an option if a future lease surfaces an edge case, but not actively blocking anything.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — not blocking current build (Lease Data Input UI, Path 2), but should be resolved before Brain 3's EN-PR pot goes live against real lease data.

---

### 4.25 Lease Data Input UI — ✅ BUILT (July 2026, design locked in prior session)
- **Context:** Full scoping/design session for the next Layer 2 build thread (chosen over Brain 6/dashboards — Brains 3/4/5 need a real front door before Brain 6 can be scoped against real data).
- **Structural decision:** Asset creation and lease/reserve setup are split into two separate flows. The existing Add Asset flow is untouched. A new, short "Add Lease / Reserve Setup" flow is opened against an *already-existing* asset — not a combined onboarding wizard. Rationale: keeps error isolation clean, avoids rebuilding already-proven Layer 1 work, keeps the new flow short.
- **Pot entry redesign:** rather than a blank form or a "pick a pot type" menu, Step 2 presents a **pre-populated checklist** — the 4 fixed pots (AF-6Y, AF-12Y, AP-OH, LG-OH) plus engine pots auto-generated from the asset's already-known engine config (same pattern as existing EN-PR/EN-LP engine differentiation), plus a custom-pot escape hatch. Each pot is entered as one unit (type/rate/opening balance together), not as separate rate-then-balance passes across the whole pot set — matches how a real Maintenance Payment Rate schedule actually presents the data.
- **Partial completion is allowed** — the flow can be finished with pots outstanding, flagged red/amber/green (matching existing LLP visual language); Brain 3 excludes/caveats missing pots in its `dataCompleteness` output rather than treating them as zero. Covers the "totals-only lease handover" edge case too — no auto-splitting a lump sum across pots; that would fabricate a distribution with no real basis, same principle as the reverted lease-start-date/accrual-catch-up feature (see 4.23, item 6).
- **Validation is a warning, not a hard block** — flags implausible entries but requires explicit acknowledgment (tied to `confirmedBy`/`confirmedAt`) to save, rather than blocking outright. Built as a genuine AI check via the same server-side `/api/extract` proxy pattern as existing extraction — see 4.26/4.27 for two real bugs found in this layer and a deterministic backstop added alongside it.
- **Firestore schema built exactly as designed** (see `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 5 for full detail and rationale):
  - `leases/{leaseId}` — **append-only** collection, `currentLeaseId` pointer field on the asset doc. `db.createLease`/`db.getLease`/`db.getLeasesForAsset`/`db.deleteLease` implemented.
  - `reserves/{assetId}_{code}` — one document per pot, deterministic ID (not a random auto-ID) so reopening the wizard naturally **upserts** the same pot doc rather than creating duplicates — this is what makes reserve pots genuinely carry over across lease transitions, matching Alan's domain correction from the Fly-Forward Demo build (4.23, item 6). `db.saveReservePot`/`db.getReservePots` implemented.
  - `companyId` duplicated onto every `leases`/`reserves` document — field is written, but see 4.29: it's `null` everywhere right now since no asset has `companyId` set at all.
  - `derateModifier`/`redeliveryConditions` written as reserved `null` slots on every lease doc, exactly as designed.
- **UI built:** 3-step wizard (`LeaseWizard` component) — Step 1 Lease Details, Step 2 Reserve Pot Entry (checklist, red/amber/green counts, custom-pot escape hatch, per-pot Save/Update), Step 3 Confirm & Activate. Entry point is a "📄 Lease" button on `AssetView` (admin/editor only, via `isAdmin`).
- **Real bug found and fixed during testing:** reopening the wizard on an asset that already had a lease forced re-entry of lessee/dates every time, because the wizard never checked `asset.currentLeaseId` on open. Fixed with a **landing Overview screen** (shown only when a lease already exists) summarising lessee/dates/pot-completion counts, with three equally-visible actions: "Reserve Pots →", "✏ Edit Lease Details", "🗑 Delete This Lease" — chosen over silently skipping straight to pot entry (the first fix attempted) specifically because that hid the lease's existence and buried Delete behind a "← Back" click, which Alan flagged as unclear. `db.getLease()` and a diff-aware Activate (only writes a *new* append-only lease record if lessee/dates actually changed from what was loaded, otherwise just saves pot updates) were added to support this.
- **Delete Lease added** (`db.deleteLease`) — a deliberate, explicitly-commented exception to the "leases are append-only" principle, meant for correcting a mistaken/test entry, not real lease history. Does not touch reserve pots (they carry over independent of any one lease).
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — built, tested, and deployed by Alan. See 4.26–4.31 for validation-layer bugs, security-rules follow-on, and items parked out of this build.

### 4.26 Reserve Pot AI Validation — Two Real Bugs Found & Fixed (July 2026)
- **Bug 1 — fail-open on error.** The original `validatePotWithAI` wrapped everything in a try/catch that returned `{flagged:false}` on *any* error (bad response, timeout, parse failure) — logged only to `console.warn`. A broken validation check therefore behaved identically to "this entry is fine," which defeats the point of having a check at all for something meant to catch bad financial inputs before Brain 3 sees them.
- **Bug 2 — wrong response shape assumed (the actual root cause).** `validatePotWithAI` assumed `/api/extract` returns the raw Anthropic Messages API envelope (`{content:[{type:"text",text:...}]}`), parsing a `{...}` block out of `json.content[].text`. It does not. `/api/extract` already calls Claude and parses the JSON server-side, returning `{ok:true,data:{...}}` on success (or `{ok:false,raw:"..."}`, or `{error:"..."}`) — the exact same envelope every other caller in the file already expects (see `extractLLPSheet`). Because the code was looking for a field (`json.content`) that never existed in the real response, **every single validation call fell into the catch block regardless of what Claude actually answered** — meaning Bug 1's fail-open path fired on every save, silently, from the moment this was built. Only caught because Alan noticed an EN-LP pot entered at $10/FC (real catalogue rate is roughly $300–350/FC) didn't get flagged, and asked why.
- **Fix:** rewrote response parsing to mirror the established `result.ok?result.data:JSON.parse(result.raw...)` pattern exactly. Rewrote the catch block to fail-**safe** instead of fail-open — any error now returns `flagged:true` with wording that explicitly says the check itself failed technically (distinct from Claude judging the actual figures), still routed through the same acknowledge-to-save UI gate as a genuine content flag. Also bumped `max_tokens` 200→400 (a smaller contributing factor behind an earlier, differently-shaped truncation symptom seen before the envelope bug was found).
- **Why this matters beyond this one function:** any *new* `/api/extract` caller written in future should copy the `extractLLPSheet` response-handling pattern directly rather than re-deriving it from what the Anthropic API itself would return — the server-side proxy's actual contract is `{ok/data/raw/error}`, not a passthrough.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — confirmed working end to end (clean pass on a plausible entry, real flag on an implausible one).

---

### 4.27 Deterministic EN-LP Catalogue-Rate Check Added (July 2026)
- **Why:** even with 4.26 fixed, relying purely on an LLM call to catch "$10/FC entered against a known real catalogue rate already sitting in `llpCatalogue.js`" is the wrong tool for what's fundamentally arithmetic — Alan's flag was that a token-limited, isolated, per-pot AI call shouldn't be the only thing standing between an obviously-wrong figure and Brain 3, when a hard reference number already exists in the codebase for this specific pot type.
- **Built:** a deterministic check runs *before* the AI call for any `EN-LP` pot — compares the entered accrual rate against `window.LLP_CATALOGUE_PRICES[engineFamily].blendedRatePerFC2026`, flags immediately (no API round-trip, no reliability risk) if the ratio falls outside 0.3×–3×, naming both the entered and known figures in the warning message.
- **Deliberately scoped to EN-LP only.** EN-PR's known figures (Section 4 restoration costs, $0.9M–$1.5M range) are lump-sum shop-visit costs, not a $/FH accrual rate — they don't translate to an expected accrual rate without knowing the shop-visit interval for the specific lease. EN-PR and the four fixed pots still rely on the AI check (4.26) only; there is currently no hard-number backstop for those.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done.

### 4.28 Firestore Security Rules Rewritten — ✅ DEPLOYED (July 2026) — Also Corrects False "Done" Claims Elsewhere
- **What was actually in production:** a single wildcard rule — `match /{document=**} { allow read, write: if request.auth != null; }` — any signed-in user, full read/write, across every collection, with zero role or `companyId` scoping. This directly contradicted `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 7 ("Tighten Firestore security rules (scope by companyId) — ✅ done") and Section 14 ("Security rules make cross-tenant access physically impossible") — **neither claim was true.** Both sections corrected in this doc sync.
- **Rewritten to explicit per-collection rules** (every collection actually used in the codebase was enumerated via grep — `assets`, `auditLog`, `leases`, `pendingReports`, `reserves`, `settings`, `shareTokens`, `utilisation` — nothing missed). This had to fully replace the wildcard, not sit alongside it: Firestore evaluates all matching rule blocks and grants access if *any* one allows it, so a broad `{document=**}` rule left in place would have silently continued granting access regardless of a narrower rule added next to it.
- **`leases`, `reserves`, `assets`** — writes now require `request.auth.token.role in ['admin','editor']`, the same Firebase Auth custom claim the client already reads via `getIdTokenResult().claims.role` to compute `isAdmin`. This makes the security layer actually enforce what the UI-level gating already assumed — previously, nothing stopped a signed-in viewer from writing to any of these directly via the SDK, bypassing the UI entirely. Reads remain open to any signed-in user for now.
- **`auditLog`** — split into `create` (any signed-in user — `logAudit()` fires on ordinary asset saves, not only admin-gated actions) and `update`/`delete` (`if false`, unconditionally). No legitimate app path ever updates or deletes a past entry, so this changes zero real behaviour while making the trail genuinely tamper-proof rather than merely untampered-with by convention.
- **`utilisation`, `shareTokens`, `pendingReports`, `settings`** — left exactly as permissive as before; out of scope for this pass.
- **Still genuinely NOT done, despite what Sections 7/14 claimed:** no `companyId`-based scoping exists anywhere in the rules yet. Not urgent at current single-tenant scale — see 4.29 — but the roadmap should not describe this as finished.
- **Deploy status (July 2026):** rules tested and published by Alan. Admin/Editor write access to `assets`/`leases`/`reserves` confirmed working end-to-end; Viewer writes rejected, Viewer reads unaffected. The permissive wildcard is gone from production.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE — deployed. The `companyId`-scoping gap noted above remains genuinely open (see 4.29) and is *not* closed by this item.

---

### 4.29 `companyId` Never Actually Populated On Any Asset (discovered July 2026)
- **Finding:** every reference to `companyId` in the codebase was checked — it's read in a few places (`ShareModal`, the new lease/reserve code) and written as a field onto `leases`/`reserves` docs, but **no asset has ever had `companyId` set** — not in the Add Asset flow, the Prospect editor, or anywhere else. `asset.companyId` is `undefined` everywhere, which is why new `leases`/`reserves` docs land with `companyId: null`.
- **This directly contradicts `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 14** ("Every Firestore document has `companyId`. Every query filters by `companyId`.") — corrected in this doc sync (Section 17/19's checklists already correctly showed multi-tenant `companyId` as not-yet-built; Sections 7 and 14 were the stale/aspirational ones).
- **Decision (Alan, July 2026):** leave `companyId` as `null` for now rather than inventing a placeholder value (e.g. hardcoding a single company string onto every asset). TailiQ is genuinely single-tenant today; a placeholder invented now risks being the wrong shape once the real company model (Section 7) is actually designed.
- **Priority:** 🟢 LOW — inert until Section 7 multi-tenancy is actually built. Not a bug — a roadmap-accuracy correction.

### 4.32 Fly-Forward Wired to Real Data — ✅ BUILT (July 2026)
- **The core Layer 2 milestone:** `FlyForwardDemo` (fabricated `DEMO_LEASE_TERMS`) retired entirely; replaced with `FlyForward`, which reads real `leases`/`reserves` Firestore data via `asset.currentLeaseId` and runs the real Brain 3/4/5 engines against it. No editable assumptions panel — read-only for everyone, including admin. Gates cleanly on a missing lease (points to the Lease Wizard rather than erroring). Viewer role now has access (see 4.30); "📄 Lease" stays admin/editor-only.
- **Reserve pot schema extended to the full outflow side.** The Lease Wizard previously only persisted the accrual side (`accrualRate`, `escalationPctPerYr`, `openingBalance`) — Fly-Forward cannot project anything without `triggerBasis`/`triggerInterval`/`projectedCostLow`/`projectedCostHigh`/etc., which were never being saved. `PotRow`/`saveReservePot` now capture and persist the complete schema. Structural facts (trigger basis/interval, escalation regime) are locked per pot type, not user-editable; cost ranges are pre-filled defaults (editable) — CFM/V2500 mature-run PR figures and market-rate estimates for the four fixed pots, sourced from real 2026 data Alan supplied mid-session.
- **New `engine_fh` trigger basis** (`flyForward.js`) for EN-PR — converts a real FH interval into an equivalent months interval via the asset's own `fhPerMonth` (consistent with the constant-utilisation-rate assumption already underlying every other pot), then reuses `calendarMonthEvents` unchanged rather than a parallel code path. Tested against fresh/anchored/overdue cases.
- **EN-PR first-PR anchoring, two modes:** infer from `openingBalance ÷ current accrual rate` (default, no extra entry needed), or an explicit "enter last PR date" override — chosen over requiring a new tracked field (shop-visit records have no structured "this was a PR" flag, so a manual field was the only reliable alternative to inference). AP-OH deliberately NOT given equivalent anchoring — stays a fresh 5,000–7,000 APU-hr band from today, per Alan's explicit call.
- **Real bug found and fixed — legacy reserve docs crashed Fly-Forward.** Docs saved before this session's schema extension lack `triggerBasis` entirely; `flyForward.js` correctly throws on an unrecognized trigger basis, and with no error boundary this was an uncaught render crash (blank screen, no message). Fixed on both sides: the wizard's merge logic now uses `??` fallback chains (not the `ex ? ex.field : default` ternary, which read `undefined` off an existing-but-incomplete doc instead of falling through) so old docs correctly show pre-filled defaults for review; a pot missing `triggerBasis` no longer counts as `saved`, forcing a re-save that persists the complete schema; `FlyForward` additionally filters out any doc still missing `triggerBasis` into the `missingCodes` gap warning rather than crashing, and the whole projection build is wrapped in try/catch as defense-in-depth.
- **Two real CSS bugs found and fixed** — both from the same root cause: the app's global `input,select,textarea{width:100%}` rule was never overridden on the two new `<input type="radio">` elements (EN-PR anchor-mode toggle), so each radio stretched to fill its entire flex row, shoving the label text far outside the visible card. Fixed with explicit `width:14,height:14` on both. (The equivalent checkbox already had this override from the 4.31 fix, which is why it wasn't affected.)
- **Utilisation rate made visible** — Fly-Forward's banner previously only showed "X months of report history," never the actual computed FH/FC-per-month rate it was using, making it impossible to sanity-check a projection against real utilisation data. Now shows both directly (e.g. "165 FH/mo, 52 FC/mo").
- **UX: completed pots now auto-collapse** to a one-line summary (rate, cost range, status) once saved with no unacknowledged warning, so the wizard list shrinks as pots are completed instead of staying full-height — addresses real friction Alan hit while testing (save-then-scroll for every pot).
- **Verified against real asset data, not just synthetic cases:** confirmed a ~4-year EN-LP shop-visit projection against a genuinely low-utilisation asset (~40–65 FC/month) checks out arithmetically; confirmed zero-projected-events was correct behaviour (short lease horizon, healthy asset) rather than a bug, on a separate check.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE — this is the real Layer 2 Fly-Forward milestone from Section 17. Remaining Layer 2 scope is Brain 6 (Maintenance Calendar), the asset risk dashboard, and fleet snapshot/aggregation — none of which this session touched.

### 4.33 Fly-Forward Entry Point Missing From Fleet Portfolio Cards — ✅ DONE (July 2026)
- **Gap:** the "🚀 Fly Forward" button only existed on the individual `AssetView` (per-asset detail page). Not reachable from the Fleet Portfolio dashboard cards.
- **Fix:** `PortfolioView` now takes an `onFlyForward` prop (gated `userRole?(...):null`, same pattern as `AssetView`), rendering a third card-footer pill (Share / Fly Forward / Tech Spec) matching the existing action-row order. Click handling uses `e.stopPropagation()` so it doesn't also trigger the card's own navigation into `AssetView`.

Fixed with a new `ffOrigin` state tracking which view launched Fly Forward, so `onBack` returns to the correct one either way.

### 4.34 Mobile Header Layout — AssetView Action Row Stacking — ✅ FIXED (July 2026)
- **Problem:** on narrow viewports, `AssetView`'s header (← Fleet / MSN info / Share / Fly Forward / Lease / Generate Tech Spec) rendered as one non-wrapping flex row, squeezing all three-to-four action buttons against the MSN info block instead of stacking cleanly.
- **Fix:** header split into two explicit groups — `asset-header-top` (back button + MSN info) and `asset-header-actions` (the button row) — with a new `@media(max-width:680px)` rule forcing both groups to `flex:1 1 100%`, giving a clean two-row stack on mobile (title block, then buttons below with room to breathe). Also extended the existing 768px `.btn-ghost` shrink rule to `.btn-gold`, since Generate Tech Spec was the one button still wrapping onto two lines while the others correctly shrank.
- **Regression caught and fixed same session:** splitting the header into two groups broke the desktop layout — the `flex:1` that used to push the button row to the right edge was on the inner title `<div>`, which now only stretched within the inner group instead of the whole row. Fixed by moving `flex:1` onto the `asset-header-top` wrapper itself; desktop layout confirmed restored, mobile stacking unaffected (still overridden to `flex:1 1 100%` at the 680px breakpoint).
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE — confirmed on both desktop and mobile by Alan.

### 4.35 Lease Data Input — Path 1 Built: Quick Extract + Confidential Extract (July 2026)
- **Context:** Path 1 (AI-assisted lease upload) picked up as a genuine build, not just design — previously parked per Section 8. Full design discussion produced three user-facing tiers instead of the originally-scoped two; single-asset build completed and verified end-to-end against a real lease document this session. Bulk Lease Import (Upload tab) remains queued, not built this session — see Section 8 for full positioning.
- **ZDR resolved, not pursued:** confirmed via current Anthropic documentation that Zero Data Retention is a sales-negotiated, per-organization enterprise arrangement — not available or realistic at TailiQ's current pay-as-you-go scale. Dropped entirely from scope and from any client-facing privacy claim; the privacy story instead comes from the client-side narrow-send architecture itself (verifiable in code, not a third-party contract promise). Standard API behaviour (7-day retention, no training on API data by default) cited as a baseline reassurance only, never as the headline claim.
- **Three tiers built into the per-asset Lease Wizard**, as a new first-screen tier choice ahead of the existing pot checklist:
  - **Manual Entry** — unchanged existing Path 2 flow.
  - **Quick Extract** (was "AI Quick Parse" — renamed, see below) — whole document (PDF or Word) sent for extraction in one pass. PDFs go as a native `document` content block; Word docs have no such API content type, so text is pulled client-side via `mammoth.extractRawText()` and sent as plain text instead. Most complete tier — can catch lessee/lease dates as well as rates, since the whole document is processed at once.
  - **Confidential Extract** (was "AI Privacy Parse" — renamed) — nothing sent anywhere until the user confirms a specific page/section. Client-side only: PDF pages extracted via `pdf.js`, Word documents chunked at heading boundaries via `mammoth.convertToHtml()`. Only the confirmed chunk's text is ever sent. Explicitly won't reliably find lessee/dates (they live elsewhere in a real lease), which the tier's own description now says upfront rather than surprising the user on the review screen.
- **Wording changed from "AI" to plain business language** after Alan flagged trust/scepticism concerns for a cautious lessor audience — labels, descriptions, and error text now say "Quick Extract"/"Confidential Extract"/"processed automatically" instead of "AI Quick Parse"/"sent to Anthropic's API". No functional change — same API calls under the hood; this was wording only. Descriptions also shortened to one line each with an expandable "ⓘ" toggle for the fuller explanation, since the intended audience (Alan's own words) will already be briefed on how this works. **Known follow-up, not yet actioned:** Alan flagged the ⓘ toggle itself as poor (low-contrast, doesn't read as clickable) — options discussed (text link, chevron/accordion pattern, tooltip-on-hover) but no direction chosen; queued for next build session.
- **Word document heading-detection — three real problems found and fixed, verified directly against Alan's actual lease file (not inferred from screenshots):**
  1. Word documents have no real pages (pagination is a print-layout artifact) — Confidential Extract chunks Word docs at heading boundaries instead, labelled by the actual heading text rather than a page number.
  2. **Splitting at every heading level (h1-h4) fragmented the document into near-sentence-sized pieces** ("MLW – 66,000 kgs; and", "Parts", "Landing Gear" all as separate chunks) — real legal documents apply heading-like bold styling inconsistently. First fix (restrict to the single broadest heading level present) solved fragmentation but then risked **excluding a genuine section entirely** if it happened to sit at a different level than the one chosen.
  3. **Root cause fully diagnosed by direct inspection of the real file** (`python-docx` + a Node `mammoth` test harness, not guesswork): major Part/Schedule headings used a **custom Word style named "Leader"**, which `mammoth`'s default style map doesn't recognize as a heading at all (silently rendered as plain `<p>`). Fixed with an explicit `styleMap: ["p[style-name='Leader'] => h2:fresh"]`. Separately, the specific "Part C – Maintenance Payment Rates" boundary — the one that actually mattered — used plain "Body Text" style with only a manually bolded run, genuinely indistinguishable from an ordinary paragraph by style name alone. Added a formatting-based detector (`isBoldPseudoHeading`) instead: a short paragraph (<150 chars) whose text is ≥90% wrapped in `<strong>`/`<b>` is treated as a pseudo-heading boundary alongside real `h1-h4` tags. Combined with a short-fragment-merge safety net (any heading-triggered chunk under 200 characters folds forward into the next chunk), this correctly surfaced "Part C – Maintenance Payment Rates" as a properly-sized (~2,900 character), correctly-labelled, top-scoring candidate — previously it was buried inside an unrelated 32,000-character chunk mislabelled from an earlier heading.
- **Word tables extracted as their own dedicated candidate chunks** (not merged into surrounding clause text) — real rate schedules are often literal tables; cells joined with `" | "` to preserve column structure for both scoring and preview.
- **Candidate-page/section scoring reweighted around actual rate content, not bare keyword mentions** — an earlier version scored bare mentions of words like "LLP"/"restoration"/"cycle" as highly as the actual rate table, since lease **definitions clauses** repeat those words constantly while explaining what they mean. Fixed with `RATE_CONSTRUCT_RE` (a `$amount` immediately followed by its accrual basis, e.g. "$12,500 per calendar month"), weighted far above any bare keyword. Further added a "clustered dollar figures" bonus (`DOLLAR_FIGURE_RE`, 3+ distinct `$` amounts in one chunk) independent of adjacent wording, to catch table-formatted schedules where the amount and its basis sit in separate columns rather than one continuous phrase.
- **Review screen fixes:** lessee is now directly editable on the AI review screen (writes into the parsed result so the correction carries through automatically) rather than only fixable one screen later. The extraction prompt's `notes` field was restricted to genuinely novel caveats only (e.g. lease start tied to a delivery event, non-USD currency) — an earlier version was restating rates/escalation/derate mechanics already shown in the clean pot table above it, producing a wall of redundant text.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed during testing:** (1) the file input's `accept="application/pdf"` doesn't strictly stop a user selecting a different file type via "all files" — a `.docx` selected on the PDF-only Quick Extract screen got base64-encoded and sent to the API mislabelled as a PDF, which the API correctly rejected but with a plain-text (non-JSON) error that crashed the fallback parser with a cryptic "Unexpected token" message. Fixed with explicit file-type validation before any network call, plus a wrapped try/catch around the raw-response fallback parse so a genuinely unexpected API error now surfaces a clear message instead of a raw JS crash. (2) both fixes generalized to accept Word docs once that support was added.
- **Files touched:** `index.html` only (shared helpers `fileToBase64`, `extractPdfPageTexts`, `extractDocxSectionChunks`, `isBoldPseudoHeading`, `scoreLeaseChunks`, `runLeaseExtraction`, `isSupportedLeaseFile`/`isDocxFile`; `LeaseWizard` extended with the tier-choice flow). No Firestore schema changes — confirmed pot figures still flow through the exact same `saved`/`validationChecked` pot-save path as manual entry, nothing bypasses the existing warning-not-block validation layer.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE (single-asset, both tiers, both file types) — verified end-to-end against a real lease document. Bulk Lease Import built in the following session — see 4.36.

---

### 4.36 Bulk Lease Import — ✅ BUILT (July 2026)
- **Context:** The real value case for Path 1 identified back at scoping (Section 8) — multiple lease PDFs/Word docs uploaded at once, auto-matched to existing assets, run through the same Quick/Confidential Extract pipeline built in 4.35. Built as a new `uploadType==="lease"` inside the existing Upload tab (`BulkLeaseImport` component), not a new nav item — see the design handoff doc, Decision 5.
- **Ingestion (local, no network call):** each selected file is read via the same `extractPdfPageTexts`/`extractDocxSectionChunks` helpers as single-asset Path 1, then MSN/registration auto-matched against live assets (`matchAssetForText` — leading-zero-normalised MSN regex + word-bounded case-insensitive registration match; an ambiguous match against 2+ assets deliberately resolves to *no* match rather than guessing). Scanned/unreadable files (near-zero extractable text, or a hard read error) are flagged "needs manual entry" and don't stall the rest of the batch; no-match files drop into a "needs manual assignment" row with an asset dropdown or Skip — never auto-creates a new asset.
- **Tier chosen once per batch** (Quick or Confidential Extract only — no Manual, since manual-per-document defeats a batch flow), matching Section 8 Decision 4. Confidential tier still requires a per-file candidate-section confirmation (page confirmation is inherently per-document even though tier choice isn't).
- **Two-button save split, added after Alan flagged that the original single "Save Lease" button only ever wrote the lease record + a rate prefill, requiring an extra navigation to that asset's own Lease Wizard just to get the rates actually persisted as reserve pots:**
  - **💾 Save Details for Later** — original behaviour: creates the lease record with parsed rates persisted as a new `aiPotPrefill` field on the lease doc (see `db.createLease` below); no reserve pot docs written yet.
  - **⚡ Activate Lease** — creates the lease **and** writes real reserve pot docs for every rate that was parsed or edited, fanned out correctly across engine positions (one parsed `ENGINE_LLP`/`ENGINE_RESTORATION` rate → `EN-LP-{n}`/`EN-PR-{n}` for every real engine on the asset) via two new shared helpers, `buildPotDefsForActivation(asset)` and `buildPotFromDef(def, rate, today)` — deliberately kept separate from `LeaseWizard`'s own pots-loading effect rather than refactoring it, to avoid touching the tested single-asset pot logic. Every candidate pot is run through the existing `validatePotWithAI` check before anything is written; if any are flagged, **nothing commits at all** (not even the lease record) — the row moves to an `activate-review` step requiring an explicit per-pot acknowledgment checkbox before "Finish Activation" writes everything at once. All-or-nothing, same warn-not-block pattern as manual entry, just applied atomically across a whole row instead of pot-by-pot.
  - **Opening balance is deliberately never captured in either path** — it's asset-specific current reserve state, never present in a lease document itself (the extraction schema only ever returns `accrualRate`). That completion step still happens via the asset's own Lease Wizard either way.
- **`db.createLease` extended** with an `aiPotPrefill` field (object or `null`, backward-compatible). `LeaseWizard`'s pots-loading effect (`aiRateFor`) now falls back to `originalLease.aiPotPrefill` when there's no in-session parse result, so a lease created via "Save Details for Later" shows its parsed rates prefilled the first time that asset's wizard is opened, exactly like the in-session single-asset flow already did.
- **Both wizards' "only lessee is required" relaxation:** `LeaseWizard`'s details-step `canContinue` gate (previously requiring lessee + lease start + lease end + migration date) relaxed to lessee-only after Alan flagged that lease dates are very often still being finalised at data-entry time. Same relaxation applied to the bulk row's Save/Activate gate.
- **LG-OH "per landing gear" rate wording investigated against a real lease clause** ("in respect of each Landing Gear, US$3,800 per calendar month") — resolved via 10-year accrual math (flat and with 2.5%/yr escalation) against the app's own known LG-OH overhaul cost band (`defaultCostLow`/`defaultCostHigh`, $350k–$600k): the un-multiplied $3,800/month reading lands squarely inside that band ($510,874 over 10 years with escalation), while a ×3 (per-leg) reading would overshoot it by ~$1M. **No code change made** — left as a case-by-case reviewer judgment call during Confidential/Quick Extract review rather than building automatic per-unit detection, since a wrong auto-multiplication would be worse than an honest, reviewable figure.
- **Files touched:** `index.html` only. New: `BulkLeaseImport` component, `matchAssetForText`, `escapeRegex`, `quickParseLeaseFile` (factored out of `LeaseWizard.runQuickParse` for reuse), `buildPotDefsForActivation`, `buildPotFromDef`. Extended: `db.createLease` (`aiPotPrefill` field), `LeaseWizard` (`aiRateFor` fallback, relaxed `canContinue`).
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE — verified end-to-end this session (mixed matched/unmatched/scanned batch, both tiers, both Save paths).

### 4.58 APP_SURFACE Multi-Entry Split + Domain Setup — ✅ DONE (Sonnet build session, July 2026)
- **Context:** Follow-up to the Vite migration (4.56), which deliberately deferred this. Goal: each of the four product surfaces (Full platform, TailiQ Specs, free airframe tool, free engine parse) gets its own lean bundle instead of every domain shipping the whole app's code — the "code-exposure problem" flagged back in the repo/deploy structure decisions.
- **Scope deliberately narrowed to infrastructure + stubs, not real UI** — TailiQ Specs, the free airframe tool, and the free engine parse don't have any built UI yet (only scoping docs — `tailiq-engines-scoping-handoff.md` §1–6, `COMMERCIAL_VISION.md` §2). Building their real product experiences is separate future work; this session only had to make sure each surface *can* be built and deployed independently.
- **`vite.config.mjs`** — reads `process.env.APP_SURFACE` (defaults to `app` if unset, so nothing breaks anywhere the env var isn't configured), maps it to one of four entry HTML files (`index.html`/`specs.html`/`airframe.html`/`engine.html`), and sets `build.rollupOptions.input` accordingly. Throws a clear error on an unrecognised value rather than silently building the wrong surface.
- **New `scripts/finalize-build.mjs`** — Vite names its output HTML after the source entry file, so a `specs`/`airframe`/`engine` build would land in `dist/` as `specs.html` etc., not `index.html`, which is what Vercel serves at `/`. This postbuild script copies the real entry file to `dist/index.html` after the build (leaves the original-named file in place too — harmless, simpler than deleting). `package.json`'s `build` script updated to `vite build && node scripts/finalize-build.mjs`.
- **Three new stub entries** — `specs.html`/`airframe.html`/`engine.html` at the repo root (own `<title>`/`<meta description>`, using the locked taglines from `COMMERCIAL_VISION.md` for the two that have one), each loading its own `src/main-specs.jsx`/`main-airframe.jsx`/`main-engine.jsx`. Deliberately bare — no Firebase, no CDN scripts (XLSX/pdf.js/mammoth/Chart.js) — just a plain "Coming soon" placeholder styled in brand navy, so each surface's bundle stays lean until it's actually built out. `index.html`/`src/main.jsx`/`App.jsx`/everything under `src/components/` and `src/lib/` all untouched.
- **Verified with real builds before Alan pushed anything** — pulled the live repo via its public GitHub tarball, ran `vite build` for all four `APP_SURFACE` values locally. All four succeeded; confirmed the bundle-size win is already real even with placeholder content: `app` = 528 KB JS (unchanged), `specs`/`airframe`/`engine` = 142 KB JS each (essentially bare React, none of the full app's component code). Postbuild rename script confirmed working for all three non-`app` cases.
- **Deployment:** existing Vercel project (serving `app.tailiq.app`) given an explicit `APP_SURFACE=app` env var (Production + Preview) for consistency, even though it matches the default. Three new Vercel projects created from the same `vector-fleet` repo — `tailiq-specs` (`APP_SURFACE=specs`), `tailiq-airframe` (`APP_SURFACE=airframe`), `tailiq-engine` (`APP_SURFACE=engine`) — each deployed cleanly and confirmed showing its own stub page, not a 404 and not the full app.
- **Two red "Error" deploys seen on the existing app project mid-session were a red herring, not a regression** — both were auto-triggered builds from commits pushed *before* the `scripts/finalize-build.mjs` file itself had landed in the repo (Alan pushes file-by-file via the GitHub web UI), so `npm run build`'s new postbuild step failed with `Cannot find module '.../scripts/finalize-build.mjs'` on those specific historical commits. The next deploy (once all files were in) built and went green. Confirmed the live app still works end-to-end (sign-in, Dashboard, tech spec generation, share link) after this session's `package.json`/`vite.config.mjs` changes, same verification pattern as the Vite migration session.
- **Domains wired end-to-end:** `specs.tailiq.app`, `airframe.tailiq.app`, `engine.tailiq.app` added in each Vercel project (Settings → Domains), each producing its own unique CNAME target (Vercel's newer per-domain format, e.g. `1e574478dfa47073.vercel-dns-01...`, not the older generic `cname.vercel-dns.com`). Matching CNAME records added in Cloudflare DNS for `tailiq.app`, each set to **DNS only** (not proxied) to avoid SSL/redirect conflicts between Cloudflare's and Vercel's edge networks. All three confirmed by Alan loading correctly over HTTPS with a valid cert.
- **Known follow-up, not solved this session:** all four Vercel projects still deploy the entire `api/` folder as serverless functions, since they all build from the same repo — the three new surfaces carry function weight (Firebase admin, email ingestion, invite/share endpoints, etc.) they don't actually need yet. Not a problem functionally today (nothing calls those endpoints from the stub pages), but worth pruning once TailiQ Specs/the free tools are actually built out for real, since some of that weight is genuinely surface-specific (e.g. `api/email-ingest.js` is Full-platform-only).
- **Domain structure now fully live:** `app.tailiq.app` (Full platform), `specs.tailiq.app` (TailiQ Specs, personal tier), `airframe.tailiq.app` (free airframe tool), `engine.tailiq.app` (free engine parse) — matches the naming/domain decisions already locked in `COMMERCIAL_VISION.md` §2 and `tailiq-engines-scoping-handoff.md`.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE — infra and all four domains confirmed live. Building the real TailiQ Specs / free airframe / free engine product experiences behind each stub is separate, unstarted future work.

### 4.65 Four-Layer Navigation Restructure — ✅ BUILT (closes 4.53, July 2026)
- **Fleet-level nav** (`App.jsx`) — flat bar (Dashboard, Fleet Exposure, Upload, Prospects, Admin) replaced with the four-layer model: Details · Calendar · Financials · Scenarios, then Prospects · Upload, then Admin — role-gated per `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` §7a's table (Data Entry: Details/Prospects/Upload only; Viewer: everything except Upload/Admin; Editor: everything except Admin; Admin: everything). Calendar and Scenarios at fleet level render a plain "Coming soon" card — neither is built yet, per Alan's explicit choice when asked how to handle the not-yet-built tabs.
- **Asset-level nav** (`AssetView.jsx`) — the old flat sub-tab row (Overview/Specs/Engines/…) is now nested one level under a new **Details** top tab, with **Calendar**, **Financials**, and **Scenarios** as sibling top-level tabs (hidden entirely for Data Entry). **Financials** is the old Fly-Forward page, **Calendar** is the old Maintenance Calendar page — both are no longer separate full-page views reached by a button; they render as tabs inside `AssetView`, with their old internal "← back" headers removed (the tab bar handles navigation now). Jumping in directly from Portfolio or Fleet Exposure ("🚀 Fly Forward") now opens the asset straight onto its Financials tab via a new `initialLayer` prop, rather than a separate `flyforward`/`maintenancecal` app-level view state (both removed from `App.jsx`).
- **See 4.59 for the follow-up visual-hierarchy pass** (pill styling, header repositioning) done in a later round the same day.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE and confirmed live — verified in a later session by direct read of the live `App.jsx`/`AssetView.jsx` (four-layer nav rendering correctly, role-gated per Section 7a). Closes `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.53.

### 4.64 Four-Role Permission Model — ✅ BUILT (closes 4.54, July 2026)
- **Firebase custom claims:** `dataEntry` added as a fourth role value alongside `editor`/`viewer` (admin remains bootstrap-only via `/api/bootstrap-admin`, unchanged). `api/set-role.js` and `api/invite-user.js` both updated — role-change and invite validation now accept `editor`/`viewer`/`dataEntry`.
- **Role-change now forces a prompt logout — new, not part of the original 4.54 scoping.** Alan flagged that changing a signed-in user's role had no visible effect until they happened to sign out and back in on their own. Fixed two ways together: (1) `api/set-role.js` calls `auth.revokeRefreshTokens(uid)` immediately after `setCustomUserClaims`; (2) client-side, `App.jsx` now force-refreshes the signed-in user's ID token every 45 seconds and on window focus, comparing the fresh claim against the currently-loaded role — a mismatch or a refresh failure (revoked session) triggers an explicit sign-out with a "please sign in again" notification. Not instant, but bounded to well under a minute in practice rather than up to an hour (the token's natural cache lifetime).
- **Firestore rules updated** (`firestore.rules`, published by Alan in Firebase Console) — `dataEntry` added to the allowed-roles list for `leases`/`reserves`/`assets` writes only, matching the role's actual job (lease/reserve pot entry, and specs/LLP/avionics data written via the Quick Import/Upload flows). Deliberately **not** added to `scheduledEvents`/`seasonalityProfile` — those are Calendar-tab data, and Data Entry never sees the Calendar tab in the nav.
- **Admin UI** (`AdminView.jsx`) — Invite User and Manage Users both gained a "Data Entry" option; Data Entry given its own colour in the role list; copy line updated to describe all three assignable roles (Admin remains system-assigned only).
- **Files touched:** `App.jsx`, `AdminView.jsx`, `api/set-role.js`, `api/invite-user.js`, `firestore.rules`.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE and confirmed live — verified in a later session by direct read of the live `App.jsx`: `canSeeAdvanced` correctly excludes only `dataEntry` (so Viewer/Editor/Admin all see Financials/Fleet Exposure), and `canUpload` correctly excludes `viewer`. Closes `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.54 and the underlying Fleet Exposure role-gating item (4.45).

### 4.63 Vercel Hobby deployment cap hit mid-session — operational, not a code issue (July 2026)
- **What happened:** every individual file uploaded via GitHub's web UI auto-triggers its own Vercel deployment. A session involving ~10+ separate file pastes burned through the Hobby plan's 100-deployments-per-rolling-24-hours cap, blocking further deploys with `Resource is limited - try again in 24 hours (code: "api-deployments-free-per-day")`.
- **Not a regression** — the site keeps serving the last successfully-built deployment; a blocked deploy does not take the app offline. Confirmed via the rolling-window mechanic: each blocked deployment slot frees up individually, 24 hours after that specific deployment, not all at once at a fixed daily reset.
- **A related, now-resolved build failure was caused by the same one-file-at-a-time upload pattern:** `App.jsx` (importing the new `NavPill` export) and `AssetView.jsx` (adding that export) were committed ~40 seconds apart, and Vercel auto-deployed in between — building `App.jsx` against a not-yet-updated `AssetView.jsx` and failing with `"NavPill" is not exported by "src/components/AssetView.jsx"`. Confirmed both files were correct once both commits landed; the next deploy would have built clean had the deployment cap not also been hit around the same time.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — Vercel Pro removes the daily cap entirely and was already a known prerequisite before onboarding any paying client (4.x Vercel Pro upgrade item, roadmap Section 6). Until upgraded: paste multiple related files into GitHub in as tight a window as possible, or batch several file changes into a single local commit/upload where the workflow allows, to reduce the number of intermediate (possibly inconsistent) auto-deploys.

### 4.62 Prospects/Upload fine-grained edit-permission audit — ✅ RESOLVED, not a gap (confirmed with Alan, July 2026)
- **Gap flagged while building the four-role model:** nav-level visibility for Prospects/Upload is correctly role-gated (4.64), but `ProspectEditor` itself has no role gating — a signed-in Viewer or Data Entry can save an edit to a prospect asset.
- **Confirmed with Alan: this is intended, not a bug.** Viewer's "edits nothing permanent" principle exists to protect live fleet financial data (leases, reserve pots, Fly-Forward inputs) — Prospects are ad hoc/deal-evaluation assets, entirely separate from the live fleet (see 4.15), so editing one carries none of that risk. Alan wants both Viewer and Data Entry able to create and edit Prospects freely.
- **No code change needed.** `ProspectEditor` stays ungated by design. Closing this item — do not re-open it as a permission gap in a future audit.
- **Priority:** ✅ Resolved — confirmed deliberate, not tracked as debt.

- **Lease Wizard relocated from the asset header into the Financials tab.** The old always-visible "📄 Lease" header button is gone entirely — `FlyForward.jsx` (Financials tab) now owns the Lease Wizard state itself, showing "📄 Set Up Lease" when `asset.currentLeaseId` is unset, or "📄 Edit Lease" once a lease exists. Avionics uploader relabelled "Upload Avionics Listing" (was "Avionics Spec") and gained the explanatory sentence it had been missing since the very first wording-consistency pass.

Fixed with an early `if(!open)return null;` so nothing renders at all until the dropdown is actually opened. ### 4.56 Vite Migration — ✅ DONE (Sonnet build session, July 2026)
- **Context:** Migrated from the single-file `index.html`/Babel-in-browser app to a Vite-bundled modular project, per the July 2026 Opus scoping session (2.1). Single entry point only — reproduces the old app 1:1, no feature changes. The `APP_SURFACE` multi-entry split is separate future work, not done here.
- **Structure:** 7 files under `src/lib/` (non-component helpers, grouped by purpose — `db.js`, `assetHelpers.js`, `techSpec.js`, `extraction.js`, `pots.js`, `uploadHelpers.js`, `flyForwardHelpers.js`) and 14 files under `src/components/` (one per feature area — Auth, Dashboard, AssetView, LeaseWizard, AssetTabs, PhotosAndSpecs, UploadView, BulkLeaseImport, AdminView, PortfolioView, Prospects, FlyForward, GuideView), plus `App.jsx` as orchestrator. `/calculations` and `techSpecBuilder.js` untouched in content — moved to `public/calculations/` so Vite serves them as static assets exactly as before. `/api` untouched in content.
- **Real bugs found and fixed during the deploy, not just the code split (all confirmed live):**
  1. **`public/calculations/utilisation.js` alone wasn't enough — `api/email-ingest.js` reads `calculations/utilisation.js` directly off disk** (`fs.readFileSync`, not a `<script>` tag), so it needed the file restored at the repo root too, alongside the copy in `public/`. Two copies of this one file now exist by design — the root one for the serverless function, the `public/` one for the browser.
  2. **`package.json`'s `"type": "module"` broke every `/api/*.js` function** — they use CommonJS `require(...)`, and Node treats all `.js` as ESM once that flag is set. Fixed by removing the flag and renaming `vite.config.js` → `vite.config.mjs` (Vite auto-detects `.mjs` configs without needing the package-level flag).
  3. **`firebase-admin`, `busboy`, and `xlsx` (server-side) were never in `package.json`** — before this migration there was no `npm install` step at all, so six of eight `/api` functions (`bootstrap-admin`, `email-ingest`, `invite-user`, `landing-interest`, `set-role`, `share/[token]`) had unresolved dependencies that only surfaced once a real install/build step existed. All added; `config.js` and `extract.js` needed nothing extra.
  4. **`middleware.js` imports `@vercel/functions`** — same root cause as #3, added to dependencies.
  5. **Vite's default build target doesn't support top-level `await`** — the Firebase-init inline module script (kept verbatim from the old app) uses it directly; `vite.config.mjs`'s `build.target` set to `esnext` to fix, since every browser this app targets already supports it natively.
- **`public/share.html`, `public/tailiq_landing.html`** — both moved from the repo root into `public/` for the same reason as the calc scripts (Vite only copies `public/` verbatim into `dist/`); `middleware.js`'s rewrite to `/tailiq_landing.html` and `vercel.json`'s `/share/:token` rewrite both continue to resolve correctly post-move, unchanged themselves.
- **`vercel.json`** — merged rather than replaced: kept the existing `functions`/`rewrites` blocks (60s `api/extract.js` timeout, `/share/:token` rewrite), added `framework`/`buildCommand`/`outputDirectory` for the Vite build.
- **Verified end-to-end post-deploy:** sign-in, Dashboard, tech spec generation, share link (QR + direct), Fly-Forward/Maintenance Calendar, Admin invite/set-role, and both email-ingestion paths (new report on an existing asset, and a brand-new asset) — all confirmed working by Alan.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt. Follow-up (`APP_SURFACE` multi-entry split) is separate, queued next.

### 4.55 Layer 3 (Scenarios) — 📋 FULLY SCOPED, NOT YET BUILT (Opus session, July 2026, NO CODE CHANGED)
- **Context:** Layer 3 was the one major Layer 2/3 surface with zero prior design work — no scoping session, no build handoff, unlike every other Brain 3–6 feature. This session resolved it completely. Full detail in `layer3-scenarios-build-handoff.md`; do not re-scope, open the build session directly against that doc.
- **What Layer 3 actually is (four original roadmap items resolved to three):** (1) per-asset scenario exploration — three sliders (utilisation rate, lease extension, FH:FC ratio) plus one-at-a-time natural-language chat box, side-by-side base case vs. scenario, lives inside a restructured asset view (see 4.53); (2) Route Suitability Matcher — fleet-level, input FH/month + FC/month + start/end date, ranks assets by operational fit and financial impact (re-scopes Section 11 of the roadmap with a concrete design); (3) AI narrative summaries — confirmed alive as a scoped exception to deterministic-outputs-only (see below). **Killed:** "portfolio stress testing" as a standalone feature — replaced by an equivalent fleet-wide chat box on Fleet Exposure, same one-at-a-time pattern as the asset level.
- **AI narrative exception, reasoning locked in:** the deterministic-outputs-only principle (no probabilistic modeling or AI-generated narrative in financial outputs) applies to Layer 1/2, where a hallucinated number kills the product. Layer 3 is explicitly hypothetical "what if" exploration, not a statement of fact — the numbers are still produced deterministically by Brains 3/4/5/6, the AI narrative sits on top explaining the delta with visible caveats (must be styled/labelled distinctly from calculated figures so it's never mistaken for one). This does not reopen the Layer 1/2 kill.
- **No new calculation engines.** Brains 7 (`scenarioEngine.js`), 8 (`routeMatcher.js`), 9 (`narrativeGen.js`) are orchestration/translation/narration only — they call Brains 3/4/5/6 unmodified. The AI's job is translating natural language into concrete Brain inputs (behind the scenes, not shown to the user as an intermediate confirmation step — Alan explicitly rejected a "confirm the AI's interpretation" step as too much friction) and narrating the result, never calculating.
- **One scenario at a time, no stacking** — deliberate risk mitigation. Stacking natural-language modifications compounds translation error (a misinterpretation in one layer compounding with a correct interpretation in another), producing a result that looks plausible but is wrong in a hard-to-spot way. Clearing and re-describing a new scenario avoids this entirely.
- **Whole-asset scope, not per-pot** — sliders modify asset-level inputs (utilisation, lease end date, FH:FC ratio); every pot recalculates downstream. No per-pot scenario controls.
- **Killed slider:** escalation rate. Reviewed yearly against the real catalogue — a periodic factual update, not a hypothetical.
- **Fully non-destructive** — nothing in Layer 3 writes to Firestore. This is what makes it safe to give the Viewer role (C-suite, see 4.54) unrestricted Scenarios access.
- **Not this session's scope:** any code, any Firestore schema change, any UI build. This is scoping only.
- **Priority:** 📌 Scoped and ready — recommended build order (per `layer3-scenarios-build-handoff.md` §9): nav/role restructure (4.53/4.54) first since everything else depends on it, then per-asset Scenarios, then Route Matcher, then fleet-wide chat box, then AI narrative layered on last.

### 4.54 Four-Role Permission Model — ✅ BUILT (superseded by 4.64, July 2026 — kept here for the original design rationale)
- **Context:** the three-role model (Admin/Editor/Viewer) couldn't cleanly serve two real use cases surfaced by the Layer 3 design: C-suite needing full financial/scenario visibility with zero edit rights, and a data-entry person (intern/ops staff) needing Upload+Prospects without seeing financials. This also closes the open Fleet Exposure role-gating item (4.45) and the nav-IA restructure question (`VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 19).
- **New model — four roles, replacing the three-role structure in `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 7:**
  - **Admin** — everything + user management (unchanged from today)
  - **Editor** — everything except user management (unchanged from today)
  - **Viewer** (redefined) — sees everything including financials and Scenarios; **edits nothing permanent.** Full read access carries no write risk because Scenarios are inherently non-destructive (4.55). This is the C-suite role.
  - **Data Entry** (new) — Dashboard (Details), Prospects, Upload, asset detail for verification. Handles the legwork: utilisation reports, LLP sheets, specs, prospects, lease upload, reserve pot entry. Sees and enters the raw inputs, never sees the outputs (Fly-Forward projections, Fleet Exposure, Scenarios) — same principle as the person entering invoices not seeing the P&L.
- **Portfolio View is not a role, it's a presentation mode** — already built, no toggle needed. The same Viewer (C-suite) who reviews financials at their desk clicks to Portfolio before sharing their screen with a lessee or handing over their phone.
- **Role naming** — "Data Entry" is a working label, not final. Deferred to a later naming pass once the app is in more hands.
- **Not built this session** — this is a Firebase custom-claims + Firestore rules change (currently `admin`/`editor`/`viewer` claims exist; needs a fourth `dataEntry` claim added) plus nav-visibility logic in `index.html`. See 4.53 for the nav side.
- **Priority:** ✅ Built and confirmed live — see 4.64 for build detail and live confirmation. This entry retained for the original design rationale (why four roles, why Viewer sees financials, why Data Entry is scoped the way it is).

### 4.53 Four-Layer Navigation Restructure — ✅ BUILT (superseded by 4.65, July 2026 — kept here for the original design rationale)
- **Context:** the current flat nav bar (Dashboard, Portfolio, Fleet Exposure, Upload, Prospects, Admin) has no hierarchy and was about to get worse as Layer 3 adds more surfaces. Also resolves the "Fly-Forward is getting overloaded" problem (projection + maintenance calendar + seasonality + now scenarios, all crammed into one page).
- **The model — same language at fleet and asset level, replacing `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 3's Layer 1/2/3 framing with something a user actually navigates by:**
  - **Details** — fleet: Dashboard/overview. Asset: specs, engines, LLPs, avionics, lease data.
  - **Calendar** — fleet: event clustering across the fleet. Asset: maintenance calendar, seasonality.
  - **Financials** — fleet: Fleet Exposure. Asset: projection, pot-by-pot gap/surplus.
  - **Scenarios** — fleet: Route Matcher + fleet-wide chat box. Asset: sliders + chat box, side-by-side.
- **Why four, not three:** the old Layer 2 was doing two distinct jobs (money + maintenance calendar) crammed into one Fly-Forward page. Splitting them gives each a clear, single-purpose tab.
- **"Fly-Forward" stops being a user-facing page name** — it becomes the internal engine powering the asset-level Calendar/Financials/Scenarios tabs. User navigates by concept, not by engine name.
- **Upload and Prospects stay visible, not buried** — they're daily-use workflow tools, not views. Same nav bar as the four layers, visually separated (subtle divider/spacing) rather than moved to a secondary menu, per Alan: *"i dont want to bury uploads and prospects tho, these will be regular tools."*
- **Portfolio keeps its own nav pill** — presentation-safe surface, not a layer, unchanged.
- **Nav visibility is role-dependent** (see 4.54) — no role ever sees an empty group. Full per-role breakdown in `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 7.
- **Not built this session** — `index.html` nav bar restructure + asset-view tab restructure (Fly-Forward becomes three tabs instead of one page with buried buttons).
- **Priority:** ✅ Built and confirmed live — see 4.65 for build detail and live confirmation. This entry retained for the original design rationale.

- **Original roadmap item:** "portfolio stress testing" — apply a fleet-wide what-if (e.g. utilisation drop) and see the aggregate impact on Fleet Exposure.
- **Why killed:** once the fleet-wide chat box (4.55) was scoped, a dedicated stress-testing mode became redundant — the same outcome (including a COVID-style all-grounded scenario) is achieved by describing the hypothetical in natural language, no separate UI needed.

### 4.51 Tech Spec Competitive Comparison + Avionics Manufacturer Decision (Sonnet session, July 2026, NO CODE CHANGED)
- **Context:** Alan uploaded three full third-party tech specs (GECAS/TAP A330-200 CF6, Deucalion A330-223 PW4168A, Kahala 737-800 CFM56-7B) for a general format comparison against TailiQ's own tech spec output, then ten real avionics spec sheets (ITA Airways ×2, Spring Airlines, Croatia Airlines, D-AICP/CTN, Ultra Air, SmartLynx, Condor, KM Malta) specifically to settle the Manufacturer-field question left open by 4.50.
- **General comparison findings (GECAS/Deucalion/Kahala):** confirmed TailiQ's existing Avionics ATA-chapter grouping, engine LLP table shape, and most-recent-only shop-visit display are all in line with market practice — Kahala (simplest, broker-style doc) matches most-recent-only; GECAS/Deucalion (fuller lessor-issued docs) show full shop-visit history, which is the more thorough end of the range rather than evidence TailiQ's choice is wrong. TailiQ's card-based/icon-header/progress-bar tech spec is visually more developed than all three references, which are plain-table documents.
- **Decided this session — queued for a build session:**
  1. **Check facility** — new free-text field on each Check History row (alongside existing Last Date/Next Date), same visibility-toggle pattern as the rest of that tab. Low risk, additive only.
  2. **Annual utilisation / FH:CY ratio** — derived-on-read stat at the airframe level (from existing TSN/CSN/DOM), same pattern as the FH:FC ratio already shown for engines (`tailiq-engines-scoping-handoff.md` §4). No new stored fields.
  3. **Avionics Manufacturer field** — reopens part of 4.50's original reasoning. A 10-sample tally across real airline avionics sheets found Manufacturer present in 7/10 (vs Part Number, present in 10/10 and Description in 10/10) — common enough to be worth adding back, but genuinely inconsistent enough (confirmed by Alan against GECAS specifically, which carries Manufacturer with no P/N at all — the reverse of the norm) that it must stay an **independently optional field**, never paired with or inferred from Part Number. **Decided approach:** AI best-effort extraction, identical treatment to Description/P/N — populated when the source document has it, left blank when it doesn't, corrected on the existing mandatory review/edit screen same as any other field. This is a partial reversal of 4.50's "dropped by design, too inconsistent to keep" reasoning for Manufacturer specifically (Description/P/N remain as-is; Qty/Serial Number/Install Date remain dropped — those stayed genuinely rare across the full 10-sample set). **Description field also confirmed to stay verbatim, no normalisation** — real samples use wildly different terms for the same LRU (e.g. "ADIRU" vs "Air Data Inertial Reference Unit"), and Alan confirmed whatever the source document says is what should show, no mapping to a standard term list.
- **Explicitly dropped/parked this session:**
  - **Registration history** (full date/reg/country table, per Deucalion) — dropped, not worth the manual-entry effort for the trading-value case it would serve.
  - **Airframe-level certification basis** (type certificate numbers, per Deucalion) — parked, not rejected. Alan flagged this is really a Type Certificate Data Sheet reference rather than a per-asset field, and wants to look into whether it's worth tracking before it's designed. No schema decision made.
  - **Owned vs Operated weight distinction** (per Deucalion) — considered, kept as-is. Current single-weight-set model is fine for Maverick's own fleet; the owned/operated split matters more to a lessor tracking a separate title-holding structure.
  - **Scraping Airfleets.net for registration/operational history** — explicitly rejected, not just deprioritised. Reasoning: scraping a no-API third-party site is an ongoing maintenance liability (any HTML change silently breaks it), sits in a legal grey area re: terms of use, and Airfleets' community-maintained data is unverifiable — putting it in a spec the product positions as auditable and defensible would undercut that standard. Automating a feature already judged not worth manual effort doesn't change the value side of that equation, only moves the cost elsewhere.
  - **Kahala-style side-by-side dual-engine summary card** — considered, rejected as pure duplication. TailiQ's existing per-engine full-page treatment (pages 3–4: ESN/model/thrust header, FH/CSN, lowest-limiter badge + progress bar, most-recent shop visit, full LLP table) already carries more information per engine than Kahala's compact two-column summary row. A side-by-side card would restate the same TSN/CSN/limiter numbers in a denser, less complete form. Logged here specifically so this doesn't get re-proposed later.
  - **Standalone Avionics disclaimer footnote** (re: part-number interchangeability, per ITA Airways' standard caveat) — not needed; the existing page 1 disclaimer (see Section 13, `TECH_DEBT.md` 0a's "Full Branding Sweep + Tech Spec Disclaimer System Rebuilt") already covers this class of caveat.
- **Priority:** 📌 Decisions logged, three items queued for next build session (none built yet this session).

### 4.50 Avionics LRU List — ✅ BUILT, replaces 4.14 entirely (Sonnet session, July 2026, `index.html` + `techSpecBuilder.js`)
- **Context:** 4.14's fixed 8-field Avionics tab (FMS, EFIS/Displays, Autopilot, Comms, Nav, Surveillance, Weather, Connectivity) is retired outright, not extended. Reviewed 7 real avionics spec sheets across different airlines/formats (ITA Airways, Spring Airlines, Croatia Airlines, D-AICP, Ultra Air, SmartLynx, Condor) during Opus-equivalent scoping earlier in this session — no two shared the same column set; Description + Part Number were the only two fields present in every single sample. Manufacturer, Qty, Serial Number, Install Date, and ATA/FIN position codes were all dropped by design — real information in some samples, but inconsistent enough across formats that keeping them would have meant a parser silently guessing at column meaning per document. **No live-data migration** — Alan confirmed existing 8-field data does not need preserving.
- **New schema:** `asset.avionicsLRU = {rows:[{id, ataChapter, description, partNumber, hidden}], hiddenChapters:[chapterLabel,...]}`. `ataChapter` is a string label (e.g. `"ATA 34 — Navigation"`) or `null` — never inferred from description text alone.
- **Grouping logic, three cases in priority order:** (1) explicit ATA chapter headers already printed in the source document — used verbatim; (2) no headers, but every row carries its own ATA number — chapter label derived via a new `ATA_CHAPTER_MAP` lookup (`index.html`, standard ATA-100 chapter names, 21 through 80); (3) neither exists — row's `ataChapter` stays `null`, rendered in an "Ungrouped" flat list in-app (no header at all in the tech spec print output, since there's nothing genuine to print).
- **Visibility toggles — independent per-row AND per-chapter.** Hiding a whole chapter does not destroy individual row `hidden` state underneath — un-hiding the chapter restores exactly what was visible before, since row-level and chapter-level hidden state are stored and checked separately (`d.hiddenChapters` vs each row's own `hidden` flag).
- **Entry point:** dedicated "Upload Spec" button directly on the asset's own Avionics tab (same per-asset pattern as the Lease button) — no fleet-matching step, since the asset is already known. One parse tier only (no Quick/Confidential split like lease import — a full parse always goes through review regardless).
- **Extraction (`extractAvionicsLRU`, `index.html`):** same `/api/extract` document-upload pattern as `extractLLPSheet` — PDF sent as base64 `type:"document"` content alongside `AVIONICS_LRU_PROMPT`. **Sonnet, not Haiku** — same reasoning as `ENGINE_LLP_PROMPT` (dense multi-page tabular extraction is at/above Haiku's reliable ceiling). `max_tokens` raised to 8000 (vs LLP's 4000) since real samples reviewed ran up to 5 pages / 50+ rows. Same `{ok,data}`/`{ok:false,raw}`/`{error}` response-envelope handling as every other `/api/extract` caller.
- **Flow: upload → parse → review/edit screen → confirm → save.** `AvionicsLRUUploader` shows every extracted row as editable (description/P/N/chapter, plus per-row delete) before anything touches Firestore — matches the lease-import precedent of never auto-saving straight from an AI extraction. **A confirmed save fully REPLACES `asset.avionicsLRU.rows`**, not a merge — known trade-off: a previously-hidden row's hidden state isn't preserved across a re-upload of the same asset's updated spec sheet, since new rows have no stable identity to match against old ones. Acceptable for now; flag if re-uploads become a real workflow pain point.
- **Tech spec rendering (`techSpecBuilder.js`):** Avionics section rewritten to read `asset.avionicsLRU` instead of the old `asset.avionics`. Reuses the file's existing `col2()` table-based two-column helper (already used elsewhere for e.g. MTOW/MLW) — **not** CSS grid/flexbox, consistent with the June 2026 Specifications-table lesson that wkhtmltopdf silently collapses those back to single-column. Splits into two columns once total *visible* rows exceed a tunable threshold (`AVIONICS_LRU_SPLIT_THRESHOLD`, starting value 25 — Alan's own estimate, "eyeball and tune" per his stub-buffer-tolerance precedent, not yet load-tested against a huge real fleet asset). Split is **whole-page, not per-chapter** (Alan's explicit call): tables are opened lazily, only on the first row actually written to a column, so a split landing exactly on a chapter boundary never leaves a stray header-only empty table; a split landing **mid-chapter** correctly **repeats that chapter's header** at the top of column two, per Alan's "repeat for visibility" decision. Verified with a synthetic Node harness (10-row/no-split, 40-row/5-chapter/split-with-repeat, 40-row/no-chapters/split-with-zero-headers) before handoff, then confirmed working end-to-end by Alan against real generated specs.
- **Files touched:** `index.html` (`ATA_CHAPTER_MAP`, `ataChapterLabel`, `ataChapterSortNum`, `AVIONICS_LRU_PROMPT`, `extractAvionicsLRU`, `AvionicsLRUReviewRow`, `AvionicsLRUUploader`, `AvionicsTab` fully rewritten — old `AVIONICS_TEMPLATE`/`buildAvionicsRows`/`ataNumSort` removed with no dangling references); `techSpecBuilder.js` (Avionics section renderer fully rewritten).
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — built and confirmed working by Alan, both in-app and tech spec output. Supersedes 4.14 entirely (see that entry).

### 4.49 Fixed Reserve Pot Accrual Basis — Two Bugs Found and Fixed (Sonnet session, July 2026, `index.html`)
- **Bug 1 — `validatePotWithAI`'s prompt asserted a false premise.** The non-engine context string told Claude every non-engine pot is "usually accrued per flight hour," causing false-positive warnings on AF-6Y/AF-12Y/LG-OH (correctly `per_month` — checks fall due on a fixed calendar schedule, not based on flying) and AP-OH (correctly `per_APU_hr`, not flight hours at all). Found when Alan hit the warning on a genuinely-correct `$/Month` AF-6Y entry and pushed back with the underlying domain fact. Fixed by building `EXPECTED_BASIS_BY_CODE` from `FIXED_RESERVE_POT_DEFS` itself (the same table 4.43 already corrected) and telling Claude explicitly what's correct for that pot code, rather than asking it to infer from general aviation knowledge — the inference itself was the bug, not a fluke.
- **Bug 2 — accrual basis `<select>` dropdown was missing the `per_APU_hr` option entirely.** AP-OH's genuinely-correct underlying value had no matching `<option>` to render against, so the browser silently fell back to displaying the first option in the list (`$/Flight Hour`) — a display-only bug, not data corruption (React's controlled `<select>` never touches the real `pot.accrualBasis` unless `onChange` actually fires), but confusing enough that it was the direct cause of the Bug 1 report being raised in the first place. Fixed by adding the missing `<option value="per_APU_hr">$ / APU Hour</option>`.
- **Both verified against `MSN TEST-99`** — AF-6Y saves clean with no warning on `$/Month`; AP-OH's dropdown now correctly shows `$/APU Hour`. Babel transform clean on both passes.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — no longer tracked as open debt.
