### 4.45 Fleet Exposure View — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `calculations/fleetExposure.js` + `index.html`)
- **Context:** Built per `fleet-exposure-build-handoff.md` (Opus scoping session) — the fleet-level "do we have events clustering in the same month" view that's structurally invisible at per-asset level. Per-asset "asset risk dashboard" confirmed scoped OUT, not deferred (handoff §0) — closes that `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 17 line item permanently rather than carrying it forward.
- **New pure module `calculations/fleetExposure.js`** — environment-agnostic, no window/Firebase/UI, same Brain/Body discipline as the other calculation files. Takes assembled per-asset data (already-anchored pots, checks, engines, utilisation, Brain 6 inputs) plus injected Brain functions (`projectReservePot`, `projectEnLpPot`, `buildMaintenanceCalendar`) and returns `{ headline, timeAxis, assetAxis, atoms, excludedAssets }`. 35/35 Node E2E tests passing (real `flyForward.js`/`maintenanceCal.js` code, window-shim pattern, no mocks).
- **Design decision — Option B, confirmed with Alan mid-session:** the module runs its own pass-1/Brain-6/pass-2 orchestration, independent of `index.html`'s `buildFlyForwardProjection`, rather than consuming that function's output. This is deliberate duplication in service of eventual server-side portability (see 4.46 above) — not an oversight.
- **Shared, not duplicated:** pot anchoring (`asset.checks`/`landingGear`/EN-PR dates → `firstEventOverrideDate`) was extracted into a standalone `anchorReservePots()` function in `index.html`, called by both `buildFlyForwardProjection` and the new Fleet Exposure assembly path. This is thin, mechanical date-field mapping, not real orchestration, so sharing it carries none of the portability constraints that keep the two orchestration copies separate.
- **Status rule:** tested against **high**, not likely (green covers high; amber covers low but not high; red doesn't cover low) — see `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 18.
- **Horizon:** in-lease-horizon events get a full verdict; events past lease end are disclosed (date + cost band) out to a 24-month app-level constant (`FLEET_EXPOSURE_HORIZON_MONTHS` in `index.html`), with `status: null` — never red/green.
- **Exclusions are typed**, not free-text: `NO_LEASE | POTS_OUTSTANDING | STALE_UTILISATION | COMPUTE_ERROR`, per-asset try/catch, headline never zero-fills or refuses to total.
- **Real bug found and fixed during test-writing:** an invalid/malformed `lease.leaseEnd` was silently producing `NaN` throughout the projection (empty output, no error) rather than surfacing as a problem. Added explicit date-validity checking so this now correctly throws and surfaces as `COMPUTE_ERROR` — exactly the class of bug that exclusion type exists to catch.
- **UI:** new `FleetExposureView` — headline (clickable completeness gap) → time axis (month-bucketed, cost/coverage/gap totals) → asset axis (ranked worst-first). New nav-pill entry "Fleet Exposure", ungated by role (see the open item below — flagged, not resolved).
- **Deviation from the handoff, flagged rather than hidden:** the handoff specified reusing `MaintenanceCalendarView` directly for the time axis (asset filter removed, cost total added). Built as a new dedicated rendering in the same visual language instead — `MaintenanceCalendarView`'s real value beyond layout is its single-asset edit affordances (accept seasonality / airline-stated date / duration), which don't have a sensible fleet-level meaning, so branching it into a multi-asset mode felt like more re-derivation than reuse. Worth a second look if that judgment call doesn't sit right in practice.
- **Post-build fix (same session):** clicking an MSN in the time axis or asset axis originally routed to the asset overview (`AssetView`); changed to route to Fly-Forward instead (`ffOrigin` set to `"fleetexposure"` so the back button returns here) — the exposure numbers are Fly-Forward's own atoms, so the drill-down should land on the thing that explains the number, not a generic overview.
- **Not yet done / flagged for next session:**
  1. Nav placement — currently a nav-pill tab, deliberately not surfaced on Portfolio (client-facing screen-share risk — Alan does not want risk figures visible on the screen he shares with clients). Alan wants to sit with current placement before deciding anything further.
  2. **Fleet Exposure role-gating — ✅ BUILT and confirmed live (July 2026).** Was genuinely open, not resolved, at this session's close. Settled by the four-role model (4.54/4.64) and confirmed built and live by direct read of `App.jsx`'s `canSeeAdvanced` gate (`userRole!=='dataEntry'`): Fleet Exposure sits in the "Financials" nav group, visible to Viewer/Editor/Admin, never to Data Entry. No further action needed on this item.
  3. Nav IA / layer-based restructure — Alan's thinking that the three main nav-pill buttons could map to the three product layers (Dashboard≈Layer 1, Fleet Exposure≈Layer 2, a future Scenarios/slider view≈Layer 3), with Upload/Prospects/Admin — workflow tools, not layer views — moved to a secondary location. Related: `PortfolioView`'s role reconsidered as purely the client-facing/screen-share-safe view rather than a default landing screen; Viewer-role auto-landing on Portfolio may be conflating "shouldn't see financials" with "about to screen-share with a client." Needs its own scoping session once there's a clearer picture of who actually uses Viewer role day-to-day.

### 4.44 Maintenance Calendar Firestore Collections — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `index.html` + `firestore.rules`)
- **Context:** Closed the "← START HERE" item carried over from 4.39/4.40 — `scheduledEvents`, `seasonalityProfile`, and `shopVisitProjections` were still unbuilt stubs with zero references in `index.html`; `maintenanceCal.js` was taking overrides/seasonalityProfile/costProjections as permanently-empty defaults.
- **Carried-over item 1 (real-lease `accrualBasis` audit) — explicitly NOT actioned.** Alan confirmed old lease data will be deleted; no console query run, no correction path built. Closes the 4.43 follow-up with no code change.
- **Six new `db` methods**, no transform layer between Firestore and Brain 6's input — confirmed via a Node fixture test (11/11 passing) feeding the exact db-shaped objects straight into the real `maintenanceCal.js`:
  - `getScheduledEvents`/`saveScheduledEventOverride`/`deleteScheduledEventOverride` — deterministic doc id `assetId_code_dueCycle` (upsert), matching Brain 6's own identity key exactly so an accepted suggestion or airline-stated date sticks to the right recomputed occurrence.
  - `getSeasonalityProfile`/`saveSeasonalityProfile` — one reviewed profile per asset, doc id = assetId, NOT append-only (unlike shopVisitProjections below).
  - `getShopVisitProjections`/`saveShopVisitProjection` — APPEND-ONLY via `addDoc`, per Section 5's critical schema rule.
- **`buildFlyForwardProjection()` factored out of `FlyForward`'s render body** into a standalone function so `MaintenanceCalendarView` (new) calls the identical Pass-1/Brain-6/Pass-2 orchestration rather than a re-derived copy that could drift. `FlyForward` now passes real `scheduledEvents`/`seasonalityProfile`/`costProjections` into `buildMaintenanceCalendar()` — no changes needed in `maintenanceCal.js` or `flyForward.js` themselves, exactly as predicted in 4.39/4.40's postscripts.
- **New `MaintenanceCalendarView`** (reached via a "📅 Maintenance Calendar" button on Fly-Forward): lists every projected event with its source badge (derived/seasonality/airline-stated), lets an admin/editor accept a seasonality suggestion, enter an airline-stated date, or edit a C-check's grounding duration — each a `scheduledEvents` override write, never a direct edit to the derived calendar (3.4: suggest-and-accept, never auto-move). Includes `SeasonalityProfileEditor` (12 monthly weightings + active weeks/year), inert until all 12 months are filled in, matching Brain 6's own "incomplete profile is ignored" rule.
- **Monthly `shopVisitProjections` snapshot** — a `useEffect` on `FlyForward` keyed to data-load identity (not render), throttled per pot-code to ~30 days since the last snapshot for that code. Writes the already-computed grounded projection's next event date and cost band. Explicitly a **calendar-cadence historical record, never read back into the live Fly-Forward numbers** — those stay a pure recompute off the reserve pot fields, unchanged. Skips EN-LP (`llp_cycles`) — its cost snapshot needs `llpWorkscope` from Brain 2's stack-sim vector; left as a follow-up rather than approximated.
- **Design discussion, not just build:** considered and rejected triggering the snapshot off utilisation-report uploads (with a day's delay for correction/dedup) — rejected because upload timing is asset-specific and irregular (manual vs. email-ingested reports land unpredictably), which would produce an uneven, cross-asset-incomparable series; a fixed monthly cadence gives an evenly-spaced trend line and solves the same-day-correction problem as a side effect, without needing upload-window logic or a new Cloud Function. Also discussed and explicitly parked two further ideas rather than building them: a **Projection History view** (chart of snapshot history over time per pot — real gap, no consumer of the history exists yet beyond the ±3-month cost join) and **actual-vs-projected reconciliation** (comparing a snapshot against what a shop visit really cost) — parked because there's currently no field anywhere capturing real shop-visit cost, and the feedback loop per check type is years long across a small fleet, so the prerequisite data-entry point needs its own scoping first.
- **Firestore security rules updated** (`firestore.rules`) — `scheduledEvents`/`seasonalityProfile` role-gated admin/editor (same as `leases`/`reserves`, since these are financial-projection-shaping edits); `shopVisitProjections` create open to any signed-in user (Viewer can trigger a snapshot just by having Fly-Forward open) with update/delete blocked outright, same immutable-log pattern as `auditLog`.
- **✅ DEPLOYED AND TESTED (July 2026, follow-up session).** Alan published the merged rules file in Firebase Console. Verified end-to-end, same order as 4.28's rollout: Admin/Editor writes to `scheduledEvents` (accepted a seasonality suggestion, edited a C-check duration) and `seasonalityProfile` (saved a monthly weighting) both succeeded; Viewer reads worked everywhere with no errors; Viewer writes to `scheduledEvents`/`seasonalityProfile` were rejected (confirmed as a silent `permission-denied` — the app itself doesn't yet surface *why* a Viewer's edit didn't save, a real but separate UX gap, not a rules bug); `shopVisitProjections` create succeeded for a Viewer session against an untouched asset/pot-code combo (a combo already inside its 30-day snapshot throttle window will not create a fresh doc regardless of role — expected, not a bug). This closes the "not yet deployed" item from the Brain 6 handoff.
- **Not yet done / flagged for next session:**
  1. **Seasonality Profile button relocation** — move from inside `MaintenanceCalendarView` out onto the Fly-Forward page itself, beside the Maintenance Calendar button (Alan's note, this session).
  2. **Projection History view** (see above) — real gap, not yet scoped.
  3. **EN-LP monthly snapshot** — skipped this session, needs Brain 2's `llpWorkscope` vector wired through.
  4. Actual-vs-projected reconciliation — parked, prerequisite (real shop-visit cost entry point) not yet scoped.
- **Tested:** Babel JSX transform clean on the full updated script block; Node fixture test (11/11) against the real `maintenanceCal.js` confirming airline-stated stickiness, override duration, seasonality-suggestion suppression once overridden, and cost-join tie-breaking by most-recent `calculatedAt` — all using the exact db-shaped objects, no reshape. No changes to `maintenanceCal.js` or `flyForward.js` — existing 15/20/54-test suites unaffected.
- **Priority:** ✅ Built — pending Alan's Firestore rules deploy before real use.

### 4.43 Reserve pot defaults, save validation, and Fly-Forward display order — ✅ FIXED (Sonnet session, July 2026, `index.html`)
- **Accrual basis defaults corrected.** `FIXED_RESERVE_POT_DEFS` hardcoded `accrualBasis: "per_FH"` for all four fixed pots — AF-6Y, AF-12Y, AP-OH, LG-OH — contradicting the locked Section 4 schema (`VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md`), which specifies AF-6Y/AF-12Y/LG-OH as `per_month` and AP-OH as `per_APU_hr`, matching the real lease's Maintenance Payment Rate schedule. Found while testing MSN TEST-99's grounding behaviour. Corrected to `per_month`/`per_month`/`per_APU_hr`/`per_month` respectively. EN-PR (`per_FH`) and EN-LP (`per_FC`) were never affected — those are correct by design.
  - **Second bug found while fixing the first:** the Lease/Reserve Setup wizard's pot-merge logic read `accrualBasis` unconditionally from the definition list, never from an already-saved pot's real Firestore value. Left as-is, correcting the default would have silently overwritten any already-confirmed real pot's actual saved `accrualBasis` the next time its wizard was reopened and ANY field re-saved — with no review step, and no way to tell it had happened. Fixed by preserving `ex?.accrualBasis ?? def.accrualBasis` (same pattern already used for `accrualRate`/`openingBalance`), so an existing real pot's saved value is never silently touched by this change. **Any real, already-saved lease pot that has the old wrong `per_FH` value needs a deliberate, reviewed correction — this fix intentionally does NOT do that automatically.** Worth a follow-up pass to identify which (if any) real leases are affected.
- **Opening balance no longer blocks Save.** `savePot()` required both `accrualRate` and `openingBalance` to be non-empty before saving — with no pre-filled default for either, forcing an arbitrary placeholder balance just to save a pot's rate/trigger data. Opening balance now defaults to `0` if left blank; accrual rate remains required (a `$0`-rate pot isn't a meaningful financial input the same way a `$0` starting balance is).
- **Fly-Forward pot display order now matches the tech spec layout.** Previously rendered in whatever order Firestore happened to return (no sort at all). Now sorted: airframe checks (AF-6Y, AF-12Y) → each engine's PR/LLP pair in position order (EN-PR-1, EN-LP-1, EN-PR-2, EN-LP-2) → LG-OH → AP-OH, with any custom pot falling in after. Single-engine assets and gaps in the sequence (e.g. no position-2 pots) both handled correctly.
- **Tested:** all three fixes verified in isolation against realistic scenarios (corrected defaults confirmed directly from the file; sort logic tested against shuffled input and a single-engine case; accrualBasis preservation tested against new/unsaved, already-saved-with-old-value, and deliberately-custom-set cases) — 5/5 new checks passing. Full existing suite re-run clean (54/54 across all four test files: `maintenanceCal`, `flyForward` regression, wiring integration, `utilisation` bugfix). Babel JSX transform re-verified on the full updated script block.
- **Priority:** ✅ Fixed and verified same session.

### 4.42 Brain 1 (`utilisation.js`) manually-created-asset first-report bug — 🔴 FOUND AND FIXED (manual testing, July 2026)
- **Found by Alan** continuing the same manual test session as 4.41, on the same test asset (MSN TEST-99) — the first utilisation report upload showed "Could not determine reporting gap... saved to history only" instead of applying, even after a clean asset with no prior reports.
- **Root cause:** `processUtilisationReport()`'s "brand new asset" fast path (constructs a fresh asset record from scratch) only fires when `previousAsset` is entirely absent (`if (!previousAsset)`). A manually-created asset already has a real Firestore record — it just has never received a report, so `_lastPeriod` is blank. That's neither "no asset at all" (handled) nor "an asset with a genuine prior period" (the normal path) — it fell into a gap between the two, landing in the "period_unparseable" branch (built for a genuinely garbled date string, a different problem entirely), which saves to history only and never applies to live state. Worse: **there was no way to ever recover** — every subsequent upload to that asset would see the same blank `_lastPeriod` and hit the identical trap forever, since the merge that would have set `_lastPeriod` never ran.
- **Second, related risk caught and fixed in the same pass:** simply treating this as a normal merge wasn't enough on its own — `previousAsset.airframe.currentFC`/`currentFH` sit at their blank `0` defaults on a manually-created asset, so running the ordinary delta-verification check would diff a real report's stated period FC/FH against a `0` baseline and produce a huge, entirely false "delta mismatch" warning on literally the asset's first-ever report. Delta verification is now skipped for this case, the same way it already is for a same-month merge.
- **Fix:** introduced `isFirstReportForExistingAsset` (`previousAsset` exists AND `_lastPeriod` is blank AND the new report's own period parses fine) as a distinct third case, separate from "brand new asset" and "existing asset with real history." Merges straight into the **existing** asset record — preserving every manually-entered field (LLPs, check history, specs, manufacturer/model) that reconstructing a fresh asset via the brand-new-asset path would otherwise have silently discarded — with no gap/delta check, since there's nothing prior to meaningfully compare against. A genuinely garbled (non-blank) existing `_lastPeriod` still correctly falls through to the real "unparseable" warning — this fix narrowly targets "blank," not "any unparseable value."
- **Tested:** new regression suite (`test-utilisation-first-report-bugfix.js`) reproduces Alan's exact scenario end-to-end — first report applies, no false mismatch, manually-entered LLP/checks/specs all survive the merge, and a genuine second report afterward computes the correct real delta (90 FC/250 FH over one month). Also re-verified five adjacent paths this fix must NOT have touched: a genuinely garbled existing period, a genuinely unparseable new report period, out-of-order upload, same-month merge, and a real multi-month gap with widened tolerance. 22/22 passing.
- **Why this matters beyond the one asset:** this is the first time `utilisation.js` has been read/tested this closely outside its original build session — it's real, live code affecting every asset's utilisation processing, not something scoped to Brain 6. Manually-created assets (spare engines, prospects converted to live assets, or any asset not created via a first PDF/Excel upload) would all have silently hit this same trap.
- **Priority:** 🔴 → ✅ Fixed and verified same session.

---

### 4.41 Brain 6 date-format bug — 🔴 FOUND AND FIXED (manual testing, July 2026, `maintenanceCal.js`)
- **Found by Alan in manual testing** (first real test of the 4.40 wiring against a live asset, MSN 1009): Fly-Forward showed false gap warnings — "'6 Year Check' is on file but has no usable nextDate or lastDate" and the same for 12 Year Check — on an asset whose Check History clearly showed real dates for both (6Y next due 27 Oct 2028, 12Y next due 26 Oct 2034).
- **Root cause:** `maintenanceCal.js`'s date parser only accepted `DD/MM/YYYY`, based on the Quick Extract AI prompt's date format. The actual stored format for `asset.checks[].lastDate`/`.nextDate` is **ISO (`YYYY-MM-DD`)** — confirmed via `index.html`'s `CheckDateInput` component, whose commit callback is literally named `iso` and writes straight into `checks[i].lastDate`/`nextDate`. `normalizeDate()` converts the AI's DD/MM/YYYY output to ISO before it's ever saved — a normalization step that wasn't visible when `maintenanceCal.js` was built (`techSpecBuilder.js`, where these helpers live, wasn't in scope/uploaded that session). The parser silently rejected every real, valid date, and every asset with genuine 6Y/12Y check dates on file would have shown this false warning.
- **Fix:** `parseCheckDate()` (renamed from `parseDMY`) now tries ISO first, falling back to `DD/MM/YYYY` only for any pre-`CheckDateInput` legacy record that might not have gone through normalization. Both formats covered rather than assuming a single convention.
- **New regression test added** (`test-maintenanceCal.js`) reproducing the exact bug: real ISO 6Y/12Y dates must NOT fire the gap warning, the resolved date must match the real `nextDate` exactly, and the 2Y gap (genuinely absent on that fixture) must still fire correctly. A separate case confirms the DD/MM/YYYY fallback still works. 20/20 tests passing (was 15 before this fix).
- **Why this matters beyond the one bug:** this file was built and "fully tested" (15/15 tests) in a prior session without ever being checked against a real asset's actual data shape — the tests were all self-consistent with the assumption baked into the parser, so they couldn't catch the parser's own wrong assumption. Manual testing against a real, messy asset is what caught it, not additional unit tests written from the same mental model. Worth remembering next time a build session's tests all pass cleanly on the first try.
- **Priority:** 🔴 → ✅ Fixed and verified same session, before any further Firestore/UI work builds on top of it.

---

### 4.40 Brain 6 wired into Fly-Forward — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `flyForward.js` + `index.html`)
- **Context:** Follow-on to 4.39 — wires the already-built, already-tested `maintenanceCal.js` into the live Fly-Forward calculation path. `flyForward.js` and `index.html` both touched this session.
- **`flyForward.js` change — grounding-aware accrual, backward compatible.** `monthlyAccrual()` takes a new `availability` parameter (default `1`, i.e. fully flying) and multiplies it into the three utilisation-basis pots (`per_FH`/`per_FC`/`per_APU_hr`); `per_month` (calendar pots) is deliberately untouched, so those keep accruing through a grounding exactly as designed. Verified byte-for-byte identical output against the pre-change file whenever no grounding vector is supplied, or an all-1.0 one is (5/5 Node regression tests, `test-flyForward-regression.js`).
- **Real refactor, not just a multiply: EN-LP's engine-cycle clock.** The old `currentFC = baseFC + fcPerMonth*(m-resetMonth)` closed-form can't respect a month-by-month grounding fraction — a 6-week check spans two unevenly-grounded calendar months, not a uniform multiplier. Replaced with an incremental accumulator (`fcAccum`/`accumMonth`) that advances by exactly one month's `fcPerMonth × availability` per loop iteration. Confirmed mathematically identical to the old formula when ungrounded, and confirmed a grounded month correctly delays (never advances) the next EN-LP shop-visit event. The old `baseFC`/`resetMonth` variables became dead code once this landed (the FC clock no longer needs re-anchoring at harvest — only each part's own life-remaining resets) and were removed rather than left in as inert/misleading state.
- **`index.html` — two-pass wiring, no feedback loop.** `FlyForward`'s single projection pass split into: **Pass 1** (ungrounded, unchanged existing logic) sources real derived dates for LG-OH/AP-OH/EN-PR/EN-LP only — AF-6Y/AF-12Y grounding comes from `asset.checks` directly, Brain 6 owns C-check derivation itself (3.1) — → **Brain 6** (`buildMaintenanceCalendar`, overrides/seasonalityProfile/costProjections all passed as empty defaults since those Firestore collections are still unbuilt stubs per 4.39) → **Pass 2** (same pots, same ctx, plus Brain 6's `groundingAvailability`) is what's actually rendered. Added the `dataCompleteness` banner (same visual pattern as the existing `missingCodes` banner) directly under it. Added the missing `<script src="calculations/maintenanceCal.js">` tag — the file existed since 4.39 but was never actually loaded by the app until now.
- **Tested at three levels, not just the two engines in isolation:** the two engines' own regression suites (20 tests total, unchanged from 4.39/this session's `flyForward.js` work) PLUS a new integration test (`test-flyforward-wiring-integration.js`, 7/7 passing) that re-runs the actual orchestration logic added to `index.html` — not a re-implementation of it — against a realistic asset/lease/reserve fixture, confirming the assembly doesn't throw, Brain 6's calendar and gap-detection both fire correctly, and — the actual point of this session — grounding measurably reduces an accrued reserve balance versus an all-flying comparison run.
- **Not yet done:** the Brain 6 calendar itself isn't rendered anywhere (only its `groundingAvailability` and `dataCompleteness` are consumed) — a visual maintenance-calendar view is future work, not blocking. `scheduledEvents`/`seasonalityProfile`/`shopVisitProjections` remain unbuilt — once they exist, only the input-assembly layer in `index.html` needs to start reading them; neither `maintenanceCal.js` nor `flyForward.js` need to change again.
- **Priority:** ✅ Wired and tested — Layer 2's Brain 6 thread is functionally complete pending those three Firestore collections.

### 4.39 Brain 6 (Maintenance Calendar Engine) — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `maintenanceCal.js` + Node E2E tests)
- **Context:** Build session opening directly against `brain6-build-handoff.md` and 4.38 below (no re-discovery). `calculations/maintenanceCal.js` shipped as a pure function — no UI, no Firebase, no imports from index.html. Not yet wired into `index.html`/Fly-Forward — that's the next session (see below).
- **Two build-time questions resolved with Alan, both narrowing the locked spec rather than reopening it:**
  1. **Grounding math is fractional, not whole-month.** A grounding window is converted to a per-month `availability` fraction (grounded days ÷ days in that month) rather than zeroing any month it touches. Required to make the mandated Section 10 golden fixture (46wk×70FC + 6wk×0 = 3,220 FC) actually hold — whole-month zeroing overstates grounding and fails the fixture (~3,030 FC). Confirmed against the reality that exact in/out dates usually won't be available (utilisation reports just show reduced flying) — the same day-proportional math runs against either a **derived default window** (due-date + default/edited duration) or a **real/airline-stated window**, when a client does have exact dates. Seasonality (off-season placement) doesn't change this math — it only affects where a default due-date gets *suggested*, per 3.4.
  2. **Cost join tolerance = ±3 months**, nearest-`projectedDate` match by `code`, preferring the most recently `calculatedAt` doc on a tie. `shopVisitProjections` has no `dueCycle` field (out of scope to add one), so the 3.1 identity join is approximated as date-proximity rather than an exact key match on the cost side.
- **Also built, not just the C-check dates:** the 2Y-check `dataCompleteness` gap (build-start item, high severity, no reserve-pot fallback); 6Y/12Y concurrency merge (longest-event-wins, generalised beyond just that one pair); seasonality suggest-only placement (never auto-moves, always the hard due-limiter by default); `source` flag stickiness (`airline-stated` survives recompute, wins outright).
- **Found while reading the code before writing any:** `shopVisitProjections`, `scheduledEvents`, and `seasonalityProfile` are **all still unbuilt stubs** — zero references anywhere in `index.html`. Fly-Forward computes its projections in memory and discards them; nothing is persisted today. So `maintenanceCal.js` takes cost projections and overrides as plain **input parameters**, not as something it reads from Firestore itself — matches the "engines don't care how many docs it came from" principle already established for `reserves`/`leases` (Section 5 design rationale). The input-assembly layer (next session) is what will actually read these collections once they exist.
- **Confirmed no circular dependency between Brain 6 and Brain 3**, despite first appearances: grounding is C-check-only, and C-check dates come from `asset.checks`, never from Brain 3. APU/EN-LP/EN-PR/LG-OH dates DO come from Brain 3 (Section 5 build note — Brain 6 reads, doesn't recalculate), but those events never ground. So wiring is two clean passes — an ungrounded Fly-Forward pass supplies those dates to Brain 6, Brain 6 returns the full calendar + `groundingAvailability`, a second Fly-Forward pass consumes it for accrual — not a feedback loop.
- **Tested:** 15/15 Node E2E tests pass, including the mandated Section 10 golden fixture as the first test. Also covers 6Y/12Y coincidence merging, override/airline-stated stickiness across a simulated recompute, seasonality suggest-vs-default behaviour, the 2Y gap firing correctly, and the cost-join 0..n cardinality (2Y→0 docs, 6Y→1, out-of-tolerance doc correctly excluded).
- **Not yet done (next session):** wiring `maintenanceCal.js` into `index.html`'s Fly-Forward assembly (the two-pass flow above), and building the `dataCompleteness` UI banner (same visual pattern as Fly-Forward's `missingCodes` banner). `index.html` was not touched this session, so no Babel transform check was needed.
- **Priority:** ✅ Engine built and tested — ready for the Fly-Forward wiring session.

---

### 4.38 Brain 6 (Maintenance Calendar Engine) — ✅ SCOPED (Opus session, July 2026, NO CODE CHANGED)
- **What it is:** A pure function (Brain/Body separation). Inputs: asset checks + reserve pot `triggerInterval`s + user overrides + seasonality profile. Output: a projected maintenance calendar with grounding months feeding Brain 3's `basisQuantity`. Financial-projection input, not a maintenance-tracking tool — precision is deliberately loose since real utilisation reports self-correct drift.
- **Key decisions:**
  - **Derivation over manual entry** — checks/components computed forward from data Brains 1/2 and Lease/Reserve setup already hold, not a fresh manual calendar.
  - **Downtime asymmetry** — only C-Checks (2/6/12Y) carry real grounding downtime (defaults: 2/4/8 weeks, editable). Engine/LG/APU events carry negligible downtime, calendar-only for cost/visibility.
  - **Concurrency: longest event wins, not additive** — a 6Y always coincides with a 12Y (never two independent live 6Y occurrences in one lease horizon), modelled as one combined grounding event; the 12Y's duration absorbs the 6Y's.
  - **`scheduledEvents` ↔ `shopVisitProjections` linkage: read-time join by identity (`code + due-cycle`), not a stored foreign key** — a scalar FK can't survive `shopVisitProjections`' append-only recompute (immediately stale) or its 0..n cardinality (2Y check → 0 cost docs, engine off-wing → 2, EN-PR + EN-LP). `scheduledEvents` owns date/duration/downtime/concurrency; `shopVisitProjections` stays exactly as-is, owns cost only.
  - **Firestore decision: reuse, don't add** — repurpose the existing never-built `scheduledEvents` stub into an override/duration-config layer (drop `estimatedCost`, add a `source` flag: `derived`/`seasonality`/`airline-stated`); reuse `seasonalityProfile` unchanged; `maintenanceCal.js` itself is recompute-on-read, writes nothing derived.
  - **Seasonality: flag-and-accept for check placement, never auto-move** — utilisation-shaping via `monthlyWeightings` stays automatic (existing Brain 3 behaviour); check-*placement* is a suggestion the user accepts or ignores, default position is always the hard due-limiter. An `airline-stated` date is sticky and overrides both derived and seasonality guesses outright.
  - **2Y-check date gap flagged as a build-start item** — the 2Y check has no reserve pot, so its only date source is the free-form `asset.checks` array (see 4.6); if missing/misnamed, Brain 6 silently has no 2Y grounding. Build must surface this as a visible `dataCompleteness` gap, same pattern as Fly-Forward's unconfirmed-pot gaps.
  - **Condition-triggered event visualization (APU 5,000–7,000hr, EN-LP stack-sim) — concept settled, visual form deferred.** A windowed (not single-date) view with dual-labelled hour/date x-axis, balance + cost-band curves, crossover = Brain 4's risk-peak window. Brain 6's only job here is supplying window bounds; the display itself is a Brain 4/5 consumer, built later against a real asset's numbers.
- **Explicitly out of scope for the build:** seasonality profile *generation*/tier-2 pattern detection (separate future work — Brain 6 only consumes a profile); EN-PR derate/ratio mechanism (4.24, separate Opus session, does not block Brain 6); asset risk dashboard and fleet snapshot/aggregation (separate future sessions).
- **First test:** the existing Section 10 golden fixture (46wk×70FC + 6 grounded wk×0 = 3,220 FC vs. 3,640 flat-rate overstatement) as the first Node.js end-to-end test for `maintenanceCal.js`, before touching Firestore.
- **Priority:** ✅ Scoped — ready for a Sonnet build session. See `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 5 (schema) and Section 17 (roadmap) for the model-level sync.

### 4.70 Engine + Airframe Cover Art — Background Color Fix (Round 2) + Edge Artifact Cleanup — ✅ DONE (July 2026)
- **Problem found:** Testing surfaced that 4 of the 8 engine covers (LEAP-1B, CF34, CF6, PW1100G) and all 7 airframe covers had their background color-matched against the wrong reference. A prior session's pixel-shift correction (see Section 0a / the original engine-cover-art entry) targeted `#102A43` — the website header/LinkedIn banner navy — when the actual context these images render into is the tech spec cover's `.hero` div, which is CSS-set to `#111827` (confirmed directly from `techSpecBuilder.js` line 36, not assumed). The other 4 engine covers (CFM56-5B, CFM56-7B, LEAP-1A, V2500-A5) were already correctly matched to `#111827` from an earlier, different generation batch — this was flagged as a fresh mismatch to fix, not caught on first pass because the two navy values are visually similar and the wrong reference was assumed correct without checking the actual rendering CSS. Alan's own visual review ("1A is correct, 1B is off") turned out to be numerically backwards versus the true target — worth remembering: eyeballing two navy tones side by side is not a reliable substitute for sampling both the asset and the actual destination CSS.
- **Second problem found during the same pass:** a separate, pre-existing edge artifact — a thin (2–27px) strip of near-white pixels on one edge of most of the 15 covers — left over from the original AI generation, present before any color correction and not something the pixel-shift script introduced. More visible after the background correction fixed the surrounding color, not caused by it.
- **Fix applied:** re-ran the uniform pixel-shift correction (same method as the original engine-cover session — compute delta between each file's sampled dominant background and the target hex, apply uniformly, leaving linework/anti-aliasing untouched) targeting `#111827` on the 11 affected files (LEAP-1B, CF34, CF6, PW1100G, A319, A320, A321, A330, B737, B737 MAX, B787). Separately, detected and cropped the edge-artifact strip (per-file, per-edge, with a small safety margin) and refilled with solid `#111827` on all 15 covers, including the 4 that already had the correct background. Verified numerically post-fix: all 15 sample to exactly `(17,24,39)` and zero near-white pixels remain within 3px of any edge.
- **Not changed:** CFM56-5B, CFM56-7B, LEAP-1A, V2500-A5 backgrounds — these were already correct and untouched other than the edge-artifact crop.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE — all 15 files re-delivered to Alan for re-upload into Admin → Settings.

### 4.72 Mobile Viewer account logging out spuriously — flagged by Alan, not yet fixed (July 2026)
- **Symptom:** Alan's Viewer-level test account on his phone signed itself out several times over a weekend with no role change and no code change involved.
- **Root cause (diagnosed, not yet confirmed against logs):** the 45-second/on-focus force-refresh check added in 4.64 (`App.jsx`'s `checkRole`) treats **any** error from `getIdTokenResult(true)` as a genuinely revoked session and signs out:
  ```js
  }catch(e){
    notify("Your session is no longer valid — please sign in again.","error");
    await window._auth.signOut().catch(()=>{});
  }
  ```
  On mobile specifically, backgrounding the browser (phone lock) and later returning to it fires the `onFocus` listener right as the device is reconnecting to WiFi/cellular — a classic moment for a transient `auth/network-request-failed`, which this code currently treats identically to an actually-revoked session (`auth/id-token-revoked`, `auth/user-token-expired`, `auth/user-disabled`, `auth/user-not-found`).
- **4.64 itself is not being reverted** — the underlying fix (role changes taking effect promptly) is correct and stays. This item is about narrowing the `catch` block's behaviour, not removing the mechanism.
- **Fix for next build session:** only sign out on error codes that actually mean the session is invalid; on `auth/network-request-failed` (and any other transient/unknown error), skip silently and let the next 45-second tick or focus event retry, rather than signing out on the first hiccup.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — not data-loss risk, but a real annoyance for anyone using Viewer on mobile day-to-day (exactly the audience Viewer role is meant for). Small, contained fix — good candidate for a quick session.

---

### 4.73 Deleted lease's reserve pot data reappears after re-parsing a new lease — flagged by Alan, not yet fixed (July 2026)
- **Symptom:** on a test asset, Alan deleted an existing lease, parsed a new lease document, confirmed the parse was correct, but the pot-entry screen that followed showed the **old, deleted** lease's accrual rates and opening balances — not the newly parsed figures.
- **Root cause (hypothesis, not yet confirmed against code):** per the schema (Section 5), `reserves/` is a separate collection from `leases/` — one doc per pot, keyed by pot code (`AF-6Y`, `EN-LP-1`, etc.), not nested under or keyed by a lease ID. `leases/` is its own append-only collection; the asset doc just holds a `currentLeaseId` pointer.
  - **"Delete This Lease" likely only removes the `leases/` doc and clears `currentLeaseId` — it probably does not cascade-delete the corresponding `reserves/*` pot docs.** Those pot docs have no natural tie back to a specific lease to delete-by.
  - The pot-entry step of the wizard likely **pre-fills each row from any existing `reserves/*` doc it finds** (sensible-looking UX for the common case of re-confirming an unchanged pot) — but if the old pot docs were never actually deleted, this pre-fill silently wins over the freshly parsed values, with no visible indication to the user that stale data is being shown rather than the new parse.
- **Not yet reproduced/confirmed in code** — this is a diagnosis from the schema design, not a traced bug. Needs a real repro pass (delete lease → check Firestore directly for surviving `reserves/*` docs → re-parse → check what the pot screen actually prefills from) before fixing.
- **Fix shape, once confirmed:** either (a) make "Delete This Lease" cascade-delete the asset's `reserves/*` docs too, or (b) make the post-parse pot-entry screen prefer freshly-parsed values over any existing `reserves/*` doc, or (c) both — plus some visible signal in the wizard when a pot row is showing carried-over data vs. fresh parse data, so this doesn't silently repeat for any asset going through a lease renewal/replacement.
- **Priority:** 🔴 HIGH — this is a real-data-correctness bug on the actual product (not the demo assets), and lease renewal/replacement is a normal, expected workflow, not an edge case. Should be the first thing looked at in the next build session, before anything else queued.

### 4.74 Utilisation upload save fails on undefined `apuFC` — inconsistently, cause not yet identified (July 2026)
- **Symptom:** uploading a utilisation report missing APU flight-cycle data threw `Function addDoc() called with invalid data. Unsupported field value: undefined (found in field data.apuFC in document utilisation/...)` — a hard save failure, not a graceful gap-handling message.
- **Immediate trigger (demo-side, already fixed):** the synthetic demo utilisation reports for this test session were missing an APU FC/CSN field entirely (Description + LLP fields existed for engines, but the APU section only had Model/S/N/TSN). That gap has been corrected in the demo documents themselves — not a product bug on its own.
- **But the real open question is why this was inconsistent.** Asset 1's three utilisation reports had the exact same missing-APU-FC structure (same generator, same template, before the fix) and saved without error. Asset 2's did not — same gap, different outcome, on what Alan confirms was the first utilisation upload for that asset (ruling out my initial "same-month merge into an existing record" theory — there was no existing record to merge into).
- **Honestly: root cause not identified.** Candidate explanations, none confirmed:
  1. The AI extraction step itself may not be fully deterministic — structurally identical documents could still produce different extracted field sets depending on the specific numbers/wording, with `apuFC` inferred/defaulted on one and left genuinely absent on the other.
  2. Some difference in upload order or asset state between the two test assets that isn't visible from the outside (e.g., whether other documents/fields had already been entered for that asset before the utilisation upload).
  3. An inconsistency in the save code itself between two different code paths (e.g., object-spread merge vs. conditional-key-set create) that isn't actually gated on "is this a merge" the way the UI copy suggests.
- **What's needed to actually resolve this:** a real trace against the live save function (likely in `calculations/utilisation.js` or wherever `addDoc()` is called for the `utilisation` collection) — not further guessing. Regardless of root cause, the deeper fix is the same: **the save path should never let an optional/missing field reach Firestore as an explicit `undefined`** — omit the key, or write `null`, rather than letting `addDoc()` throw. This is defensive-coding debt independent of whatever the specific trigger turns out to be.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM-HIGH — currently only reproduced with synthetic/incomplete source documents, but real-world utilisation reports from real operators are exactly as inconsistent in what fields they include, so this will recur with real data. Worth pairing with 4.73 in the same session, since both are Firestore-write robustness issues surfaced by this same test pass.

### 4.75 EN-LP deterministic catalogue-rate check (4.27) firing on EN-PR pots — wrong pot, wrong unit — ✅ FIXED (July 31 2026, `pots.js`)
- **Symptom:** setting up the **EN-PR-1** pot (Engine #1 Performance Restoration, $69/FH accrual) triggered: *"Accrual rate of $69/FH is implausibly low compared to catalogue blended LLP rate of $295.80/FC for CFM engines; transcription may have missed a digit or confused per_FH with per_FC basis."*
- **This check should never have fired here.** Per 4.27's own spec, the deterministic catalogue check is built to run **"before the AI call for any `EN-LP` pot"** — comparing the entered rate against `llpCatalogue.js`'s blended $/FC figure for that engine family. EN-PR (Performance Restoration) is a completely different pot from EN-LP (LLP Replacement): different real-world cost driver, different accrual basis (per FH, not per FC), and no defined relationship to the LLP catalogue price at all. The real EN-PR reference figures are FH-based (`VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` §4: CFM56-5B $1.2–1.6M/10,000 FH, V2500-A5 $1.4–1.8M/6,000 FH; a separate real test case gives $165/FH at a 10% derate baseline) — none of which is $295.80/FC.
- **Root cause, actually confirmed this session (previous guesses in this item were on the right track but not exact):** the deterministic check itself was already correctly gated (`pot.code.startsWith('EN-LP')`, exact match, never firing for EN-PR). The real bug was one level up, in `validatePotWithAI`'s AI-context builder — it branched only on `pot.potCategory === 'engine'`, true for both EN-PR and EN-LP, and unconditionally handed the AI the EN-LP $/FC catalogue rate as "the known reference" regardless of which pot was actually being validated. Claude was then correctly flagging a unit mismatch against a number it was never supposed to be compared to — the check was doing exactly what it was told, it was just told the wrong reference figure.
- **Fix:** the context branch now checks the actual pot code. EN-LP pots get the $/FC catalogue context as before. EN-PR pots get their own real reference — the lump-sum restoration-cost band from `EN_PR_FAMILY_DEFAULTS` — plus an explicit instruction not to compare a per-FH rate against any $/FC figure.
- **Validated:** Babel JSX transform clean, `node --check` clean, diff confirmed isolated to the context-builder branch — no change to the already-correct deterministic check.
- **Priority:** ✅ Fixed and shipped.

### 4.79 Date of Manufacture — should auto-insert "/" while typing MM/YYYY — ✅ FIXED (July 31 2026, `AssetTabs.jsx`)
- **Symptom:** the Date of Manufacture field expects MM/YYYY but doesn't auto-insert the "/" separator as the user types — has to be typed manually.
- **Record corrected this session:** earlier reports (below) described this as reproducing on both "the Specs tab" and "the Asset Details screen." Direct inspection of `PhotosAndSpecs.jsx` this session found no manual Date of Manufacture input there at all — the Specs tab's DOM handling is entirely read-only, appearing only inside the AI-extraction review table before "Apply." There is exactly one manual `dom` input in the whole app, in `AssetTabs.jsx`'s Overview tab `Field` component — the two "Specs tab" reports below were most likely two people looking at the same underlying Overview-tab field.
- **Fix:** added `onInput` masking to the `Field` component's `mmyyyy` type — strips non-digits, caps at 6, inserts "/" after the second digit, same pattern as a card-expiry-date input. Applied directly against the uncontrolled DOM input (no React state change needed), so it works identically whether typed or pasted.
- **Bonus fix, same session, same file family:** `PhotosAndSpecs.jsx`'s `SpecsTab` has its own separate, editable Check History block (distinct from the Overview tab's read-only display) with the identical 4.80 ordering bug. Fixed there too, with one extra wrinkle handled: that view edits/deletes checks by array index, so the fix sorts a copy of the array while keeping each entry's *original* index alongside it for every callback — sorting must never shift which record a delete/edit button actually targets.
- **Validated:** Babel JSX transform clean on both `AssetTabs.jsx` and `PhotosAndSpecs.jsx`; diffs confirmed isolated to the intended change in each file.
- **Priority:** ✅ Fixed and shipped — both the Overview tab input and the Specs tab's Check History editor.

### 4.80 2 Year Check ordering — appears below 12 Year Check instead of above 6 Year Check — ✅ FIXED (July 31 2026, `AssetTabs.jsx` + `PhotosAndSpecs.jsx`)
- **Symptom:** adding a 2 Year Check to Check History places it below the 12 Year Check in the displayed order, when it should appear above the 6 Year Check (i.e. chronological/interval order: 2Y, 6Y, 12Y — shortest interval first).
- **Root cause, confirmed this session:** neither guess in the original item was quite right — Check History's render was a plain `.map()` over the raw array with **no sort at all**, not a string/alphabetical sort. Genuinely pure insertion order.
- **Fix:** new `parseCheckInterval()` helper pulls the leading number out of the check name (e.g. "2 Year Check" → 2); anything that doesn't parse sorts to the end rather than breaking on `NaN`. Applied in the Overview tab's read-only display (`AssetTabs.jsx` — confirmed nothing else in that file indexes into the array by position, so sorting a copy for display only is safe) and in `PhotosAndSpecs.jsx`'s separate editable Check History block (see 4.79's bonus-fix note for the index-safety handling that view needed).
- **Priority:** ✅ Fixed and shipped in both locations.

### 4.82 Knowledge Base — ✅ BUILT (Firestore layer, three-tier hierarchy, Admin UI, LLP Catalogue, July 2026)
Built per the locked design in `knowledge-base-scoping-handoff.md` (Opus scoping session) — one centralised, editable place for house-view forecasting assumptions that previously lived as scattered hardcoded constants across `pots.js`, `FIXED_RESERVE_POT_DEFS`, and `maintenanceCal.js`.

- **Firestore (`db.js`):** new `knowledgeBase/{companyId}` doc (Forecasting Defaults — check cost bands, EN-PR bands per engine family, LLP escalation %/yr per family, blended LLP $/FC rate per family, default outflow escalation %) plus an `llpCatalogue` subcollection (one doc per part number: `unitPrice`, `engineFamily`, `catalogueYear`). `companyId` uses the literal string `"default"` as the doc id pre-multi-tenancy — Firestore doc IDs can't literally be `null`, unlike the `companyId:null` *field* convention every other collection already uses; migrates the same day real multi-tenancy lands.
- **`firestore.rules`:** new `knowledgeBase/{companyId}` + `llpCatalogue` subcollection rules, Admin/Editor write, any-signed-in-user read (deliberately NOT Data Entry — this is house-view assumption data, a different category from raw per-asset input).
- **New `src/lib/knowledgeBase.js`:** the three-tier hierarchy (per-asset override → Knowledge Base company default → hardcoded code fallback) lives here as the single source of truth for tier 2/3 resolution — `getCheckCostBand()`, `getEnPrBand()`, `getOutflowEscalationPct()`, `getCheckDurationDefaults()`. Also `bootstrapKnowledgeBaseGlobals()`, called once from `App.jsx` alongside `loadAssets()`, which populates two window globals that Brain 3 (`flyForward.js`) and `pots.js` were **already written to read defensively** — `window.LLP_CATALOGUE_PRICES` and `window.lookupLLPCataloguePrice()` existed as dormant hooks in the codebase with nothing populating them before this session.
- **Brain 6 (`maintenanceCal.js`) needed zero code changes** — it already accepted a `durationDefaults` input parameter, just nothing had ever populated it. `flyForwardHelpers.js` now threads `getCheckDurationDefaults()` into both call paths (single-asset `buildFlyForwardProjection`, and the fleet-wide path via a small wrapper around `brains.buildMaintenanceCalendar` so `fleetExposure.js` — a deliberately window/KB-lookup-free pure Brain module — didn't need touching).
- **`pots.js`:** `buildRealEnginePotDefs()` and `buildPotFromDef()` now resolve EN-PR bands and non-engine check cost bands through the KB tier instead of bare hardcoded literals; `buildPotDefsForActivation()` resolves the default outflow escalation the same way.
- **`LeaseWizard.jsx`:** new-pot pre-fill (cost bands, escalation) now goes through the same KB tier for non-engine pots; an already-saved pot's real values are untouched, per the "new pots only, never retroactive" design decision.
- **Admin UI (`AdminView.jsx`):** new "Knowledge Base" tab — Forecasting Defaults form (editable cost bands, EN-PR bands, LLP escalation/blended rate, check durations, outflow escalation) and LLP Catalogue (manual entry, Excel upload, PDF upload — see 4.83/4.84 for the real-world fixes both needed), scanned live off the fleet's own LLP sheets per engine family.
- **`FlyForward.jsx`:** new read-only "Assumptions" panel, visible to every role including Viewer, showing the KB values driving the current projection — per the scoping doc's UI placement spec.
- **Deliberately not built this session:** cost pooling/benchmarking across clients ("Glassdoor model" — parked, revisit at 10+ clients), AI-driven cost-trend analysis (years of actuals away), Shop Visit cost-actuals aggregation view (separate scope once enough data exists).
- **Priority:** ✅ Built — see 4.83/4.84 for real bugs found and fixed during Alan's first live test pass with actual fleet data.

### 4.83 LLP Catalogue Excel upload — three real bugs found and fixed during first live test (July 2026)
Alan's first real-data test (`Engine_LLP_Escalation_Model.xlsx`) against 4.82's initial build reported "none of the PNs matched" for both engine families. Investigation (reproduced locally against the actual file, not guessed at) found three separate, real bugs — not a data-formatting mismatch, which was the first suspect:

1. **Only ever read the workbook's first sheet**, regardless of which family tab was selected in the app. The real file has three sheets (`CFM`/`IAE`/`V2500`) — uploading on the V2500 tab silently parsed CFM data and compared it against V2500 part numbers, guaranteeing zero matches. **Fix:** scan every sheet in the workbook; matching is inherently safe since it's filtered against the current family's own fleet part numbers regardless of source sheet.
2. **Picked the first "Price" column found, not the most recent.** The real file has one price column per year (2023–2026); the old exact-match `^price$` regex only recognised a bare "Price" header, which happened to be the oldest (2023) column — so even where matching *did* work, it was silently pulling stale pricing. **Fix:** broadened the header match to any column containing "price" (catches "2023 Price", "2025 Price", etc.) and takes the rightmost such column, assumed most recent year.
3. **The `V2500` sheet's headers (`"2026 Material"`, `"2025 Price"`) matched neither the old part-number nor price keyword list at all** — that sheet parsed zero rows. **Fix:** added "material" as an alternate part-number header keyword.
4. **Two separate React state-timing bugs found on top of the parsing fixes**, both in `AdminView.jsx`'s `handleUpload`:
   - Match-counting logic (`matched`/`unmatchedFleetPns`) was mutated as a side effect **inside** a `setRows(prev => ...)` state-updater callback, then read immediately after — React doesn't guarantee that ordering, so the count was reading stale/initial values regardless of how many real matches occurred inside the updater. Confirmed via a genuinely inconsistent log (an "unmatched" list logged as empty despite the app reporting zero matches, which is only possible if the counting logic never ran against real data). **Fix:** compute matches directly against the `rows` state already in scope, not as a side effect buried in the updater.
   - `load()` (the effect that scans the fleet and rebuilds the shopping-list table) depended on the `assets` prop directly. `App.jsx` recomputes `liveAssets = assets.filter(...)` inline on every render — a brand-new array reference every time, including every time `notify()` fires a toast (itself a state update in `App`). This gave `load` a new identity on every such re-render, re-firing the effect and silently wiping out just-matched-but-unsaved catalogue prices moments after they appeared — the "prices flash up then disappear" symptom. **Fix:** `assets` is now read via a ref inside `load`, so `load`'s own identity only changes when the `family` tab changes, not on every unrelated parent re-render.
- **Verified against the real file after all four fixes:** CFM 18 unique parts (was 18, but now correctly 2026 pricing not 2023), IAE 24, V2500 25 (was 0) — 43 unique part numbers total, up from 18. Fan Disk (`338-001-504-0`) now resolves to $462,100 (2026) instead of the previously-matched-but-stale $374,600 (2023).
- **One real (non-code) data discrepancy surfaced along the way, not a bug:** the fleet's stored Booster Spool part number (`338-001-905-0`) doesn't match this catalogue file's part number for the same component (`338-001-906-0`) — flagged to Alan to check which is correct; no code fix resolves a genuine data mismatch between two sources.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — confirmed working end-to-end by Alan against real fleet data, matches populating and persisting correctly through to Save.

---

### 4.84 LLP Catalogue PDF upload — redesigned from whole-document extraction to targeted lookup (July 2026)
The original 4.82 build sent the entire uploaded PDF to `/api/extract` as a base64 document block (same pattern as `extractLLPSheet`/`extractAvionicsLRU` in `extraction.js`), asking Claude to extract every part number and price in one call. This broke immediately against Alan's first real test — a genuine ~100+ page full CFM spare parts catalogue (not the ~50-60-part fleet-specific list the Knowledge Base design assumes) — returning a 504 Gateway Timeout from `/api/extract`.

- **Why raising the function timeout alone wouldn't have fixed it:** even given more time, a full manufacturer catalogue's worth of structured JSON rows (thousands) doesn't fit in a single model response regardless of how long the call is allowed to run. The real problem was architectural, not a parameter.
- **Redesign — lookup, not extraction:** since the fleet only ever needs prices for its own known ~50-60 part numbers (never the full manufacturer catalogue — see 4.82's scoping doc reference), the fix turns this into a targeted lookup instead of a full-document extraction:
  1. Extract raw text per page **client-side** via `extractPdfPageTexts()` (`extraction.js`) — the same function LeaseWizard's Confidential Extract already uses. No AI call, no cost, scales to any page count.
  2. Plain string-search that text for each of the fleet's known target part numbers (preferring an occurrence with a price-looking number nearby over a bare cross-reference/index mention of the same part number).
  3. Send only the matched short text snippets — proportional to the number of fleet part numbers, not to the catalogue's length — to `/api/extract` as plain text (no document/base64 block at all now).
- **Cost and latency now scale with the fleet's own part count, not the source document's size** — a 5-page fleet-specific PDF and a 500-page full manufacturer catalogue cost and take roughly the same amount to process, since only the same ~50-60 lookups happen either way.
- **`parsePdfCatalogueFile(file, targetPartNumbers)` signature changed** to accept the fleet's current part-number list (from `AdminView.jsx`'s `rows` state) — the caller no longer just passes the file.
- **Verified working end-to-end by Alan against the real 100+ page catalogue** after the redesign.
- **Known limitations, not yet hit in practice:** plain substring matching means a part number formatted differently in the PDF than in the fleet's stored data (extra whitespace, different dash character) won't match — same class of risk as the Excel path's matching. The snippet window (±60/120 characters around each match) assumes the price sits reasonably close to the part number once PDF text extraction flattens the page layout; a catalogue whose columns end up far apart in the flattened text could miss a price that's genuinely on the same visual row.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — confirmed working against a real full-length manufacturer catalogue, which was the actual, common real-world case this needed to handle.

### 4.85 Route Matcher clash detection + pandemic scenario slider + fleet Calendar tab — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `fleetExposure.js` + `routeMatcher.js` + `flyForwardHelpers.js` + `PortfolioView.jsx` + `FlyForward.jsx` + `Scenarios.jsx` + `App.jsx`)

Closes three of the four items `layer3-scenarios-build-handoff.md` §9's build sequence had left after Route Matcher itself: fleet-wide clash detection (previously blocked on Route Matcher existing), the pandemic-scenario preset (previously scoped as a chat-box shortcut), and the fleet Calendar tab (previously nav-shell-only, "Coming soon"). The fourth item, the fleet-wide chat box, is **killed this session** (Alan) — no replacement; fleet-wide "what if" coverage is now Route Matcher + the pandemic slider only, nothing more.

**Route Matcher clash detection** — a candidate asset's ROUTE-profile C-Check windows (`groundingStart`/`groundingEnd`, real duration, from `maintenanceCal.js`) are now checked against every OTHER asset's own BASE-case C-Check windows for genuine date overlap, not just same-month clustering (confirmed design: "overlapping date windows accounting for check duration," not the coarser same-month bar). `routeMatcher.js`'s `runProjection` now also returns `groundingWindows`; new `windowsOverlap()`/`detectClashes()` helpers; `compareAsset`/`matchRouteToFleet` accept an optional `fleetMaintenanceEvents` param (backward compatible — omit it and `clashes` is simply always empty, not an error). `RouteMatcherView` shows a red clash-count badge on each ranked row plus a full clash list (which asset, which codes, which windows) in the expanded detail.

**Pandemic scenario slider** — new control on the fleet Scenarios page, alongside Route Matcher (design confirmed: slider, not a fixed button — 1–12 months, adjustable). Grounds every asset from today for N months, combined with each asset's own real maintenance grounding via `Math.min` per month (no stacking) — same "longest/most-grounded wins" rule Brain 6 already applies to overlapping C-Checks, just applied at the availability-vector level via new `applyPandemicGrounding()` in `fleetExposure.js`. `buildAssetAtoms`/`buildFleetExposure` both take an optional `pandemicGroundingMonths` (default 0, fully backward compatible). New `PandemicScenarioView` component runs base (0 months) and scenario (N months) `buildFleetExposureData` calls side by side, shows the High-case gap delta. Non-destructive, same as everything else in Scenarios — nothing writes to Firestore.

**Fleet Calendar tab** — the "Calendar" nav-bar entry (fleet level) had been a literal "Coming soon" placeholder since the four-layer nav was built (4.65). Alan's decision: reuse the asset-level calendar view rather than build something new. New shared `buildFleetMaintenanceEvents()` in `fleetExposure.js` runs pass-1 + Brain 6 per asset (no pass-2 — no financial projection needed, just Brain 6's scheduling output) and returns each asset's own events with duration windows; this is the SAME building block clash detection uses for the fleet's base-case side, so there's no duplicate Brain 6 orchestration for the two features. New `FleetCalendarView` component flattens every asset's events (tagged with MSN) and feeds them straight into the existing `MaintenanceCalendarGrid` (exported from `FlyForward.jsx` for this reuse) — same month-grid, same hover tooltips, just multi-asset now (tooltip updated to show MSN per event when present). Deliberately no cost figures on this tab — that's what Financials (Fleet Exposure) is for; this tab answers "what's happening when," not "what will it cost."

**Known duplication, flagged not fixed (same tradeoff `fleetExposure.js`'s own header already documents for `buildAssetAtoms` vs. the retired `index.html` orchestration copy):** `buildFleetMaintenanceEvents`'s pass-1/Brain-6 logic repeats roughly half of `buildAssetAtoms` rather than sharing it, because it deliberately stops before pass-2 and needs per-event duration fields (`groundingStart`/`groundingEnd`) that `buildAssetAtoms`'s financial atoms don't carry. Don't consolidate without a fresh look at both call sites.

**Validation performed this session:** every touched file syntax-checked clean (`node --check`); a full production `vite build` (`APP_SURFACE=app`) compiled with zero errors across all 56 modules; the two genuinely new pure-logic pieces (`applyPandemicGrounding`'s availability merge, `windowsOverlap`/`detectClashes`'s overlap logic) were unit-tested directly against synthetic fixtures and both produced correct results.

**Not yet validated — flagged, not done:** a true end-to-end run through Brain 3/6 against real fixture/live data (no full asset/lease/pot Node fixture was reconstructed this session, to avoid guessing at shapes rather than using Alan's real data). Before calling this confirmed, run a real Route Matcher query and pandemic scenario against actual assets once deployed — same pattern every other build session here has followed.

**Files touched:** `public/calculations/fleetExposure.js`, `public/calculations/routeMatcher.js`, `src/lib/flyForwardHelpers.js`, `src/components/PortfolioView.jsx`, `src/components/FlyForward.jsx` (export only), `src/components/Scenarios.jsx` (export only), `src/App.jsx`.

**Priority:** ✅ Built, syntax/build-validated. 🟡 Live-test against real data still owed before fully closing out.

### 4.86 Fleet Calendar — leaseless assets now show real landing-gear/LLP dates, not just C-Check dates — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `flyForwardHelpers.js` + `PortfolioView.jsx` + `fleetExposure.js`)

Follow-up to 4.85, same session. Alan flagged that the Calendar tab was still showing 8 assets as "C-Check dates only" despite those assets having real utilisation reports, landing gear next-due dates, and engine LLP remaining-life data on file. Root cause was two bugs upstream of 4.85's own fix, both in `flyForwardHelpers.js`:

1. **`loadFleetExposureBundle` never queried Firestore at all for any asset without `currentLeaseId`** — it short-circuited and returned empty utilisation/reserve data immediately, regardless of what actually existed for that asset. Fixed: every query now always runs; only the lease document itself stays conditional (no ID to fetch it by without one).
2. **`buildFleetExposureEntry` only anchored reserve pots when `lease` was truthy** — but anchoring (`anchorReservePots`) only needs `rate`/`checks`/`asset`, never the lease object. Fixed for the Calendar path (see below) — left unchanged for `buildFleetExposureEntry` itself since Fleet Exposure's own `NO_LEASE` exclusion already makes this moot for Financials.

**The deeper fix — confirmed reserve pot documents were never the right requirement for Calendar/clash-detection in the first place.** Those documents only exist once the Lease/Reserve Setup wizard has been run (which requires a lease), but the actual component data driving engine/APU/landing-gear DATES — `asset.landingGear` next-due dates, `asset.engines[].llps` remaining life — is real Layer-1 tracking data that exists independently of any lease or pot. New `buildCalendarEntry()` in `flyForwardHelpers.js`: when no confirmed pot exists, synthesizes pot **structure** (triggerBasis/triggerInterval/harvestThresholdFC/engineFamily) using the exact same generator the Lease Wizard itself uses to pre-fill its checklist (`pots.js`'s `buildPotDefsForActivation`/`buildPotFromDef`, KB-backed with fixed-engineering-constant fallback, accrualRate always 0 — never a real $ figure), then the existing `anchorReservePots` attaches the REAL due date from the asset's own tracked component data exactly as it does for confirmed pots.

**Two corrections made within the same session, after Alan reviewed the first version live:**

1. **EN-PR and AP-OH are OMITTED for synthetic-pot assets, not estimated.** The first version of this fix estimated both (assuming "a full interval from today" when no real anchor existed) and flagged them as such in the UI. Alan: *"i think we shouldnt estimate engine PR dates, this goes against the app. if no data for this then ignore."* Confirmed the same applies to AP-OH (same underlying problem — no real anchor data anywhere, synthetic or confirmed pot alike). `buildCalendarEntry()`'s synthetic-pot generation now filters out both `EN-PR*` and `AP-OH` codes entirely before building synthetic pots — if there's no real data, it's not shown, not shown-as-a-guess. There is no longer an "estimated" tier at all for the synthetic-pot path: every date shown is real (LG-OH from `asset.landingGear`, EN-LP from the engine's real LLP stack), everything else is simply absent. `FleetCalendarView`'s banner text updated to match — no more "estimate" language.

2. **`DEFAULT_CALENDAR_HORIZON_MONTHS` was 24 — far too short, silently dropping real known dates.** Caught from a live Fleet Overview vs. Calendar mismatch: MSN 1473 and MSN 3056 both have a real landing gear overhaul due Apr 2029 (~33 months out) per the Fleet Overview's own LG columns, but neither appeared in the Calendar at all — no warning, just silently absent. Root cause: `calendarOrCyclesEvents`/`calendarMonthEvents`/`apuHourEvents` (`flyForward.js`) do a **hard cutoff** at `horizonMonths` with no "include the first occurrence past the horizon, then stop" grace that Brain 6's own C-Check generator has — a real, anchored due date beyond the horizon doesn't get flagged beyond-horizon, it just vanishes. 24 months was shorter than LG-OH's own 120-month interval, so this was structurally guaranteed to drop any leaseless asset's LG-OH date that happened to fall more than two years out (confirmed directly: `projectReservePot` returns zero events for a real anchored date 33 months out against a 24-month horizon, but returns the correct date — plus the following 120-months-later occurrence — against a 180-month horizon). Fixed in `fleetExposure.js`: `DEFAULT_CALENDAR_HORIZON_MONTHS` raised from 24 to **180** (15 years) — not tied to any single component's number, just a deliberate margin above LG-OH's 120-month interval (the longest of the flat-pot intervals going through this ungraceful cutoff) so no real anchored date can land exactly on the boundary and vanish again, with margin left over to also show a second future occurrence rather than a single bare data point.

**What's real vs. omitted, final state:**
- AF-6Y/AF-12Y — always real (`asset.checks`), unaffected either way
- LG-OH — real, anchored from `asset.landingGear`'s actual next-due date
- EN-LP — real, anchored from the engine's actual LLP stack/`currentFC` (the "FC remaining" data Alan referenced)
- EN-PR — omitted for synthetic-pot assets (no real "last PR date" tracked anywhere)
- AP-OH — omitted for synthetic-pot assets (same reasoning — `nowOffsetMonths` never populated by any caller, synthetic or confirmed pot alike). Note this is narrower than fixing the underlying app-wide gap — AP-OH is still an unflagged approximation in Fly-Forward/Fleet Exposure for assets *with* confirmed pots; this only stops the Calendar tab from manufacturing an AP-OH date out of nothing when there was never any pot to begin with.

**Critical safeguard, unchanged:** this is Calendar/clash-detection only — `buildCalendarEntry()` is a separate function from `buildFleetExposureEntry()`, not a shared one with a flag. Fleet Exposure (Financials) must never see synthetic pots, since accrualRate is always 0 and a financial projection built off one would show a fabricated balance.

**UI:** `FleetCalendarView` now distinguishes two data-quality tiers: a harder amber warning for `partial` assets (no utilisation rate at all — nothing beyond C-Checks can project), and a lighter informational note for `usedSyntheticPots` assets (no lease/reserve setup on file — LG/LLP dates real, EN-PR/APU simply not shown, no estimate language anywhere).

**Validated:** full `vite build` clean at every step; direct Node functional tests confirmed (a) LG-OH and EN-LP correctly project for a leaseless asset with no confirmed reserve pot, using real fixture data, (b) a real anchored date 33 months out — matching Alan's actual MSN 1473/3056 scenario — now correctly survives the 180-month horizon where it was silently dropped at 24, and (c) EN-PR/AP-OH are both confirmed absent from the output, not present-as-estimates. Two real bugs were caught in the test fixtures themselves along the way (missing `llpCalculator.js` require, then wrong LLP field names — `startFCRem`/`refFC` is the real Brain 2 schema, not `currentRemainingFC`) — worth remembering that a clean-looking test fixture isn't the same as a correct one. Existing clash-detection and pandemic-grounding regression tests re-run clean after every change in this session.

**Files touched:** `src/lib/flyForwardHelpers.js`, `src/components/PortfolioView.jsx`, `public/calculations/fleetExposure.js`.

**Priority:** ✅ Built, syntax/build-validated, functionally tested against realistic fixture data matching Alan's real scenario. Still owed: a real run against Alan's actual leaseless assets (the 8 flagged in his screenshot) to confirm live — see Section 19.

**Record corrected this session.** This item was carried as "planned, not yet built" across two sessions, based on a stale note. Alan pointed out `TailiQ_Security_Review_Summary_v2.docx` already exists in the project files and matches this item's brief closely: system overview, per-control status table, control detail, anticipated questions, and known open items — internal-build framing only, Full platform (`app.tailiq.app`) scope only, exactly as specified below.

**Why this is needed:** the original V1 IT/Security Gate (Section 3a, `TECH_DEBT.md`/`VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md`) covered Layer 1 only and was written before Layer 2 (financial intelligence — real lease/reserve data) and Layer 3 (Scenarios, Route Matcher, Calendar) existed. That review has not yet actually happened (per the roadmap timeline, it's still pending), and a substantial amount had shipped since the checklist was written — v2 catches the doc up to that, correctly framed as "the review that was always planned, just later and covering more than originally scoped."

**What the v2 checklist covers, per Alan's brief:**
- **What was built** — plain inventory of what exists now, not just what's changed since V1
- **Why it was built** — the reasoning behind each major piece, not just a feature list
- **How it was built** — architecture/security posture (Brain/Body separation, Firestore rules, auth, backup/restore, data residency — same categories as the original V1 checklist, refreshed)
- **Anticipated questions** — a genuine attempt to think like the reviewer, not just document what's already been decided

**Explicit scope constraints (confirmed with Alan, do not drift from these without asking):**

- **Full platform (`app.tailiq.app`) only.** The three free/lead-gen surfaces (`specs.tailiq.app`, `airframe.tailiq.app`, `engine.tailiq.app`) are explicitly out of scope for this document — they're separate, unbuilt-beyond-stub surfaces with their own security posture to review later, not part of this internal-tool conversation.

**Open items the v2 doc itself already flags** (unchanged as of this sync): multi-tenant data isolation (`companyId` unset everywhere), read-level Firestore rules on `leases`/`reserves`/`assets` (open to any signed-in role, differentiated only at write/UI level), backup-failure alerting (fix retested and working, no automated alert on a future silent failure).

IT review meeting scheduled (Nikifor Hristov, Vectorgroup IT).

### 4.88 End of Lease Position — wired into Fly-Forward (4a) — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `FlyForward.jsx` + `index.html`)
- **Context:** `endOfLeasePosition.js` (the calc module) had been built and validated in an earlier scoping session, but nothing in the app ever called it — per `eol-position-session-handoff.md` §4a, this was purely a wiring task.
- **Built:** New `📄 End of Lease Position` button in Fly-Forward's header row, gated on `lease.endOfLeaseTerms?.applies` (falling back to `getEndOfLeaseTermsDefaults()` for leases predating the EOL step). Opens `EndOfLeasePositionView` — two cards:
  - **Money** (`EOLMoneyCard`): one row per engine, calling `window.computeEngineEOLAdjustment` directly (not the combined `buildEndOfLeasePosition` wrapper, since money is computed per-engine but physical checks are shared/asset-level).
  - **Physical** (`EOLPhysicalCard`): all three redelivery margin checks via `window.buildPhysicalPositionChecks`.
- **New Body-layer helpers** in `FlyForward.jsx`: `buildEOLMoneyInputs()` (assembles `engineParts` from `asset.engines[].llps`, projects FC/FH to Expiry, resolves `escalationPctPerYr`/`catalogPriceToday` off the Knowledge Base globals) and `buildEOLPhysicalInputs()` (lowest-limiter LLP projection across all engines, landing gear months-to-due, engine on-wing FH).
- **Design call made and confirmed with Alan:** clause 6.3 (engine on-wing FH) has no real "expected removal date" field anywhere in the schema — used the relevant `EN-PR-{position}` pot's next projected event as a proxy, with an extra-prominent caveat in the UI (stacking a projection on top of a clause that's already a Lessor judgment call under 6.4).
- **`escalateAnnualFn` adapter gotcha:** `endOfLeasePosition.js`'s `ctx.todayDate` field is passed positionally as `escalateAnnualFn`'s 2nd argument, but `window.escalateAnnual`'s real signature (confirmed against `anchorReservePots`' own call) takes a **base year number**, not a Date — `buildEOLMoneyInputs` passes `new Date().getFullYear()` in that slot despite the field's name.
- **Real bug found and fixed the same session — blank screen on open:** `endOfLeasePosition.js` had never been added to `index.html`'s `<script>` tags at all (built in an earlier session, never wired in — a step earlier than the `flyForward.js` file-location mixup logged in the previous session's handoff). `window.computeEngineEOLAdjustment is not a function` in the console gave it away. Fixed with one added `<script src="/calculations/endOfLeasePosition.js"></script>` line alongside the other Brain files.
- **Verified live against MSN 1009:** correctly shows every part as `uncomputable — no TAC on file` until 4b (below) supplies real `D` values — confirmed as expected behaviour, not a bug, per the handoff's own framing.
- **Open, deliberately not built this session:** the "Generate Report" PDF export (money + physical cards via `techSpecBuilder.js`/wkhtmltopdf) — deferred, Alan confirmed current in-app view is enough for now. Also open: whether Engine 2-style partial engines (some parts computable, some not) should show the computable rows alongside the blocked ones, instead of the current all-or-nothing per-engine message — Alan's call, not yet decided.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done.
