### 4.89 TAC (Technical Acceptance Certificate) upload pipeline (4b) — ✅ BUILT (Sonnet session, July 2026, `UploadView.jsx` + `db.js` + `FlyForward.jsx`)
- **Context:** `D` (delivery/installation FC baseline) is the one input `endOfLeasePosition.js` cannot derive itself — per its own file-header comment, a missing `D` must surface as uncomputable, never default to 0. This session built the only source for it.
- **New upload type:** `📄 TAC — Delivery Baseline` tab in `UploadView.jsx`, reusing `ENGINE_LLP_PROMPT` and the Sonnet model exactly as the existing Engine LLP Sheet tab does — a TAC's LLP table has the identical shape to a status-sheet disk report, just captured at delivery instead of today. No new prompt needed.
- **Where it's stored — deliberate departure from the append-only lease pattern:** new `db.saveTACSnapshot(leaseId, tacData)` does a **merge write onto the existing lease document**, not a new append-only lease record. Reasoning: a TAC is a fixed historical fact about delivery that never gets renegotiated the way lessee/dates/`endOfLeaseTerms` do — versioning it the same way would just be noise. Attaches to `asset.currentLeaseId` specifically (D is a per-lease fact).
- **D itself:** `deliveryBaselineFC = cycle_limit − fc_remaining` at the TAC's own reading — the same "engine CSN progression stands in for a specific LLP's cycles-since-new" convention Brain 2's `calcLLPRem` already relies on.
- **Review table:** P/N and S/N stay read-only text (Alan's call — these are identity fields, not meant to be edited). **FC Remaining, Cycle Limit, and Delivery Baseline (D) are all editable.** D is seeded from the formula above as a starting suggestion but is a genuinely independent field from that point on — editing it directly does not get recalculated from Cycle Limit/FC Remaining, and vice versa. Whatever's showing in the D box at Confirm & Save time is exactly what gets saved.
- **`FlyForward.jsx` follow-up:** `buildEOLMoneyInputs` now reads `deliveryBaselineFC` from `lease.tacSnapshot`, matched per part by serial number, instead of the hardcoded `null` from 4.88.
- **Known limitation, not yet built:** APU LLPs are out of scope for TAC uploads (`componentsCovered` only ever lists `ENGINE_LLP` today) — the APU review table has no Delivery Baseline column and `confirmTAC` never touches `asset.apu`.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done.

### 4.90 TAC upload — real merge-bug found and fixed during live testing (July 2026)
- **Found by:** Alan, testing 4.89 live against MSN 1009 with a real two-engine aircraft whose TAC arrives as **two separate single-engine PDFs** (per-engine LLP status documents), not one combined document.
- **Bug:** `confirmTAC` originally replaced the entire `tacSnapshot.engines` array on every save. Uploading and confirming the LH engine's TAC first, then the RH engine's TAC second, silently wiped the LH engine's already-saved delivery-baseline data — Firestore's `merge: true` deep-merges map fields but replaces array fields wholesale, and `engines` is an array.
- **Symptom that surfaced it:** re-opening End of Lease Position after both uploads showed Engine 1 fully back to "no TAC on file" (18/18 parts uncomputable) while Engine 2 showed real, populated figures — the engine uploaded *last* survived, the one uploaded *first* was overwritten.
- **Fix:** `confirmTAC` now fetches the lease's already-saved `tacSnapshot.engines` (from `matchedLease`, refetched fresh at extract time) and merges the newly-uploaded engine(s) in by `position`, leaving any other engine's entry untouched, before calling `saveTACSnapshot` with the full merged array.
- **UI improvement bundled in:** the TAC review banner now shows which engine(s) already have a TAC on file for this lease, so the merge behaviour is visible before confirming, not just correct after the fact.
- **Also caught in the same live-test round (data issue, not a code bug):** a genuine OCR column-selection error on a real LLP sheet — HPC Rear Air Seal's Cycle Limit/FC Remaining were pulled from the wrong engine-variant sub-column (5B1/2P instead of the correct 5B4/P), confirmed by cross-checking against the part's own CSN. Correctable in the review screen now that FC Remaining/Cycle Limit/D are all editable (this item's fix depended on 4.89's editable-fields work landing first).
- **Priority:** ✅ Done.

### 4.69 Operator History — ✅ BUILT (engine tab, extraction, tech spec section, July 2026)
Built per the locked design in `operator-history-scoping-handoff.md` — a new third data type on the engine record (distinct from the LLP table and Shop Visits), the chain-of-custody record of which operators/aircraft an engine has served on.
- **Schema:** `eng.operatorHistory[]` — `{operator, aircraft, installDate, removalDate, tsnAtRemoval, csnAtRemoval, reason, source, extractedFrom}`, matching the canonical row schema in the handoff doc exactly. No Firestore rule changes needed — governed by the existing `assets` collection role-gated writes.
- **Extraction:** new `OPERATOR_HISTORY_PROMPT` (`assetHelpers.js`) + `extractOperatorHistory()` (`extraction.js`), Sonnet, same `/api/extract` envelope pattern as `extractLLPSheet`/`extractAvionicsLRU`. Prompt handles the real-world format variation from the 12 reference documents reviewed at scoping: combined vs. paired IN/OUT rows, still-on-wing detection, operator-per-row vs. operator-from-letterhead, and date-format normalisation to ISO.
- **UI (`AssetTabs.jsx`):** new `OperatorHistoryEditor` component, rendered on every engine card directly below `ShopVisitEditor` — upload → parse → editable review table → confirm/save (nothing written until confirmed); manual add-row for when no document exists; inline edit/delete on existing rows, same interaction pattern as Shop Visits.
- **Merge + validation:** new `mergeOperatorHistory()` (`extraction.js`) — sorts by `installDate` ascending and flags (⚠, non-blocking) any stint whose TSN/CSN comes in lower than the previous stint's beyond a small rounding tolerance (2hr/1cycle). This is a practical equivalent of the handoff's §8 handoff-continuity check, adapted for a schema that stores only removal/current TSN-CSN per stint (no install-time reading) — a genuine handoff mismatch shows up as TSN/CSN regressing over time. Same function used by upload-confirm, manual-add, and edit/delete so the flag logic lives in one place.
- **Tech spec (`techSpecBuilder.js`):** new Operator History table (Operator / Aircraft / Installed / Removed / TSN / CSN / Reason — matching the handoff's §9 column set) appended after Shop Visit History, only rendered when history exists. Wired into the standalone engine-only spec output (`engSec()`); **not yet wired into the full-aircraft multi-page card-layout engine highlight section** — flagged as a follow-up rather than risking the more complex card layout blind.
- **Full-aircraft tech spec section:** ✅ Built and deployed — August 2026. Operator History table now renders in both the standalone engine-only spec and the full-aircraft multi-page spec.
- **Not built this session (deliberately):** the ~50-calls/24hr soft ceiling and extraction logging for anomaly review — queued for a future session.
- **Priority:** ✅ Built and confirmed.

### 4.68 Airframe Stock Photos — ✅ BUILT (new feature, July 2026)
No equivalent mechanism existed for airframes before this session — the tech spec cover always used the per-asset uploaded "Airframe"-labelled photo with no fallback. Built the same pattern engines already had:
- **`assetHelpers.js`:** new `airframeFamily()`/`airframeStockPhotoKey()` — coarse match on `asset.model` into 7 buckets (A319, A320, A321, A330, B737, B737 MAX, B787), "MAX" checked before plain "737" so MAX variants don't land in the classic/NG bucket. Unmatched models return `null` — no stock art, same convention as unmatched engine types.
- **`techSpecBuilder.js`:** `buildTechSpecHTML()` gained a 5th param (`stockAirframePhoto`) — cover photo now falls back to the model-matched stock photo only when no per-asset "Airframe" photo exists; a real uploaded photo always wins.
- **Admin (`AdminView.jsx`):** new "Airframe Stock Photos" card, 7 upload slots, same upload-to-Cloudinary-then-Firestore-setting pattern as `EnginePhotoSettings`.

- **Known gap:** `public/share.html` (the public share-link page) does not fetch either engine or airframe stock photos at all — pre-existing minimal path, untouched by this session. A shared link for an asset with no real per-asset photo will show a blank cover.
- **Priority:** ✅ Built.

### 4.67 Engine Stock Photos — Dynamic Lookup Replacing Hardcoded CFM/V2500 Binary (July 2026)
- **Problem:** the engine stock-photo lookup was a hardcoded `isCFMEng ? "engine_photo_cfm56" : "engine_photo_v2500"` ternary, duplicated across 5 call sites (`AssetTabs.jsx`, `AssetView.jsx`, `PortfolioView.jsx`, `Prospects.jsx`, `src/lib/techSpec.js`). This only ever supported 2 settings keys, even after the full 8-family engine cover art set was completed — the other 6 families' art had nowhere to be uploaded into.
- **Fix applied:** new `engineFamily()`/`engineStockPhotoKey()`/`assetEngineStockPhotoKey()` in `assetHelpers.js`, generalising the lookup to all 8 families via a `ENGINE_FAMILY_PHOTO_KEYS` map. The two pre-existing keys (`engine_photo_cfm56`, `engine_photo_v2500`) were deliberately kept unchanged so photos already uploaded via Admin keep working; only the 6 new families got new keys. All 5 call sites updated to use the shared helper instead of their own copy of the ternary.
- **Admin (`AdminView.jsx`):** `EnginePhotoSettings` generalised from a hardcoded 2-slot layout to an 8-slot layout driven by a shared `ENGINE_PHOTO_TYPES` list.

### 4.66 Thousand-Separator on Hour Readouts — ✅ DONE (July 2026)
- **Problem:** `fmtHHMM()` (the single shared hour formatter, `techSpecBuilder.js`, loaded as a global and used across the whole app and every tech spec) rendered large hour values with no thousands separator (e.g. `49485:50`), inconsistent with cycles (CSN), which were already comma-formatted via `.toLocaleString()` almost everywhere.
- **Fix applied:** one-line change — `Math.floor(dec).toLocaleString()` instead of `Math.floor(dec)` in the whole-hours part. Since `fmtHHMM` is a single shared global function, this fixed every call site at once (Dashboard, Portfolio, asset detail tabs, all tech spec PDF output) without needing to touch each screen individually.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done.

### 4.37 Post-Bulk-Import UX Polish — ✅ DONE (July 2026)
A short follow-up pass fixing several small issues Alan caught while testing 4.36 live, plus closing out two previously-queued cosmetic items:
- **Upload tab icons for consistency** — Engine LLP Sheet and APU LLP Sheet tabs now reuse the exact same SVG path data as `techSpecBuilder.js`'s Engine (`svgEngine`) and APU (`svgBolt`) section headers instead of generic emoji, recolored via a new small `TabIcon` component to match the tab's active/inactive text color. (Tech spec doesn't have a distinct "LLP" icon separate from Engine/APU — both LLP subsections reuse the same generic list icon regardless — so this maps Engine LLP → engine icon, APU LLP → APU icon.)
- **Fly Forward button gated on lease presence** — `AssetView` and `PortfolioView` now both hide the "🚀 Fly Forward" button unless `asset.currentLeaseId` is set, in addition to the existing role check. Previously a Viewer or Admin could open Fly Forward for an asset with no lease/reserve data at all.
- **📄 lease-on-file indicator** added everywhere an asset appears: Dashboard list view (narrow column, rightmost — after Last Report), Dashboard's own card-toggle view, and the separate light-themed `PortfolioView` cards. Checks `asset.currentLeaseId` only (presence, not completeness — doesn't distinguish "activated with full pots" from "details saved, pots pending").
- **Dashboard list view — pointless "View" button removed.** Rows already navigate to the asset on click (`onSelect`); the button was dead weight. Removed the button column entirely and relocated the lease indicator into that freed-up rightmost slot.
- **Tier-choice screen "ⓘ" info-toggle fixed** — replaced the low-contrast circled-i (queued since the previous session, no direction chosen between text-link/accordion/tooltip) with a "Details ▾" text link + rotating chevron on all three tier options (Manual/Quick/Confidential). Chosen over a hover-only tooltip since it works identically on touch and desktop.
- **Dashboard NLG/LLG/RLG columns centered** — both header and cell content (dates, `—`, the 🔧 at-shop glyph) now explicitly center-aligned. Two real regressions caught and fixed in the same pass before landing on the final version: (1) adding `whiteSpace:"nowrap"` to keep dates on one line instead widened the columns enough to force the whole dashboard into horizontal scroll — reverted, wrapping to two lines is fine once centered; (2) the pill `<span>`'s horizontal padding was splitting across the wrapped two-line date (day/month landing at a different horizontal offset than the year below it) — fixed by making the pill `display:inline-block` so its padding wraps the whole two-line box as one unit instead of being applied per-line.
- **Fleet Overview header banner (navy → white)** — confirmed done by Alan this session (see Section 19/17 — pre-existing entry, this session just confirms it's genuinely live, closing out any docs/reality drift on that item).
- **Files touched:** `index.html` only.
- **Priority:** ✅ DONE — confirmed working by Alan.

### 4.91 EOL Position + TAC pipeline — ✅ live-tested end-to-end against Demo Asset 4 (MSN 5533, July 2026)
- **Context:** `endOfLeasePosition.js` (4.88) and the TAC upload pipeline (4.89/4.90) had been built and confirmed working in isolation against MSN 1009, but MSN 1009 has no TAC on file — this was the first live test of the full chain, D-baseline through to a real EOL Position figure.
- **Test asset:** MSN 5533, A320-232/CFM56-5B4/P, built specifically for this (see `DEMO_CHEAT_SHEET.md` Asset 4). Uploaded: lease doc, 3 utilisation reports, 2 Engine LLP sheets, 1 APU LLP sheet, 2 TAC documents (one per engine position, D = 11,200 FC on both).
- **Result — EOL Position screen, projected to Expiry 15-Nov-2026:**

  | Engine | Gross adjustment | Pot balance at Expiry | Net adjustment (Lessee owes) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Engine 1 (EN-LP) | $926,328 | $676,262 | **$250,066** |
  | Engine 2 (EN-LP) | $926,328 | $671,262 | **$255,066** |
  | **Combined** | | | **$505,132** |

  Physical Position margins both showed "Meets requirement" (Engine LLPs 3,025 FC remaining vs. ≥1,000 FC threshold; Landing Gear 31.0 months vs. ≥12 months threshold) — as designed, this asset's story is the EOL payment only, nothing else should flag.
- **Cross-check against the cheat sheet's back-of-envelope estimate (~$520,000 combined):** live figure ($505,132) is ~3% below the estimate — well within the margin flagged in the cheat sheet's own caveats (live calc engine vs. hand arithmetic; the two gross adjustments came out identical at $926,328 rather than the slightly different $952,800/$952,794 hand-estimated per position, suggesting the app derives C/D more precisely per part than the single-figure approximation used when picking the demo numbers). Direction and scale both hold — this is a working confirmation, not a discrepancy to chase.
- **TAC review screen:** confirmed both engines' 18-part stacks matched cleanly against the known CFM56-5B LLP list with no part-count mismatch, per the strict-confirm design in `end-of-lease-position-handoff.md` §7.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — first full live confirmation of the EOL feature end-to-end, including TAC.

### 4.92 TAC upload requires an active lease already on file — sequencing gap, needs a design discussion (July 2026)
- **Symptom, flagged by Alan during the Demo Asset 4 test:** the TAC upload flow can't be used until a lease already exists on the asset — TAC data attaches to `asset.currentLeaseId` (per 4.89), so there's no "upload the TAC first" path.
- **Why this matters more for external clients than for Maverick:** per `end-of-lease-position-handoff.md` §7, "no TAC" is expected to be the *normal* onboarding state for a boutique lessor arriving mid-lease with little more than the lease document itself — and the completeness gap ("EOL position unavailable — no delivery baseline") is meant to be a sales hook, not friction. If TAC genuinely cannot be attached before a lease is entered, that's consistent with the intended order (lease first, TAC second) for most onboarding — but worth explicitly confirming this is the *only* order anyone would ever hit it in, rather than assuming.
- **Not yet scoped as a fix** — may not need one; could simply be documenting the required order clearly in the UI (e.g. TAC upload tab disabled/greyed with a tooltip until a lease exists) rather than a data-model change. Flagging for a short design discussion, not a build task yet.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — not a data-loss or correctness risk, but a real onboarding-flow question worth resolving deliberately before external clients hit it, rather than discovering it live.

### 4.93 Fleet Snapshot Writer — ✅ BUILT AND TESTED (Cloud Run function, July 30 2026)

- **Built per the locked design in `fleet-exposure-build-handoff.md` §6.** A scheduled Cloud Run function (`fleet-snapshot-writer`, europe-west2, Node.js 22), triggered monthly by Cloud Scheduler (`fleet-snapshot-writer-monthly`, cron `0 2 1 * *`, timezone Europe/London — an IANA zone that auto-adjusts for BST/GMT, not a fixed offset). Single writer — no client-side snapshot write exists anywhere in the app, and none should be added; a scheduled write plus an on-login write would race and produce duplicate snapshots.
- **Field-shape decision:** the original §6 stub specified `totalReserveBalance`/`totalProjectedLiability`/`netPosition`/`riskPeaksByQuarter`/`liquidityClusters`/`assetsInRedZone[]`/`assetsInAmberZone[]`. None of these are computed anywhere in the shipped Fleet Exposure View — the redesign session (`fleet-exposure-redesign-handoff.md`) produced a different model instead: `totalHighCaseGap`, `statusCounts`, `timeAxis`, `assetAxis`. Rather than invent numbers to match a stub the code had already moved past, the writer snapshots the real, current output of `buildFleetExposure()`. `riskPeaksByQuarter` and `liquidityClusters` were never wired at fleet level at all — `riskPeak.js`/`shortfall.js` exist in the repo but nothing calls them from `fleetExposure.js`. See `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 5 — the `fleetSnapshots/` stub there was stale, not the code.
- **Portability — extends the `api/email-ingest.js` `vm` shim pattern** from one self-contained Brain (`utilisation.js`) to six: `llpCatalogue.js`, `llpCalculator.js`, `realAssetContext.js`, `flyForward.js`, `maintenanceCal.js`, `fleetExposure.js` — loaded into one shared `vm` sandbox, in that exact order. Order matters: `flyForward.js` reads `window.calcLLPRem`/`window.llpVector`/`window.validateStubBuffer` (set by `llpCalculator.js`) and `window.lookupLLPCataloguePrice` (set by `llpCatalogue.js`), mirroring the `<script>` tag load order already in `index.html` — these six files are not independently portable, only portable as a set.
- **Body-layer assembly rewritten server-side, not shimmed.** `loadFleetExposureBundle`/`buildFleetExposureEntry` (originally in `src/lib/flyForwardHelpers.js`) are an ES module coupled to the client Firestore SDK and to `window.parseDMYDate`/`window.escalateAnnual` set elsewhere at runtime — not portable as-is, rewritten directly against the Admin SDK.
- **Verified end-to-end against real production data:** correctly excluded 13/17 real assets as `NO_LEASE` (Electra/Arkia lease records not yet loaded in TailiQ), correctly refused to write given the >50% exclusion threshold, and correctly sent a SendGrid alert rather than writing a misleading partial snapshot. Will begin writing real snapshots automatically once lease coverage improves — no further code changes required for that.
- **Not yet built, deliberately out of scope for this session:** the Monthly Report itself (§4.100) — correctly waiting on team logins existing as an audience.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — infrastructure proven end-to-end.

### 4.94 Security finding — `firestore-backup`'s Cloud Scheduler job has no auth header (found July 30 2026, during 4.93 build)

- **Found while wiring up `fleet-snapshot-writer`'s own scheduler job** — checked the existing `firestore-backup` Scheduler job's configuration for a service account to reuse, and found its Auth header is set to "None." This means that job invokes the `firestore-backup` Cloud Run function's endpoint without any authentication.
- **Not fixed this session** — unrelated to the Fleet Snapshot Writer build, and backup infrastructure changes deserve their own deliberate pass rather than a drive-by fix.
- **Risk:** an unauthenticated HTTP endpoint that triggers a full Firestore export to Cloud Storage is a real, if narrow, attack surface — low likelihood (URL isn't published anywhere), but the fix is cheap once scheduled: same OIDC + IAM Invoker pattern now proven working for `fleet-snapshot-writer`.
- **Fix for a future session:** switch `firestore-backup`'s Cloud Run service to "Require authentication," create or reuse a dedicated service account for its Scheduler job, grant it Cloud Run Invoker scoped to that function, update the Scheduler job's Auth header — mechanically identical to what was just done for 4.93.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — not urgent, but a real gap worth closing deliberately.

---

### 4.95 EAG two-engine LLP sheet upload failure — ✅ fixed (July 30 2026)

- **Symptom:** LLP extraction failed for a real two-engine asset (EAG) roughly half the time — "The AI could not extract structured data from this file," firing before any review screen, before any save. Initially suspected to share a root cause with the MSN 1009 two-engine TAC merge bug (4.90), but confirmed to be a different, upstream failure — extraction itself was failing, not the post-extraction Firestore merge.
- **Root cause:** the model was narrating every LLP row individually in prose before writing any JSON, causing non-deterministic token consumption that failed roughly half the time — not a token-limit or timeout problem in itself, but the *variability* of an unbounded narration step colliding with a fixed budget.
- **Fix:** `ENGINE_LLP_PROMPT` tightened to explicitly forbid per-row narration while retaining the brief column-identification reasoning step; automatic per-page splitting added for multi-page PDFs (`pdfjsLib` page-count detection client-side); `api/extract.js`'s `maxDuration` raised from 60s to 300s as a second line of defence.
- **Verified:** four consecutive clean passes on the real EAG file after the fix.
- **Priority:** ✅ Fixed and confirmed.

### 4.100 Monthly Report — scoped, locked scope (July 30 2026)

- **What it is:** a monthly email report (PDF via SendGrid) comparing the current fleet position to the previous month's `fleetSnapshot`. The delta is the story — makes a number that moved £900k impossible to unsee. Reaches C-suite people who don't log in.
- **Five sections confirmed, one future:** (1) Fleet Headline — total exposure, change since last snapshot, asset coverage count, delta labelled with the actual previous snapshot date ("since 12 June," never "since last month"). (2) What Moved — 2–5 line items on which assets drove the change and why. (3) Pot Health Summary — fleet-wide green/amber/red counts per pot type and which changed colour, framed as rate-negotiation intelligence ("EN-LP surplus across 4 assets, LG shortfall on 2 — tighten LG rates on next renewal round"); a future addition splitting shortfalls by lessee vs lessor obligation is pending senior-manager confirmation of whether lease terms data supports the split. (4) Upcoming Events — next 3–6 months of the maintenance calendar. (5) Utilisation Exceptions — assets deviating >20% (tunable to 15%) from their trailing 6-month average FH/FC. (6, future, blocked on 4.101) Completed Events — projected vs actual once the Cost Tracker has data.
- **Killed/excluded:** EOL flag and EOL receivable summary (permanent noise on a mature fleet — stays in-app), data quality/completeness score (admin view, not board-level), rate coverage ratio (moves too slowly for monthly), lease maturity profile (always something there, always noise), product analytics (different audience), AI-generated industry news (staleness/hallucination risk against an otherwise fully deterministic report — if added later, a human-curated free-text field instead).
- **Prerequisites:** `fleetSnapshots` (done, 4.93), team logins (not built — this is the actual blocker), SendGrid (already configured).
- **Priority:** 🟢 Scoped, not started — blocked on team logins, not on design.

---

### 4.101 SV Cost Tracker — completed-event recording, schema locked (July 30 2026)

- **What it is:** a structured entry form for recording actual costs and details when a maintenance event completes — feeds (a) projected-vs-actual comparison, (b) the Monthly Report, (c) the Rate Recommendation Engine (4.102) over time.
- **Nudge mechanism:** when a projected event passes its projected date by 30 days, it surfaces as a "pending completion" item at the top of the Maintenance Calendar — self-populating, no manual trigger. Two paths per item: Enter costs (opens a pre-populated entry form, delta calculated automatically) or Dismiss (acknowledges the event happened but no cost data is available — saved as "completed, no cost data," an honest gap rather than a hidden one). Items remain on the list until one action is taken.
- **Schema — required (minimum viable record):** asset, event type, MRO region, total cost. **Optional:** MRO name, turnaround time or date in/out, SV number for this engine (materially affects benchmarking — first SV workscope is lighter than subsequent visits), routine/base scope cost vs non-routine/findings cost, planned-vs-finding flag per workscope line, scope notes. **Derived automatically:** asset age at event, asset type/engine family/operator region, projected-vs-actual delta, post-SV build life (populates retrospectively from the next utilisation report or disk sheet).
- **Workscope bundling:** one shop visit record (asset, MRO, region, dates, total cost) can contain multiple workscope lines (e.g. PR + LLP combined) — intelligence layer can segment PR costs from LLP costs even when physically combined in one visit.
- **Knowledge Base interaction:** actual costs do NOT auto-update KB cost bands. KB stays human-curated — the system surfaces divergence, the human decides whether to adjust.
- **Applies to all event types** — Engine PR/LLP/APU (highest cost variance, most interesting to the intelligence layer) and Airframe 6Y/12Y/Landing Gear (lower variance but builds the regional benchmarking dataset).
- **Priority:** 🟢 Scoped, standalone build, not blocked on anything — should be built and in use as early as possible since every completed event entered, even bare-minimum, is a future data point.

---

### 4.102 Rate Recommendation Engine — design concept locked, future (July 30 2026)

- **What it is:** once enough completed events exist (4.101), the system derives recommended reserve rates per pot type, backed by actual cost data rather than industry assumptions — segmented by engine family, event type, SV number, asset age bracket, MRO region. Displayed alongside the current lease rate, e.g. "Recommended EN-PR rate based on 8 completed events: $145/FH. Your current rate: $118/FH. Structural underfunding of 19%."
- **Phase 1 (current scope):** internal only — Maverick's own completed-event history. Phase 2 (anonymous cross-client pool) and Phase 3 (rate recommendation as commercial hook) are go-to-market decisions documented in `COMMERCIAL_VISION.md`, not active build items.
- **Context:** third-party industry data sources use top-down (MRO list prices, industry estimates). A completed-events pool would be bottom-up (actual invoices, actual non-routine findings, actual turnaround times) — potentially more actionable for the specific "what should my reserve rates be" question.
- **Priority:** 🟢 Scoped as strategic direction, build blocked on Cost Tracker data volume (Phase 1) and multiple clients (Phase 2).

### 4.103 SV Cost Tracker — ✅ BUILT (July 31 2026, `db.js` + `FlyForward.jsx`)

Builds 4.101's locked schema and nudge mechanism into a working feature, plus one gap found and closed the same session after Alan tried it live.

- **`db.js`:** new `getCompletedEvents(assetId)` / `saveCompletedEvent(assetId, companyId, data)`, matching the existing flat-collection + `assetId`-field convention (`saveShopVisitProjection`/`saveUtilisation`) — append-only, one `addDoc` per completed event, never overwritten.
- **`PendingCompletionsPanel`:** self-populating list at the top of the Maintenance Calendar tab. An event qualifies once it's 30+ days past its own projected date with no matching `completedEvents` record for that `code`+`dueCycle`. Two actions per item: **Enter Costs** (opens `CostTrackerEntryForm`, pre-populated from the projection) or **Dismiss** (writes a `noCostData:true` record directly — "completed, no cost data" is an honest gap, not a hidden one, so this path deliberately does not require MRO region/total cost).
- **`CostTrackerEntryForm`:** required fields per the locked schema (MRO region, total cost) up front; everything else (MRO name, turnaround/dates, SV number, routine/non-routine split, workscope lines, scope notes) behind a collapsed "+ Add more details." SV number only shows for engine/APU codes. Derived fields (asset age at event from `asset.dom`, asset type, engine family, projected-vs-actual delta) computed automatically at save.
- **Gap found and fixed same session:** the first version was *only* the pending nudge — invisible unless something was currently overdue, with no way to review a past entry once saved. Alan: *"why did we not... can we look to see prev entries."* Added **`CompletedEventsHistory`** — an always-visible card listing every logged event for the asset (date, MRO region, total cost, delta vs. projected), plus a **"+ Log Completed Event"** manual path (pick any event type already on the asset — pulled from its real reserve pots, no re-derivation — and any date) for back-filling historical data or logging something not currently in the pending list.
- **Role gating:** both write paths (Enter Costs, Dismiss, manual log) gated on the same `canEnterLeaseData` flag `AssetView.jsx` already threads through (Admin/Editor/Data Entry, not Viewer) — Viewer sees the pending list and history but no action buttons.
- **Knowledge Base interaction confirmed unchanged from 4.101's spec:** completed-event costs do NOT auto-update KB cost bands anywhere in this build — purely a data-collection layer for the future Rate Recommendation Engine (4.102).
- **Validated:** Babel JSX transform clean on every touched file; diffs confirmed additive/isolated.
- **Not yet built, per 4.101/4.102's own scope:** any read-back into KB cost bands, the Rate Recommendation Engine itself, post-SV build-life retroactive population.
- **Priority:** ✅ Built and usable — every event logged from today is a real data point banked for 4.102.

### 4.104 Maintenance Calendar — now leaseless-safe at the ASSET level too — ✅ BUILT (July 31 2026, `flyForwardHelpers.js` + `FlyForward.jsx`)

Follow-up to 4.86, which fixed this at the **fleet** level only (`buildCalendarEntry`/`PortfolioView.jsx`) — the asset-level `MaintenanceCalendarView` still shared `buildFlyForwardProjection` with the Financials tab and inherited its hard lease requirement, until Alan asked for asset-level parity directly: *"i want the maintenance calendar to work regardless of lease status at an asset level."*

- **New `buildAssetMaintenanceCalendar()`** in `flyForwardHelpers.js` — the asset-level twin of `buildCalendarEntry`. No lease → 180-month default horizon (same constant already established at fleet level). No confirmed reserve pots → synthesizes pot *structure only* (never a rate — `accrualRate` stays 0) via the same `buildPotDefsForActivation`/`buildPotFromDef` generator the Lease Wizard itself uses, then anchors real dates from `asset.landingGear`/engine LLP data. **EN-PR and AP-OH are omitted, not estimated** — same reasoning as 4.86 (no real anchor data exists for either without a lease, so nothing is guessed). Stops after Brain 6 (dates only, no pass-2/dollar projections) — same scope as the fleet-level function, same "known duplication, don't consolidate without a fresh look at both call sites" tradeoff already accepted for `buildCalendarEntry`.
- **`buildFlyForwardProjection` (Financials) deliberately untouched** — still requires a real lease, exactly as before, so the Financials tab can never show a fabricated dollar figure. The two functions are not shared and should not be, for the same reason Fleet Exposure and the Fleet Calendar tab already keep synthetic and real pots strictly separate.
- **`MaintenanceCalendarView`:** the old `if (!asset.currentLeaseId || loadError || !lease)` gate is gone — now only blocks on an actual load failure. When running on synthesized pots, a plain informational note (not a warning) explains exactly what's real (landing gear, LLP) and what's omitted (EN-PR, AP-OH) — matching the fleet-level tone, no "estimate" language anywhere.
- **Net effect:** the Cost Tracker (4.103) now works on any asset with real component data on file, leased or not.
- **Validated:** Babel JSX transform + `node --check` clean on both files; diff confirmed additive (new function + one changed gate), nothing in the existing Financials path touched.
- **Priority:** ✅ Built. 🟡 Live-test against a real leaseless asset owed before fully closing out (same pattern every build session here follows) — confirm real dates show up, confirm EN-PR/AP-OH are cleanly absent rather than showing zeros, confirm Financials still correctly requires a lease.

### 4.105 Portrait/Landscape dual layout — first pass built (Financials tab) (July 31 2026, new `layoutMode.js` + `FlyForward.jsx`)

Build session against `landscape-portrait-layout-scoping-handoff.md`'s locked decisions (manual toggle, 2-up/3-up grids, current stacked layout = portrait baseline). Recommended first step per the handoff (`FlyForward.jsx`'s Financials tab) — the rest of the rollout (Scenarios, `PortfolioView.jsx`/Dashboard) is queued, not started.

- **New `src/lib/layoutMode.js`:** a `useLayoutMode()`/`LayoutModeProvider` Context pair — per-user preference stored via the existing generic `db.getSetting`/`setSetting` key/value store (no new Firestore collection), gated behind a ~900px viewport-width floor. Below the floor, the *effective* mode is always portrait regardless of the saved preference — this is a hard rendering constraint (a 2/3-up grid genuinely can't render usefully below that width), not the auto-orientation-detection the scoping doc explicitly ruled out. One shared Provider at the app root means every page reads the same value with a single Firestore read, rather than each page independently re-fetching on every navigation.
- **`FlyForward.jsx` (Financials tab):** in landscape, Portfolio Shortfall Summary + Risk Peaks sit side by side (only when Risk Peaks has content — otherwise Shortfall Summary alone stays full width, same as portrait); pot cards go into a grid, 2-up under 1300px / 3-up above it. Portrait mode is byte-identical to what existed before — grid styles are conditionally `undefined` when not landscape.
- **Known imperfection, not fixed this pass:** `FFPotCard` has its own baked-in `marginBottom:14`; left untouched per the scoping doc's own non-goal ("card internals stay untouched, only the outer arrangement changes"). Column gaps are clean in the grid; row-to-row vertical spacing is whatever that 14px produces, not perfectly tuned.
- **Placement saga, resolved this session (see 4.106 for the final outcome):** first placed as an icon in the Financials tab header (wrong — Alan wanted it app-wide, not per-tab); then as an icon built directly into the trailing nav pill, app-wide via a shared Provider (Alan: icon "not very clear"); ultimately moved into the new Settings tab as a clearly labeled Portrait/Landscape control — see 4.106.
- **Build error found and fixed same session:** the file was first authored as `layoutMode.js` but contained real JSX (`<button>`/`<span>`), which this project's Vite build only parses in `.jsx` files — first attempted fix was renaming to `.jsx`, which Alan correctly pushed back on (*"you sure its jsk?"*) since every other file in `lib/` is `.js` with no exceptions, and a rename means deleting-and-re-adding a file via his GitHub-web-UI workflow rather than replacing content in place. Corrected properly: converted the two JSX-using functions (`LayoutModeProvider`, `LayoutModeToggle`) to `React.createElement` calls, keeping the file genuinely JSX-free and valid as plain `.js`, matching every other file in `lib/`.
- **Scenarios tab + `PortfolioView.jsx`/Dashboard:** ✅ Built and deployed — August 2026. Portrait/landscape rollout now complete across all tabs.
- **Priority:** ✅ Done — portrait/landscape available across all tabs.

### 4.106 Settings page — Admin Panel renamed and re-gated per-tab, not whole-panel-admin-only (July 31 2026, `App.jsx` + `AdminView.jsx`)

Alan, mid-session: *"lets rename this panel to settings and not admin, we can gate the content rather than the whole panel."* Also resolved a real question about existing role access along the way.

- **`App.jsx`:** the "Admin" nav-pill entry (previously `userRole==='admin'` only) replaced with "Settings," visible to every role. The `view==="admin"&&userRole==='admin'` / `"Admin access required"` render pair replaced with a single ungated `view==="settings"` render, passing `userRole` through so `AdminView` can gate its own tabs.
- **`AdminView.jsx` — heading renamed "Admin Panel" → "Settings."** Tabs now gate individually instead of the whole page gating on admin: **Guide** and **Settings** — every role; **Knowledge Base** — Editor and Admin only (not Viewer/Data Entry, matching the existing "Data Entry doesn't see financial outputs" pattern); **Admin Panel** (Assets + Users, merged from what were two separate top-level tabs into one) — Admin only. Each tab body re-checks its own gate too, not just the nav row, matching the defense-in-depth pattern the old `view==="admin"` code already used.
- **Layout toggle lands here** (see 4.105) — a clearly labeled Portrait/Landscape segmented control at the top of the Settings tab, replacing the unclear header icon. Visible to every role, since it's a personal preference, not a permission; shows an explanatory note instead of the control below the width floor rather than a disabled/dead button.
- **Judgment call made and flagged, not silently assumed:** the existing Tech Spec Logo/Disclaimer and Engine/Airframe Stock Photo *editing* controls (also in the old Settings tab) were gated to Editor+Admin specifically, not opened to every role — these are real writes to company branding, not a personal toggle, so Viewer/Data Entry now see a "can only be changed by an Editor or Admin" note instead of the controls. Easy to loosen or tighten if this reading of Alan's "all users get access to guide and settings" was too narrow.
- **Stock photos — considered moving into Knowledge Base, left in Settings.** Alan asked whether they belonged there; recommendation given and applied: Knowledge Base is specifically financial forecasting assumptions (cost bands, LLP catalogue pricing), stock photos are cosmetic tech-spec branding — they sit more naturally next to Logo/Disclaimer, unrelated to forecasting. Reversible if Alan disagrees.
- **Real question resolved, no code needed:** Alan asked why Viewer didn't have Financials/Scenarios access, given C-suite will be Viewer role. Confirmed by direct read of `App.jsx`: `canSeeAdvanced=!!userRole&&userRole!=='dataEntry'` already covers Viewer — it only excludes Data Entry. Calendar/Financials/Scenarios/Portfolio were never gated away from Viewer; only the *new* Knowledge Base tab is admin/editor-only, and that's config, not the numbers themselves (Viewer still sees the KB values driving a projection via Fly-Forward's existing read-only Assumptions panel).
- **Validated:** Babel JSX transform clean on both files; diffs reviewed line-by-line, confirmed scoped to exactly this change.
- **Priority:** ✅ Built. 🟢 A full standalone Settings page (rather than folding into the former Admin Panel) remains a possible future direction per Alan — not today's scope.

*This session (July 2026 — Calendar leaseless-asset fix + IT checklist v2 planning, Sonnet build + notes): Follow-up to the same day's Layer 3 build (4.85). Fixed two real bugs Alan caught live-testing the new Calendar tab — `loadFleetExposureBundle` never queried Firestore at all for leaseless assets (regardless of what data actually existed), and pot anchoring was gated on lease presence even though it only needs rate/checks/asset. Deeper fix: confirmed reserve pot documents were never the right requirement for Calendar/clash-detection — new `buildCalendarEntry()` synthesizes pot structure from the same generator the Lease Wizard uses (`pots.js`) when no confirmed pot exists, then real component data (landing gear next-due, engine LLP remaining life) drives the actual dates exactly as it does for confirmed pots. Kept strictly separate from Fleet Exposure's financial path — synthetic pots always have accrualRate 0, never feed a dollar figure. Two corrections made after Alan reviewed the first version live: EN-PR and AP-OH are now OMITTED for synthetic-pot assets rather than estimated ("if no data for this then ignore" — no real anchor data exists for either, so any date shown would be a fabricated guess); and `DEFAULT_CALENDAR_HORIZON_MONTHS` raised from 24 to 180, after Alan's own Fleet Overview vs. Calendar comparison caught real landing-gear dates (MSN 1473/3056, ~33 months out) silently vanishing — the underlying event generators have no beyond-horizon grace, so 24 months (shorter than LG-OH's own 120-month interval) was structurally guaranteed to drop real dates. New `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.86 (full detail, all three fixes). Validated with direct functional tests at each step, including one replicating Alan's exact scenario (a real date 33 months out, confirmed dropped at 24-month horizon, confirmed correctly shown at 180). Also logged a new planned item, 4.87: a refreshed IT/Security Review checklist (the original V1 gate, Section 3a, predates all of Layer 2/3 and hasn't actually happened yet) — scope explicitly constrained per Alan to internal-build framing only (no marketing/ISTAT language) and the Full platform only (excludes specs/airframe/engine domains). Not yet started — flagged for a dedicated session. Files: `src/lib/flyForwardHelpers.js`, `src/components/PortfolioView.jsx`, `public/calculations/fleetExposure.js`.*

*This session (July 2026 — APP_SURFACE multi-entry split + domain setup, Sonnet build): Follow-up to the Vite migration, which had deliberately deferred this. Built the infrastructure for all four product surfaces to deploy independently — `vite.config.mjs` now picks an entry HTML (`index.html`/`specs.html`/`airframe.html`/`engine.html`) based on an `APP_SURFACE` env var, and a new `scripts/finalize-build.mjs` postbuild step renames the output to `dist/index.html` regardless of which surface built, since Vite otherwise keeps the source HTML's name and Vercel serves `/` from `index.html`. Verified all four builds actually work (not just syntax-checked) by pulling the live repo's public GitHub tarball and running real `vite build`s locally before Alan pushed anything — confirmed the bundle-size split is already real with placeholder content: `app` 528 KB JS vs. 142 KB JS for each stub surface (bare React, no app component code). Deployed: existing app project given an explicit `APP_SURFACE=app` env var; three new Vercel projects created from the same repo (`tailiq-specs`/`tailiq-airframe`/`tailiq-engine`, each with its own `APP_SURFACE` value), all confirmed showing their correct stub pages. Two red "Error" deploys seen mid-session on the existing app project were traced to commits pushed before `finalize-build.mjs` itself had landed (Alan pushes file-by-file via GitHub's web UI) — not a regression, confirmed superseded by the next green deploy, and the live app re-verified working end-to-end after this session's `package.json`/`vite.config.mjs` changes. All three new subdomains (`specs.tailiq.app`, `airframe.tailiq.app`, `engine.tailiq.app`) wired via Vercel's per-domain CNAME targets + matching Cloudflare DNS-only records, confirmed live over HTTPS by Alan. New `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.58. *Last updated: July 2026 — This session (Knowledge Base built + real-world testing/bugfix pass, Sonnet, git tag pending): Built the Knowledge Base end to end per `knowledge-base-scoping-handoff.md` — Firestore layer (`knowledgeBase/{companyId}` doc + `llpCatalogue` subcollection), `firestore.rules` (Admin/Editor write, any-signed-in read), the three-tier hierarchy helper (`src/lib/knowledgeBase.js`), and Admin UI (Forecasting Defaults form, LLP Catalogue with manual entry + Excel/PDF upload) — full detail in new `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.82. Wired the KB into three places that were already shaped to receive it: `pots.js`/`LeaseWizard.jsx` (cost bands, EN-PR bands, escalation now resolve through the KB tier instead of bare hardcoded literals), and — notably — Brain 6 (`maintenanceCal.js`) needed **zero code changes**, since it already accepted a `durationDefaults` parameter that simply had nothing populating it before this session; same story for two window globals in `flyForward.js`/`pots.js` (`window.LLP_CATALOGUE_PRICES`, `window.lookupLLPCataloguePrice`) that existed as dormant, defensively-coded hooks with nothing behind them until now. New read-only "Assumptions" panel on Fly-Forward, visible to every role including Viewer, per the scoping doc's UI spec. **Alan's first live test against real fleet data surfaced three genuine bugs, all found and fixed in the same session, not deferred** (`TECH_DEBT.md` 4.83/4.84): (1) the Excel catalogue upload only ever read the workbook's first sheet regardless of which family tab was selected, and separately took the *oldest* price column when a real multi-year escalation-model spreadsheet had one per year — both confirmed and fixed against Alan's actual `Engine_LLP_Escalation_Model.xlsx`, going from 18 matched parts (with stale 2023 pricing) to 43 (with correct 2026 pricing); (2) two React state-timing bugs stacked in the upload's match-counting and re-scan logic — one mutating counters inside a `setRows` updater and reading them back before React had run it, the other an unstable `assets` prop reference causing the fleet re-scan to silently wipe out just-matched prices moments after they appeared ("flashes up then disappears") — both root-caused via direct evidence (an impossible empty "unmatched" array logged alongside a reported zero-match result) rather than guessed at; (3) the PDF catalogue upload was built against a whole-document extraction pattern (matching `extractLLPSheet`/`extractAvionicsLRU` in `extraction.js`) that broke against Alan's real ~100+ page full CFM manufacturer catalogue with a 504 timeout — redesigned from "extract everything" to "look up the fleet's known ~50-60 part numbers" (client-side text extraction + string search + only matched snippets sent to `/api/extract`), making cost and latency scale with the fleet's own part count rather than the source catalogue's length. All three confirmed fixed and working end-to-end against Alan's real files, not just unit-tested in isolation. Files touched: `firestore.rules`, `db.js`, new `src/lib/knowledgeBase.js`, new `src/lib/llpCatalogueImport.js`, `pots.js`, `App.jsx`, `AdminView.jsx`, `FlyForward.jsx`, `LeaseWizard.jsx`, `flyForwardHelpers.js`. `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 5/17/18/19 and top status line updated in this sync — see there for the model-level summary.*

*Last updated: July 31, 2026 — This session (three bug fixes + SV Cost Tracker build + leaseless Calendar at asset level + portrait/landscape first pass + Settings restructure, Sonnet build, multiple sub-sessions synced together): Six threads closed out across one long session, each with real course-correction along the way rather than a straight build-and-ship.

**Three carried bugs fixed** (4.75, 4.79, 4.80), two with the record corrected rather than just closed: 4.75's actual root cause turned out to be one level up from where earlier sessions had guessed — the deterministic EN-LP catalogue check was already correctly gated, the bug was in the AI-context builder handing EN-PR pots the EN-LP $/FC rate regardless of pot type. 4.79 turned out to have no real "Specs tab" instance at all — direct inspection found the Specs tab is read-only for DOM, the single real bug lived in the Overview tab's `Field` component, now fixed with card-expiry-style auto-slash masking. 4.80 confirmed as pure insertion order (no sort at all), not the suspected string sort, fixed with a `parseCheckInterval()` helper in both the Overview tab's display and a second, previously-undiscovered instance in `PhotosAndSpecs.jsx`'s own editable Check History block (fixed with index-safe sorting, since that view edits/deletes by array position).

**SV Cost Tracker built** (4.103) against 4.101's locked schema — entry form, self-populating pending-completion nudge, role-gated write paths. **Real gap found and closed same session:** the first version was pure write-only (no way to review a past entry), caught when Alan tried it live and asked where the tracker even was — added an always-visible Completed Events history plus a manual "+ Log Completed Event" path.

### 4.108 Backup Failure Alerting — ✅ DONE (August 2026, revised)
- **Original fix (superseded):** Cloud Monitoring log-based metric `firestore_backup_success` + a Metric Absence alerting policy. Discovered structurally broken within days of shipping — GCP caps Metric Absence trigger time at 23.5 hours, which is less than the backup's 24-hour cadence, so the policy false-positived every single night regardless of threshold. Deleted.
- **Replacement fix:** dedicated `backup-watchdog` Cloud Run function (2nd gen, Node.js, europe-west2), triggered by Cloud Scheduler every 6 hours (`backup-watchdog-check`, UTC). Lists the `daily/` folders in `vector-fleet-firestore-backups`, reads the actual creation time of the most recent one, and emails `alan.shorten@gmail.com` via SendGrid only if the gap exceeds 26 hours — bypassing the Metric Absence cap entirely since it's a plain storage check, not a log-absence detector. Deduplicates repeat alerts for the same outage via a marker file (`_watchdog/last-alert.json`) in the bucket.
- **New infra:** dedicated service account `backup-watchdog@vector-fleet.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, scoped to Storage Object Admin on the backup bucket only (not project-wide) — least-privilege, separate from `firestore-backup-runner`. SendGrid sender `invites@tailiq.app` reused; dedicated Mail-Send-only API key created for this function rather than reusing the shared key.
- **Verified end-to-end:** deployed, scheduler force-run confirmed `200` response, no false email sent (backup was genuinely healthy at test time), old Cloud Monitoring policy deleted.
- **Also closes** the backup-failure-alerting open item in `TailiQ_Security_Review_v4.docx` Section 6.
- **Small open item noticed in passing, not yet actioned:** `nightly-firestore-backup` and `fleet-snapshot-writer-monthly` Cloud Scheduler jobs have inconsistent timezones (one appears unset/blank, the other explicit BST) — worth normalising to UTC deliberately in a future session, not touched here to avoid an unintended run-time shift on the snapshot writer.

Four changes shipped together in `LeaseWizard.jsx`:
1. **Tier order:** tier-choice screen resequenced to Manual → Confidential → Quick (cheapest/most-private first, increasing data exposure after).
2. **Dashed dropzone:** both Quick Extract and Confidential Extract upload screens replaced native `<input type="file">` with the app-wide dashed-dropzone pattern (📁 icon, "Click to select file" label, hidden input behind label, filename shown in gold on selection) — consistent with Upload tab and LLPExtractor.
3. **Explicit Extract button:** gold "Extract →" button added to both screens, disabled until a file is selected. Extraction no longer fires on file selection — only on explicit button click.
4. **`aiFile` state reset:** `aiFile` reset to `null` on every back-navigation path to the tier screen (Back from quick-upload, Back from privacy-upload, Start Over from ai-review, Back from details when an ai-result was in progress) — prevents stale filename showing if the user switches tier.
- **File touched:** `LeaseWizard.jsx` only.

*This session (July 2026 — reserve pot defaults/validation/display order, end-of-session batch): Three fixes agreed and shipped together to close out the MSN TEST-99 manual test session. (1) `FIXED_RESERVE_POT_DEFS`' `accrualBasis` corrected for AF-6Y/AF-12Y/AP-OH/LG-OH (all four wrongly defaulted to `per_FH`) to match the locked Section 4 schema — and while fixing it, found the wizard's merge logic never actually preserved an already-saved pot's real `accrualBasis`, which would have let this same fix silently overwrite real live lease data on next save; fixed both together. (2) Opening balance no longer blocks saving a pot — defaults to 0, accrual rate stays required. (3) Fly-Forward's pot cards now render in tech-spec order (airframe checks → each engine's PR/LLP pair → landing gear → APU) instead of arbitrary Firestore order. All three tested in isolation (5/5 new checks) plus a full re-run of the existing 54-test suite across all four calculation test files, clean. New `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.43. Flagged for a follow-up: whether any real (non-test) lease already has the old wrong `accrualBasis` saved — this fix deliberately does not auto-correct existing saved data, by design.*

*This session (July 2026 — Brain 1 `utilisation.js` bugfix, found in the same manual test session): Continuing the same test on MSN TEST-99, found a second, unrelated real bug — the asset's first-ever utilisation report also failed to apply, showing "Could not determine reporting gap... saved to history only." Root cause: `processUtilisationReport()`'s brand-new-asset fast path only fires when `previousAsset` is entirely absent; a manually-created asset already has a real Firestore record with a blank `_lastPeriod`, which fell into neither the "no asset" case nor the "real prior period" case — landing in the unparseable-period branch instead, with no way to ever recover since every future upload would hit the same trap. Fixed by adding a distinct `isFirstReportForExistingAsset` case that merges into the *existing* record (preserving manually-entered LLPs/checks/specs, unlike the brand-new-asset path which would have discarded them) and skips delta verification (avoiding a second false "mismatch" against the asset's blank 0 baseline). New regression suite added (22/22 passing), including five adjacent-path checks (garbled period, out-of-order, same-month, real gap) confirming nothing else moved. New `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.42. Note: this is the first time `utilisation.js` has been read this closely outside its original build session, and it's live code affecting every asset, not something scoped to Brain 6 — worth remembering that "hasn't been touched" isn't the same as "known correct."*

*This session (July 2026 — Brain 6 date-format bugfix, found in manual testing): Alan's first manual test of the 4.40 wiring against a real asset (MSN 1009) surfaced false "no usable date" gap warnings on an asset with genuine 6Y/12Y check dates on file. Root cause: `maintenanceCal.js`'s date parser only accepted DD/MM/YYYY, but `asset.checks[].lastDate`/`.nextDate` are actually stored as ISO (confirmed via `index.html`'s `CheckDateInput`, whose commit param is literally named `iso`) — a normalization step (`normalizeDate()`) that wasn't visible in the session that built `maintenanceCal.js`, since `techSpecBuilder.js` wasn't in scope that day. Fixed by accepting ISO first, DD/MM/YYYY as a legacy fallback. Added a regression test reproducing the exact bug (real ISO dates must not fire the gap, DD/MM/YYYY fallback still works) — 20/20 tests passing (was 15). New `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.41. Worth noting for next time: this file had a clean 15/15 test pass in the session that built it, but every test shared the same wrong assumption the parser itself was built on, so none of them could have caught this — only testing against real data did.*

*This session (July 2026 — Brain 6 wired into Fly-Forward, Sonnet): Wired the already-built `maintenanceCal.js` into the live Fly-Forward path. `flyForward.js`'s `monthlyAccrual()` now takes a grounding-availability multiplier (backward compatible — identical output when absent/all-1.0, 5/5 regression tests). EN-LP's engine-cycle clock refactored from a closed-form calc to an incremental accumulator, since grounding varies month-to-month and a straight multiply can't express that; dead `baseFC`/`resetMonth` variables removed once the accumulator made them redundant. `index.html`'s `FlyForward` component now runs two Brain 3 passes — an ungrounded one to source LG-OH/AP-OH/EN-PR/EN-LP dates for Brain 6, then a grounded one using Brain 6's output — plus a `dataCompleteness` gap banner, plus the `<script>` tag actually loading `maintenanceCal.js` (present in the repo since last session but never loaded until now). Added an integration test re-running the real orchestration logic (not a reimplementation) against a realistic fixture — 7/7 passing, confirming grounding measurably lowers accrued reserve balances. New `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.40.*

*This session (July 2026 — Brain 6 build, Sonnet, `maintenanceCal.js` shipped): Built the Maintenance Calendar Engine against the locked `brain6-build-handoff.md` spec — pure function, no Firestore, no UI. Two narrowing decisions made with Alan during the build: grounding math is fractional (day-proportional per month) rather than whole-month, required to pass the mandated Section 10 golden fixture and consistent with most clients only having reduced-utilisation signal rather than exact in/out dates; cost join tolerance set to ±3 months since `shopVisitProjections` has no `dueCycle` field to match on exactly. Found `shopVisitProjections`/`scheduledEvents`/`seasonalityProfile` are all still unbuilt stubs with zero references in `index.html` — Brain 6 takes cost/override data as plain parameters rather than reading Firestore itself, so no rework needed once those collections exist. 15/15 Node E2E tests passing, including the golden fixture. `index.html` not touched this session — wiring into Fly-Forward's two-pass flow is next. New `TECH_DEBT.md` 4.39. See `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 5/17 for the model-level sync.*

### 4.110 Brain 3 Post-Lease Event Generation — ✅ DONE (August 2026)
- **Problem:** All three event generators in `flyForward.js` (`calendarMonthEvents`, `calendarOrCyclesEvents`, `apuHourEvents`) hard-stopped at `horizonMonths`, so `partialFundedNote` was always `null` — the first event beyond the lease horizon was never computed, meaning post-lease chart extensions and shortfall figures could never be shown regardless of what the UI did.
- **Root cause:** Loop conditions `m <= horizonMonths` / `while (m <= horizonMonths)` / `if (monthMin > horizonMonths) break` all terminated before producing the first beyond-horizon event. `nextEventIdx < eventMonths.length` in `projectReservePot`'s `partialFundedNote` block therefore always evaluated false.
- **Fix:** Each generator now produces one event beyond `horizonMonths` before stopping. That event is never fired in the projection loop (which still terminates at `horizonMonths`) but sits as `eventMonths[nextEventIdx]` after the loop, ready for `partialFundedNote` to consume.
- **Files touched:** `public/calculations/flyForward.js`

---

### 4.111 Brain 3 `partialFundedNote` Enriched + EN-LP `leaseEndLimiter` — ✅ DONE (August 2026)
- **Problem:** `partialFundedNote` previously only carried `{ monthIndex, date, note }` — no cost data, no shortfall. The UI couldn't show what a post-lease event would cost or how far the frozen pot balance would fall short. EN-LP's post-lease state was completely opaque.
- **Fix — `projectReservePot`:** `partialFundedNote` now includes `costLow`, `costLikely`, `costHigh` (from `escalatedCostRange` called at the post-lease event date), `balanceAtLeaseEnd` (last entry in `monthlySeries`), `shortfallLow`, `shortfallHigh`.
- **Fix — `projectEnLpPot`:** At `m === horizonMonths`, captures `leaseEndLimiter: { desc, remainingFC, fcPerMonth }` from the working LLP stack and returns it alongside the result. `partialFundedNote` remains `null` for EN-LP (stack sim cannot be projected cheaply past the horizon — cost is genuinely unknown).
- **Files touched:** `public/calculations/flyForward.js`

---

### 4.112 Financials Tab — Post-Lease Chart Extension + Lease-End Divider — ✅ DONE (August 2026)
- **Problem:** Per-pot Financials cards showed a straight accrual line ending at lease end with no indication of what happens next. For 7 of 8 pots on MSN 33552 (13-month lease), every event is post-lease — the chart gave no signal about redelivery exposure.
- **Fix — chart extension:** `FFPotCard` extends the chart past lease end when `partialFundedNote.costLikely != null` and `projection.events.length === 0` (suppressed when a within-lease event already fired). Extension logic: flat frozen balance stub up to 6 months post-lease (compressed if event is further out — final label shows real date regardless). Post-lease segment uses a dashed grey `"Frozen balance (post-lease)"` dataset distinct from the within-lease coloured line.
- **Fix — lease-end divider:** `MiniLineChart` accepts a `leaseEndIdx` prop. Inline Chart.js plugin draws a vertical dashed line and "Lease end · Accruals stop" label at that position, with grey shading over the post-lease zone. Critical bug found and fixed: `x.getPixelForValue(leaseEndIdx)` was passing a numeric index to a category-axis scale — category scales require the label string. Corrected to `x.getPixelForValue(labels[leaseEndIdx])`.
- **Fix — post-lease table row:** Below each chart, a POST-LEASE row shows event date, cost range, balance at lease end, shortfall band. Amber "⚠ Post-lease shortfall" pill added, distinct from red within-lease pill. Subtitle updated to "next event post-lease YYYY-MM". Post-lease section suppressed when there is a within-lease event.
- **EN-LP cards:** No chart extension (stack sim too expensive past horizon). Instead, a compact info block: "Balance at lease end: $X · Lowest limiter: [desc] — [N] FC remaining". Post-lease section suppressed entirely on EN-LP-1 (which has a within-lease event) — only the next event matters.
- **Files touched:** `src/components/FlyForward.jsx`

*Last updated: August 2026 — This session (Asset-level UI layout overhaul — Financials, Calendar, Scenarios tabs + Fleet Scenarios, Sonnet build): Pure layout and information-architecture session — no new Brains, no new Firestore collections, no schema changes.

**Brain 3 post-lease event generation fixed (4.110/4.111):** All three event generators previously hard-stopped at `horizonMonths` — `partialFundedNote` was always `null`. Fixed by letting each generator produce one event beyond the horizon boundary before stopping (never fired in the projection loop, only consumed by `partialFundedNote`). `partialFundedNote` enriched with `costLow/costLikely/costHigh`, `balanceAtLeaseEnd`, `shortfallLow/shortfallHigh`. `projectEnLpPot` now additionally captures `leaseEndLimiter` (lowest-limiter desc + remaining FC + FC/month) at the lease horizon end. Root cause of earlier failed attempts confirmed in retrospect: UI changes could never have surfaced a note that was never computed at the Brain level.

**Financials tab — post-lease chart extension (4.112):** Per-pot cards now extend past lease end with flat frozen balance line, vertical "Lease end · Accruals stop" divider (grey shaded zone), event dot at next event date, post-lease table row. Amber "Post-lease shortfall" pill distinct from red within-lease pill. Post-lease section suppressed when a within-lease event already fired. EN-LP: balance at lease end + lowest limiter FC remaining as compact info block instead. Chart.js divider bug found and fixed: `x.getPixelForValue(leaseEndIdx)` (numeric index) fails on category scales — corrected to `x.getPixelForValue(labels[leaseEndIdx])` (label string).

**Financials tab — header restructure (4.113):** Three button states lifted from `FlyForward` into `AssetView`, rendered directly in the asset header row when `layer === "financials"`. Three top info cards now in a 3-column equal-height landscape grid.

**Calendar tab — layout restructure (4.114):** Completed Events + Calendar description side by side (2-col, stretch). Event rows 4-column grid; expanded edit rows span all 4. Seasonality button lifted into `AssetView` header row.

**Asset Scenarios tab — layout restructure (4.115):** 2-col top (description + sliders left, per-pot right full height). 3-col bottom (Base Case | Scenario | Risk Peaks). IIFE removed.

**Fleet Scenarios — Route Matcher + Pandemic card heights (4.116):** Route Matcher description and form merged into one card; Pandemic `marginTop: 16` removed.

Files touched: `public/calculations/flyForward.js`, `src/components/FlyForward.jsx`, `src/components/AssetView.jsx`, `src/components/Scenarios.jsx`, `src/components/PortfolioView.jsx`.*

### 4.117 Portrait/mobile nav — asset-level nav pill vanishing + Share/Tech Spec overflow (August 2026, `src/App.jsx`)

- **Symptom 1:** On portrait/mobile, navigating into an asset view caused the Details/Calendar/Financials/Scenarios nav pill to completely disappear. The hamburger menu rendered correctly (fleet nav inside it), but there was no way to switch asset layers.
- **Root cause:** The `isMobile` branch in `App.jsx`'s header conditional was a single flat case — it short-circuited immediately to "Portfolio button + ☰ only" regardless of whether `view === "asset"`. The `view === "asset"` sub-case that renders the second row (NavPill + Share/Tech Spec) was only reachable when `isMobile === false`.
- **Symptom 2:** Once fixed, the Share and Generate Tech Spec buttons overflowed off-screen to the right on a narrow mobile viewport — the NavPill and trailing pill were in the same `flexWrap:"nowrap"` row, which pushed the trailing pill outside the viewport.
- **Fix:** Split the mobile branch into two sub-cases matching the desktop structure: `isMobile && view==="asset" && selectedAsset` renders Portfolio+☰ row, then NavPill row, then Share/Generate Tech Spec row (both buttons `flex:1`, splitting the full width). `isMobile` (fleet view) renders Portfolio+☰ only, unchanged. Desktop branches untouched.
- **Files touched:** `src/App.jsx`

### 4.119 iQ Tab Shell + SV Interval Analytics — ✅ BUILT (August 2026)
- **Context:** Steps 2–3 of `sv-analytics-iq-tab-build-spec.md`. First feature in the new iQ tab — the intelligence/analytics layer, distinct from Financials (position) and Calendar (scheduling).
- **`IQView.jsx` (new):** Tab shell mounted at `view==="iq"`, admin-gated. Holds `SVAnalyticsCard` plus two reserved placeholder cards (Completed Events migration, Rate Recommendations) per spec §5e/§10 — position reserved, not built.
- **`SVAnalyticsCard.jsx` (new):** Aggregates shop visit records across every engine and every APU that already has visits on file — APU is included now, even though the bulk document-upload path (APU SV history uploader) remains a separate, not-yet-built item; APU shop visits already exist via the manual Add Visit form, so the analytics consume them the moment they exist, per spec §2/§10. Engines grouped by `engineFamily()` (the same 8-family bucket used for stock photos); APU grouped under a single synthetic "APU" family rather than split by P/N.
- **TSI computation:** First SV per component = raw run from zero, flagged (not excluded) if it looks like it swallowed an earlier unrecorded visit (more than double the very next interval). Subsequent SVs = delta from the prior visit. Zero/negative interval = data-quality issue, flagged and excluded from summary stats, still shown in the raw table.
- **Summary stats:** PR and Hardware only, shown once n ≥ 3 for the segment — mean/median/range for both FH and FC.
- **Raw table:** sortable by any column, filterable by reason category (multi-select chips, 8 categories + Uncategorised), family selector scopes both the summary and the table.
- **Files touched (new):** `src/components/IQView.jsx`, `src/components/SVAnalyticsCard.jsx`. **Modified:** `src/App.jsx` (routes `view==="iq"` to `IQView`, admin-gated).

### 4.120 Shop Visit `reasonCategory` Taxonomy — Extraction, Review Screens, Manual Entry (August 2026)
- **Context:** Steps 4–6 of `sv-analytics-iq-tab-build-spec.md`. Closes the gap SV Interval Analytics (4.119) launched with — every shop visit showed as Uncategorised since nothing anywhere let a user set the category.
- **Scope decision:** Shop Visit and APU shop visit only. **Operator History does NOT get this field** — built then explicitly reverted same session per Alan: "we dont need the categories on the operator history at all." Operator History's `reason` field remains free text only, no categorisation, no schema addition.
- **Canonical list:** `REASON_CATEGORIES` (8 categories — PR, Hardware, FOD, Lease Event, Swap, TIMEX, Scheduled LLP, Other) and the categorisation rule text (`REASON_CATEGORY_RULES`) now live once in `assetHelpers.js`, imported everywhere else that needs them (`extraction.js`, `AssetTabs.jsx`, `SVAnalyticsCard.jsx`) rather than duplicated. Declared ahead of `SHOP_VISIT_PROMPT` in file order — both are `const` template literals referencing `REASON_CATEGORY_RULES`, so declaration order matters (TDZ).
- **Extraction:** `SHOP_VISIT_PROMPT` updated to ask the AI to suggest a `reasonCategory` alongside the existing fields, using the same 8-category rule set as the spec. `extraction.js`'s `extractShopVisits` validates the AI's answer against the fixed list via a new `normaliseReasonCategory()` helper, falling back to `"Other"` if the model returns something outside the taxonomy or omits it — matches spec §3's "AI suggests, human confirms" approach; the review screen is where the user actually confirms/overrides.
- **UI — every place a shop visit can be touched, for both engine (`ShopVisitEditor`) and APU (inline editor in `APUTab`):** a new shared `CategorySelect` dropdown and `CategoryBadge` display component (`AssetTabs.jsx`) added to: the inline Edit row, the manual "+ Add Visit" form, the post-extraction review screen, and a new "Category" column on the display table.
- **Backfill:** No retroactive AI pass on existing records (per spec) — existing shop visits show "Uncategorised" until manually edited. This is the direct fix for what an in-app screenshot showed after the 4.119 build: every row Uncategorised with no way to change it.
- **Files touched:** `src/lib/assetHelpers.js`, `src/lib/extraction.js`, `src/components/AssetTabs.jsx`, `src/components/SVAnalyticsCard.jsx` (now imports the canonical list instead of a local duplicate).

### 4.121 Vercel Auto-Deploy Silently Stopped Mid-Build — Recurrence of 4.63-era Issue, Still Not Root-Caused (August 2026)
- **Symptom:** After 4.118 (nav-only commit, `142188c`) deployed and went live automatically, the very next commit (`6d9e4fd`, "iq" — the real `IQView.jsx`/`SVAnalyticsCard.jsx` build) never triggered a Vercel deployment at all. No failed build in the Deployments tab, no error — just nothing.
- **Diagnosis:** GitHub's unauthenticated API rate-limited quickly mid-investigation (shared container IP), so `git clone`d the actual repo directly (`codeload.github.com`/`github.com` are allow-listed) to read real commit history rather than trusting the Vercel UI's commit labels — confirmed `142188c` was the placeholder-only commit and `6d9e4fd` (current `main` HEAD) had the real files, meaning `6d9e4fd` genuinely never got picked up.
- **Wrong turn, corrected:** First attempted fix was Vercel's **Redeploy** button on the existing (stale) deployment — this rebuilds the *exact same commit* the deployment was already on, not the branch's current HEAD. Redeploying twice just rebuilt `142188c` twice; the placeholder text stayed on production both times. Confirmed by checking the deployment detail page's `Source` commit SHA against `main`'s actual HEAD.
- **Working fix:** A trivial commit (blank-line edit to an unrelated file) via GitHub's web UI triggered a fresh webhook delivery and a correct auto-deploy off current `main` HEAD. Unblocked immediately.
- **Root cause NOT fixed:** This is very likely the same class of issue already flagged open in `VECTORIQ_ROADMAP.md` Section 19 ("Vercel `app` project — stale GitHub webhook, needs Disconnect/Reconnect to prevent recurrence") from the July pre-IT-review session — that item was worked around with a manual Deploy Hook then, never actually fixed, and has now recurred in a different form (this time looking like a GitHub App repository-access drop rather than a stale webhook specifically, though the underlying symptom — auto-deploy silently not firing — is identical). Suggested but not yet checked: GitHub → account Settings → Applications → Installed GitHub Apps → Vercel → Configure → confirm `alanshorten/vector-fleet` is still in the allowed repository list.
- **Priority:** 🟡 MEDIUM — has now recurred twice across two different sessions with two different workarounds; the actual GitHub App permission check still hasn't been done.
